### Splash polish: minimum hold and compose-then-reveal (August 3, 2026, web only)

**Scope.** `artifacts/gujarati-coach`: `src/components/brand-splash.tsx`, `src/index.css`, `src/test/home-brand-splash.test.tsx`, plus this doc. Owner-ruled after device viewing: a fast dev load painted the splash piecemeal (carriage instantly, Bolo late) then blinked away because the ready signal settled instantly.

**Minimum hold.** A qualifying splash MOUNT now plays at least `--splash-min-hold` (1500ms, :root tuning block, ms only). The primary release fires at whichever is LATER: the minimum elapsing or the ready signal settling. The 8s `--splash-max-hold` failsafe is unchanged, is NOT gated on the minimum, and always wins. The warm-cache skip is unchanged: loads whose data is ready at first paint never enter "playing", so the minimum applies only when the splash actually mounts. The min-hold clock starts at overlay mount, not at reveal, so the two new mechanisms cannot compound into a longer total wait.

**Compose-then-reveal.** The overlay's composition (scene plus wordmark) stays unrendered until all five `SPLASH_V2_ASSETS` layers are fetched AND decoded via off-DOM `Image.decode()` (not merely onload), then the whole frame appears in a single reveal; the same URLs are then cache-hot so the real img tags paint in one frame. While the gate holds, the overlay paints ONLY its gradient backdrop. IMPORTANT interpretation pin: the index.html boot gradient stops applying the moment React fills `#root` (the `body:has(> #root:empty)` rule), so the overlay itself must carry the identical gradient to be the seamless holding surface; "renders nothing" means no composition, not no gradient. Decode failure or stall never traps the user: `--splash-decode-cap` (1200ms, tunable, ms only) reveals whatever is ready, and the max-hold failsafe still bounds total time. Reduced motion follows the same gate, then shows the static composed frame.

**Test surface.** `home-brand-splash.test.tsx` gained a `pinTuningVars` helper (getComputedStyle spy on documentElement; unpinned names fall back to component defaults, so pin every var a test's timing depends on) and a module-level `FakeImage` whose `decode()` parks resolvers on `h.decodeResolvers` for exact per-layer control. Composition assertions are now awaited (the decode gate settles in a microtask under jsdom, never synchronously). Any test that expects a release must pin `--splash-min-hold` small or it will sit through the 1500ms fallback.

**ONE-train rule (August 3, 2026).** The splash carriage (public/splash/carriage.svg) derives its livery, palette, and silhouette from the boarding-pass TrainEngine (src/components/train-svg.tsx): indigo primary body, slate foreground roof/chassis/spoked wheels, teal secondary trim, white glazing at the engine opacities. Colors are hardcoded light-mode hex because an img-loaded SVG cannot read page CSS vars. Owner final art supersedes both via the SPLASH_V2_ASSETS paths in brand-splash.tsx. The transparent evenodd window aperture (x 88..152, y 34..82 of the 240x140 viewBox) and slot dimensions are BINDING; Bolo composites into it.

### Chunk 6B: trackside signals, quick games, zone closeout, line facts (August 3, 2026, web only)

**Scope.** `artifacts/gujarati-coach` plus this doc. The Story 3 server contract (context `hub|signal|closeout`, contextRef `gap-N`, `chaiGranted` on the result, shipped in c395232) is consumed, never modified. Correctness for all five new games rides the two FROZEN server models unchanged.

**Quick-game roster (`src/lib/quick-games.ts`).** Five free games in rotation order: Ticket Check (floor 4), Wrong Platform (floor 3), Luggage Match (floor 4), Express Listening (floor 4), Signal Lights (floor 2). `serverGame` maps luggage-match to `word-match` and the other four to `listen-and-pick`; the closed server enum never grows client-side. `QUICK_GAME_MIN_FLOOR` (2) is the auto-wave line: a departed stop whose plan-visible phrase count sits under it offers NO game at a signal. `eligibleQuickGames(count)` filters by floor; `gameForSignal(signalIndex, visibleCount)` is a pure modulo over the eligible roster (null = auto-wave). Storage helpers: waves are sessionStorage `bolo-signal-waved:{lang}` (never shamed, never persisted past the session; an unclaimed signal stays tappable so its Chai stays claimable), cleared markers are localStorage `bolo-signal-cleared:{lang}` (DISPLAY-ONLY; the server first-clear grant is the real idempotency and there is no read endpoint for it), closeout stages are localStorage `bolo-zone-closeout:{lang}` (absent, "beat2", "done"; seeded on first sight so pre-existing complete zones never retro-celebrate).

**Shared quick-game frame (`src/pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx`).** Built once, used by all five games: `useQuickLaunch` parses `?cat/ctx/gap` (ctx=signal WITHOUT gap is ignored entirely; the server requires contextRef there), `QuickGameShell` owns topic picker with per-game floor, round progress strip, optional per-round countdown, result beat, and the single POST via `useRecordGameSession`. The context spread is conditional so a hub launch's payload stays byte-identical to pre-6B (no `context` key at all). On a signal-success 201 the frame shows the Chai chip (`chai-earn-beat`, styled after the +XP chip pattern), invalidates the tokens query, and writes the local cleared marker. Games implement rounds via `QuickRoundProps`/`QuickRoundApi` (`lockRound` freezes the timer at answer, `submitRound` records and advances).

**Five game pages (`src/pages/games/*.tsx`).** Ticket Check: English+romanized ticket, pick the native script, 8 rounds of 4 choices. Wrong Platform: 3 locals + 1 stray from the first other non-empty category; the stray id is NEVER submitted (in-category anchor scoring: correct = anchor/anchor, wrong = anchor/other-local); no second topic = in-round message. Luggage Match: two racks, cap 6 pairs; first-try match submits self, otherwise the first wrong counterpart. Express Listening: listen-and-pick clone at 1.25x playbackRate, 7s rounds, 8 rounds, timeout = miss via an in-category distractor. Signal Lights: true/false at 4s over 10 rounds; false rounds show a same-category decoy's English and a wrong judgment or timeout submits the decoy id.

**Journey surfaces (`src/pages/journey.tsx`, `src/components/journey-scenery.tsx`).** `planTracksideSignals(totalStations)` seats a signal in the gap after every ODD global stop (1-based, no signal after the terminus); `signalIndex = (N-1)/2` drives the rotation. "The train stops at the signal" is a halted-pulse visual, not a forced modal: an active signal in the gap right behind the boardable stop empties the rail pulse run and pulses the glyph; the encounter dialog only ever opens from a tap. Showroom mode renders no signals. `planZoneSignpost(zi, count)` seats one tappable signpost per zone on a scenery-free row; tapping opens the fact dialog (salt 2). `factForZone` (`src/lib/india-facts.ts`) takes a NUMERIC salt to split the three daily rotation streams: 1 = gateway postcard strip, 2 = signpost, 3 = closeout arrival fact; region/line tag matches (INDIA_FACT_TAGS, mechanically tagged) are preferred over the generic pool. Zero new network calls.

**Zone closeout (`src/components/zone-closeout.tsx`).** Client-side detection over the zones' station groups; rendered by journey when not in showroom. Beat 1: celebration, arrival fact, closeout game CTA (confirmed Plus = `speed-round?ctx=closeout`, otherwise `express-listening?cat={zoneId}&ctx=closeout`; fail-closed on `isLoading`), Skip for now. Beat 2 only when the zone has a scenario and no stamp: capstone chat CTA or Maybe later. Every path advances the stage machine; nothing gates the map.

**Test surface.** New: `quick-games.test.tsx` (roster/rotation/storage units), `quick-game-frame.test.tsx` (launch-context POST contract), `journey-signals.test.tsx` (seating, states, encounter, auto-wave, held pulse), `zone-closeout.test.tsx` (seeding + two-beat machine). Modified: `games-hub.test.tsx` (10 cards, new games have no vignettes), `journey-scenery.test.tsx` (signpost planner + factForZone pins), `journey-rail-pulse.test.tsx` (its beforeEach now pre-waves every gap because the train halts at active signals; journey-signals owns the held behavior).

**Known debt.** Cleared-signal display state is device-local (no server read endpoint), so a second device shows an already-cleared signal as active until replayed; the grant itself stays idempotent server-side. Mobile parity for all of Chunk 6B rides the standing row 8 audit.

### Prod hotfix batch: signal taps, soft stop, fact variety, game audio, US English (August 3, 2026, web only)

**Scope.** `artifacts/gujarati-coach` plus this doc. Six hotfix items against the Chunk 6B surfaces; no server changes.

**US English ruling (owner-ruled, app-wide).** All user-facing copy is US English until localization lands: "Keep Practicing!", "canceled", "practice" as the verb. British spellings inside code identifiers, comments, CSS/API names (analyser, cancelled event fields) are exempt and stay. "Indian Space Research Organisation (ISRO)" keeps its official proper-noun spelling.

**Signal taps (journey map).** The full-width zone-postcard wrappers paint at z-index 8, above the z-index 6 signal/signpost buttons, and swallowed taps on every zone-boundary gap signal (exactly the held signal a learner reaches; jsdom clicks bypass hit-testing, so tests stayed green). The wrappers and the interchange diamond are now pointer-events-none with the postcard card itself pointer-events-auto (it holds the zone test-out link). Signal and signpost buttons grew from p-1 to p-2: hit targets 44x56 and 46x54.

**Signal visibility.** Active signals add an amber halo ring plus red glow behind the lit lamp (SignalGlyph); the button-level attention pulse now rides every active signal, motion-safe only, so reduced motion suppresses it.

**Soft stop (owner-ruled).** Reaching a held signal (active, in the gap right behind the boardable stop) auto-opens the encounter dialog once per signal, remembered under `bolo-signal-stop-shown:{lang}` (helpers in src/lib/quick-games.ts). It was sessionStorage when shipped and is localStorage since August 10, 2026 — see 10y. Never over an open dialog or a pending zone closeout; manual signal taps also mark the stop seen so closing never auto-reopens. Wave-through and the auto-wave quip are unchanged.

**Fact variety.** `factForZone`'s fallback previously let the line-tagged pool stand alone; with a single railways fact that pool has length 1 and day, zone index, and salt all vanish modulo 1, so every untagged zone showed the same fact everywhere, every day. The fallback now rotates the full fact set; region-tagged preference is unchanged.

**Signal Lights audio.** Rounds now synthesize and cache the phrase clip via the express-listening house pattern, honoring the frame's mute toggle (soundOn was dead code for this game); the 4s answer window is untouched (clip plays alongside the countdown).

**Splash blackout in production (addendum, root cause).** The production CSS minifier rewrites `:root` time values for byte savings (`8000ms` becomes `8s`, `1500ms` becomes `1.5s`), and the splash's unit-blind `parseFloat` read `"8s"` as 8 MILLISECONDS, so the max-hold failsafe unmounted the overlay before its first paint: asset requests fired (the decode gate ran) but no gradient or scene ever showed. Dev serves unminified CSS and jsdom returns empty strings (fallbacks), so the defect was invisible outside the deployed build. The same bug made `--tear-nav-delay` (500ms to 0.5ms) and `--tear-overlay-cleanup` (900ms to 0.9ms) collapse in prod, gutting the boarding-pass tear beat. Rule going forward: every JS read of a stylesheet timing var goes through `cssTimeMs` in `src/lib/utils.ts` (unit-aware, pinned by `src/test/css-time.test.ts`); a bare `parseFloat` on `getPropertyValue` output is a prod-only bug by construction.

### Hotfix 3: crossing signals, signal encounter reward clarity, live facts, timed-game feel (August 3, 2026, web only)

**Scope.** `artifacts/gujarati-coach` plus this doc. Seven consolidated client-only items against the Chunk 6B / prod-hotfix surfaces; no server changes.

**Railroad-crossing signal glyph (`src/components/journey-scenery.tsx`).** SignalGlyph is now a 32x40 level-crossing post: crossbuck, ink lamp box with a single lit lamp (red = not cleared, amber = waved, green = cleared), and a striped gate arm that is DOWN for red states and rotated up (75 degrees) for waved/cleared. State model: red bar-down covers both active and future signals at full color (never dimmed); only the ACTIVE red signal adds the amber halo, red glow, and motion-safe pulse and is tappable. Future red signals are disabled with no cursor affordance. New palette consts INDIGO/TEAL/SIGNAL_RED live beside the existing scenery colors.

**Signal seating (`src/pages/journey.tsx`).** Signals moved from the gap midpoint to the departure edge of the preceding stop (x offset 30 opposite the postcard's card side, clamped; y +30), so a signal reads as guarding the track you leave on, and the glyph no longer collides with the zone postcard. The button drops the old `opacity-60` dimming, keeps `disabled` for upcoming, and paints at `DEPTH_2_5D.layers.postcard + 1` so no postcard layer can occlude it again.

**Signal encounter dialog.** Every non-cleared open shows an aria-hidden scene header (train + dashed track + the crossing glyph, testid `signal-scene`), a state-specific body, a "+1 Chai" amber reward chip (testid `signal-chai-chip`), and a per-game one-line blurb (`GAME_BLURBS`, testid `signal-game-blurb`). Waved signals (yellow, session-scoped) reopen the FULL encounter with the chip; cleared signals show the replay dialog with NO chip (the Chai is already pocketed). "Wave me through" now confirms via the house toast.

**Live fact strip (`src/pages/journey.tsx` FactStrip + `src/lib/india-facts.ts`).** The gateway postcard's static daily fact became an auto-cycling strip: `factRotationForZone` returns the zone's full rotation with index 0 equal to today's `factForZone` pick (same modular stride, salt 1). The strip crossfades every 6s (250ms fade), pauses when scrolled out of view (IntersectionObserver, jsdom fallback = visible), advances instantly on tap, and under reduced motion never auto-cycles (tap still works, instant swap). Testid `postcard-fact-{zoneIndex}` is unchanged.

**Timed-game feel (`src/pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx` + `src/index.css`).** Timed games (Express Listening, Signal Lights) open with a 3-2-1 "Get ready…" countdown (800ms steps, testid `quick-countdown`, pop keyframe motion-safe only) during which the round surface does not mount, so neither the round clock nor round audio can start early. The round timer is restyled as a bordered pill and goes red + motion-safe pulse only inside the final 2 seconds.

**Signal Lights round audio (`src/pages/games/signal-lights.tsx` + `src/components/game-mute-button.tsx`).** Synthesis results are round-stamped: a clip resolving after its round advanced or timed out never plays, and round change or expiry pauses any playing clip. The frame's mute button takes an `active` prop and wears the practice-green treatment while a clip is audibly playing (`setAudioPlaying` is now a required QuickRoundProps member; untimed games receive it via the shell and simply never call it).

**Known debt.** The countdown length (3 steps x 800ms) and fact cycle (6s) are unexported literals pinned only by tests; waved-signal yellow remains session-scoped device-local state like cleared display state before it.

### Hotfix 3S: server wave persistence, session Chai receipt, variable signal rewards, Signalman (August 3, 2026)

**Scope.** `artifacts/api-server`, `artifacts/gujarati-coach`, `lib/db` (schema + migration 0039), generated client/zod dirs (codegen), this doc.

**signal_waves table (migration `0039_signal_waves.sql`).** New table: id serial PK, user_id text FK → users(id) ON DELETE cascade, ref text, created_at timestamptz default now(), UNIQUE(user_id, ref) `signal_waves_user_ref_unique`. Ref convention is `${languageCode}:${categoryId}:gap-N` — identical to the `earn_signal_first_clear` ledger refId. Rows are never deleted; a later first-clear supersedes the wave for display.

**POST /journey/signal-waves (`routes/learning.ts`, operationId `recordSignalWave`).** Body `{languageCode, categoryId, gap}` — zod pins a STRICT grammar (`/^[a-z]{2,3}$/` language, positive int category, gap 1-999) because stored refs feed the lesson-groups LIKE prefix scan: `%`/`_` must never enter a ref. Unknown language or category 404s (existence check, no entitlement read). The ref is composed SERVER-side, insert is `onConflictDoNothing`, response `{ ref }`. Idempotent by the unique constraint.

**Signals payload on the lesson-groups GET.** `GET /categories/:id/lesson-groups/:lang` now prepends `signals: { rewardChai, waves, clears }` (schema `ZoneSignalStates`): waves from signal_waves, clears from `earn_signal_first_clear` ledger rows, both prefix-filtered by `${lang}:${id}:` and stripped to bare `gap-N` refs, fetched in the route's existing Promise.all. Zero new read requests for the map (it already calls this six times).

**Variable signal rewards (`lib/tokenEconomy.ts`).** `SIGNAL_FIRST_CLEAR_CHAI` (flat 1) is replaced by `SIGNAL_FIRST_CLEAR_REWARDS = { default: 1, perLine: {} }` plus accessor `signalFirstClearChai(languageCode)`. The game-session grant and the served `rewardChai` both go through the accessor, so a per-line override changes the grant and the client chip together.

**Attempt-side Chai receipt.** `lib/tokenService.ts` gains `grantTokensDetailed(...)` returning `{ state, granted }` (`grantTokens` delegates). The attempts route reports `chaiEarned` (optional, only when > 0) on `AttemptResult`; today the only attempt-side-effect earn is the streak-day grant. The web Session Complete screen sums `chaiEarned` across attempts and renders an amber "+{n} Chai earned" pill (testid `session-chai-pill`) under the XP pill, only when n > 0. Zone-complete Chai is NOT attempt-side (it rides latch-on-read and test-out passes) and continues to surface via the zone closeout.

**Client server-truth merge (`src/pages/journey.tsx`).** Signal display state is now server-first: cleared if the served clears include the gap OR the local mark exists, else waved by the same rule, else active/upcoming. localStorage/sessionStorage marks remain as optimistic cache only. Both wave sites (wave-me-through and the auto-wave carry-on) fire the `useRecordSignalWave` mutation fire-and-forget alongside the local mark. The encounter reward chip renders the served `signals.rewardChai`, never a hardcoded amount. `SignalSpot` gained `rewardChai`.

**Signalman (`src/components/journey-scenery.tsx`).** `SignalmanGlyph`: hand-drawn 26x40 inline SVG (brand palette + one new `SKIN` const, TrainEngine construction pattern — flat shapes, no gradients/raster), a friendly uniformed figure holding a red flag, testid `signalman-glyph`, rendered in the aria-hidden encounter scene header beside the crossing glyph.

**Test surface.** New: `api-server/src/test/signalWaves.test.ts` (wave insert idempotency, SignalWaveBody zod, grantTokensDetailed granted flag, reward accessor, signals-payload derivation — justified as a genuinely new server surface). Modified: `gameSessions.test.ts` (accessor replaces the removed flat const), `journey-signals.test.tsx` (server-truth states, payload-driven chip, wave persistence spies, Signalman pin), `practice-streak-xp.test.tsx` (Chai receipt pill sum + absent cases).

**Known debt.** The web mock base (`api-client-mock.ts`) auto-covers new hooks, but per-file `useListCategoryLessonGroups` overrides that omit `signals` silently fall back to local-only derivation (rewardChai defaults to 1 client-side). Waves POSTed while offline are lost (fire-and-forget, no retry queue); the local mark still covers the session.

---

### Signal polish batch (Aug 3, 2026)

**Game-session pass gate (`lib/tokenEconomy.ts` + `routes/learning.ts`).** `gameSessionPassed(correct, total)`: passing means strictly more than half the server-validated rounds. Signal and closeout Chai grants fire only on passing sessions; a failing run leaves the once-ever refId unspent, so a later passing run still pays. Hub sessions, XP, and badges are ungated; the response shape is unchanged (chaiGranted is simply absent on fails). The web frame marks a signal cleared only when the response carries a positive `chaiGranted` (a client-side majority mirror would drift on wrapped plans: the server counts deduplicated first attempts per phrase, rounds may repeat phrases); replays of an already-cleared signal are covered by the journey's server-truth clears list.

**Signal lamp visibility (`components/journey-scenery.tsx`).** SignalGlyph renders 40x50 (was 32x40; viewBox unchanged so the geometry scales as one piece) with lamp-only brighter fills: `SIGNAL_AMBER #ffb300` (waved) and `SIGNAL_GREEN #22c55e` (cleared). Scenery-wide `AMBER`/`LEAF` are untouched. Hit target is now 56x66 via the existing p-2 wrapper (44px minimum still holds).

**Ticket Check reveal (`pages/games/ticket-check.tsx`).** Choices stay native-script-only during play; after the answer every ticket reveals its own romanized line (skipped when the field is empty, which covers ks/sd/ur/sat/mni) and the correct ticket adds its English meaning. Both outcomes now hold on the house "Tap to continue" beat; correct answers no longer auto-advance after 700ms, so the reveal is readable.

**Test surface.** Extended only, no new files: `gameSessions.test.ts` (pass-gate pins, including the 1-of-8 evidence run), `quick-game-frame.test.tsx` (failing run never marks cleared; Ticket Check reveal describe; the marks-cleared pin now plays a passing 2 of 2).

### Build 34A: iOS parity batch 1 (August 3, 2026, mobile only)

**Scope.** `artifacts/bolo-mobile` plus this doc. Web-parity batch; recon verified four items already at parity with no code changes (pass-card energy/tear/rail pulse/train art, record barge-in, the 88x88 hold-to-talk frame contract, app icon), and the entitlement audit found zero "pro" identifiers (the app checks 'plus' and 'one_language'; 'family' is derived server-side, never checked client-side).

**Meaning audio (`app/(app)/practice/[id].tsx` + new `lib/meaning-audio.ts`).** Port of the web practice meaning segment: after the coach phrase clip, a 400ms beat (`MEANING_SEGMENT_PAUSE_MS`), then the English meaning in an English voice (`languageName: 'English'`, `languageCode: 'en'`). `meaningSpeechText` mirrors the web rules verbatim: "means X" prefix unless the sentence flag is set, the gloss ends in sentence punctuation, or it runs 6+ words. Preference key `bolo.meaningAudio` (exported as `MEANING_AUDIO_KEY`), default on, stored in AsyncStorage; because that store is async (unlike web localStorage) the screen uses a lazy pref ref (`readMeaningPref`) so a saved "off" is never raced by the first autoplay, and the ref is read fresh at play time so a toggle applies to the very next segment. Per-session meaning cache keyed by phrase id (`meaningCacheRef`; the coach cache key shape does not apply since the meaning is always English). Prewarm overlaps the phrase clip, with reset-on-failure so `playMeaning` retries fresh; every failure path fails silent to phrase-only. `coachPlaying` spans the meaning segment (web playing_coach parity) and playback rides `playbackRef`/`playTokenRef`, so barge-in and iOS earpiece routing cover it with no extra code. The practice header gains a globe pill toggle (testID `meaning-audio-header-toggle`, secondary fill when on, web accessibility strings verbatim). ~~Review screen intentionally has no meaning segment.~~ SUPERSEDED Aug 12, 2026 by #1046: review carries the identical segment now (see the #1046 entry below).

**Review romanized guard (`app/(app)/review.tsx`).** The phrase card's romanized line is guarded like the game screens (testID `review-phrase-romanized`, hidden when empty). Curated content ships romanized for every language today (0 empty of 268 fields each for ur/ks/sd/sat/mni), so the guard is a defensive rail. The practice phrase card stays unguarded, matching web practice.

**Result band copy.** The result ladder is now the web ladder verbatim on both the practice and review result cards: 'Perfect!' / 'Amazing!' / 'Nice work!' / 'So close!' / 'Good try, keep going!' (owner-approved contingency; the emoji rungs 'Excellent 🌟' / 'Good 👍' / 'Almost there 👍' / 'Keep trying 🔄' are retired). Test pins across 13 practice suite files were updated to the new strings; no behavioral assertions changed.

**Test surface.** New file `__tests__/practice-meaning-audio.test.tsx` (justified: the play chain past the phrase clip is a genuinely new mobile surface; covers default-on synthesis/playback, stored-off skip, toggle persistence, and the meaningSpeechText rules). Extended `review-we-heard-romanized.test.tsx` with a guard describe. Pinned `bolo.meaningAudio: 'off'` in the four files that count exact synth/playback calls for other surfaces (`practice-band-audio`, `practice-voice-cache`, `practice-retry`, `practice-spoken-feedback`).

**Known debt.** The pref key literal is repeated in the four test pins (the export exists for reuse). Real-device meaning playback and earpiece routing are device-pass pending. `family.test.tsx` showed a one-off failure under full-suite load in this batch and passed solo immediately after; watch for recurrence.

### Build 34B: iOS parity batch 2 (August 3, 2026, mobile only)

**Scope.** `artifacts/bolo-mobile` plus this doc. Six items: Chai wallet surfaces, session Chai receipt, zone test-out entry, tear-sound speaker reroute, offering-driven prices, splash config (recon: already aligned with the 3S-era config, no change).

**Chai wallet (new `components/ChaiWallet.tsx` + home).** Mobile port of the web wallet (`gujarati-coach/src/components/chai-wallet.tsx`): `ChaiWalletSheet` is a bottom-anchored Modal (the journey lock dialog's Modal pattern; mobile has no shared Sheet component) with balance row, Station Pause row, Express Multiplier row, exact web copy and the exact web 409 rejection copy set surfaced through `MilestoneToast` (house transient-notice pattern). `useExpressCountdown` recomputes mm:ss from the wall clock every tick (background-return safe) and schedules exactly one tokens refetch at expiry+250ms; no polling. Spends POST `{ item: 'station_pause' | 'express_multiplier' }`; success is silent, settle always invalidates `GET /tokens` (server truth, zero client balance math). Home gains a fourth `GradientStatCell` (testID `stat-chai`, Feather coffee) that opens the sheet; `GradientStatCell` learned optional `onPress`/`testID`/`accessibilityLabel` + chevron. No Chacha-ji vignette (canonical-mascot rule).

**Session Chai receipt (`app/(app)/practice/[id].tsx`).** `sessionChai` accumulates `attempt.chaiEarned ?? 0` from attempt responses (server-authoritative grants); when > 0 the done screen renders an amber `session-chai-pill` (CountUpText "+{n} Chai earned", #D97706, delay 540) after the XP chip. Hidden at zero.

**Zone test-out (`practice/[id].tsx` + `journey.tsx`).** Web-parity zone scope: params `{ id, mode: 'testout', scope: 'zone' }` with NO group. `isZoneTestout` reuses the entire test-out session flow via two seams: `activeTestoutQuery` (`useGetZoneTestout(categoryId, activeLang)`) and `activeTestoutSubmit` (`useSubmitZoneTestout`); submit body `{ categoryId, data: { languageCode, attempts } }` (web `practice.tsx` parity). A pass invalidates the category lesson-groups key plus a predicate sweep of string keys `/api/lesson-groups/...`-prefixed ending in `/phrases` (the zone sample carries no member group ids). `zone_locked` 403 guard ported on both paths: `isZoneLockedError` drives guidance copy ("Finish the previous zone first, or test out of it.") on the load-error screen (`LessonError` gained an optional `message` prop) and on the verdict screen, where it suppresses resubmit like `throttled`. Journey entry points: lock-dialog action `link-test-out-zone` plus a dormant postcard link `link-zone-test-out-{i}` shown only when `zoneGateLocked` (access envelope null, all stations locked, none planLocked — web verbatim; showroom payloads keep it dormant).

**Tear SFX speaker reroute (`lib/audio.ts` + `lib/tearAudio.ts`).** New exported `activateSfxPlaybackRoute()`: serialized on the session queue; no-op off iOS or with a cold session; claims `playbackModeToken` FIRST even when already in playback mode (so a just-finished playback's deferred recording-mode restore cannot re-route the clip to the earpiece mid-play), then flips to the existing PLAYBACK_MODE only if currently in recording mode. Never restores; the next recorder prepare re-asserts recording mode. `playTearSfx` awaits it inside the fire-and-forget chain before seekTo/play; a flip failure still plays the clip. Haptic untouched. Device-pass pending.

**Offering-driven prices (`contexts/PurchasesContext.tsx`, `paywall.tsx`, `account/family.tsx`).** `familyMonthly`/`familyAnnual` packages derived read-only from the RevenueCat default offering (family tier deliberately NOT sold on the mobile paywall — 34A ruling; selling it would break client unlock). Paywall's hardcoded 'From $6.99/mo' replaced with 'Your chosen language'; family JoinView helpText uses `familyMonthly.product.priceString` with a "one family" no-price fallback. Ride-along: all four em dashes in paywall user-visible strings replaced with comma/period equivalents (benefit descs, one-language subhead, trial banner); code comments keep theirs.

**Test surface.** New file `__tests__/chai-wallet.test.tsx` (justified: the wallet is a genuinely new mobile surface; copy, payloads, 409 set, settle invalidation, countdown, expiry refetch). Extensions: `tear-audio` (reroute ordering, flip-failure still plays, pref-off skips the session), `lib-audio-playback-mode` (one-way flip, stale-restore token claim), `practice-testout` (zone scope: submit shape, invalidation predicate, fail path, both zone_locked paths; expo-router params made configurable via `mockState.params`), `practice-celebrations` (Chai pill sum + hidden-at-zero), `journey-map` (zone dialog link, dormant postcard link, showroom dormancy), `home-quiz-gate` (stat cell opens sheet). Mock-factory sweep added idle `useGetTokens`/zone-testout stubs across ~30 home/practice/journey test files; `family.test.tsx` needed a `@/contexts/PurchasesContext` stub (the real context imports untransformed react-native-purchases ESM). Full mobile suite: 74 suites / 502 tests (was 73/477).

**Known debt.** Tear reroute and zone test-out flow are device-pass pending (items 4 and 6). The zone pass's predicate sweep string-matches URL-shaped query keys; if the generated key shape ever changes, the sweep silently stops matching (test pins the current shape). ### Build 35: games hub redesign (web) + Word Match board clearance (mobile) (August 5, 2026)

**Scope ruling.** The hub redesign is WEB ONLY. The spec originally called for web/mobile parity, but Step 0 found mobile ships only five of the ten games (Express Listening, Signal Lights, Luggage Match, Ticket Check and Wrong Platform have no mobile routes at all), which makes both the three-group structure and the five-gated/five-free split unbuildable there. Owner ruling: mobile hub stays FLAT with its current 3 gated / 2 free split, and mobile revisits when the missing five ship. Mobile's only change in this build is the Word Match clearance fix.

**Curated grouping (`pages/games/index.tsx`).** `GAME_GROUPS` is the single source of both group order and within-group order: Listening (listen-and-pick, express-listening, signal-lights), Vocabulary (luggage-match, word-match, ticket-check, bolo-quiz), Building (wrong-platform, phrase-builder, speed-round). Nothing downstream sorts, reorders, or filters by tier: a locked card holds its curated slot. `ORDERED_GAMES` flattens the groups and `STAGGER_INDEX` maps each id to its catalog-wide ordinal, which drives BOTH the entrance cascade delay and the vignette's `--gv-delay` phase offset, so no two ambient loops pulse in sync.

**Featured slot.** `FEATURED_SLOT = { groupId: "vocabulary" }` names WHERE the hero comes from rather than naming a card inline; `resolveFeaturedGame()` returns that group's first card, currently Luggage Match. Deliberately no rotation logic. The promoted card is filtered OUT of its group's grid row (`group.gameIds.filter((id) => id !== featuredGame?.id)`) so it renders exactly once; the remaining cards keep their curated order. Owner ruling on the hero being free: with all five vignette-bearing games gated, a locked hero above a hub where every animated card is locked reads as a paywall menu rather than a games hub. Consequence to know when re-pointing the slot: Luggage Match is one of the five games with NO vignette, so the hero currently renders a static lucide icon and `GamePreview` returns a bare fallback with no test id — `featured-game-preview` does not exist in the DOM, and the hub's IntersectionObserver count is five, not six. Point `FEATURED_SLOT` at a vignette-bearing game and both facts flip. `GamePreview`'s optional `testId` prop is retained for that case.

**Web gating is now five/five.** `word-match` and `listen-and-pick` flipped to `plusOnly: true`, joining phrase-builder, speed-round and bolo-quiz. Free: ticket-check, wrong-platform, luggage-match, express-listening, signal-lights. Surface-level ONLY — the server still gates no game id (confirmed again in Step 0: `routes/learning.ts` uses game ids solely for a submission `z.enum`, round limits and XP maps; there is no `requirePlus` on any game route). Keep it that way. The gate check is unchanged: `game.plusOnly && !plusReady` where `plusReady = isPlus === true && !isLoading`, so it still fails closed while entitlements load or when `isPlus` is undefined.

**Locked-card grammar changed: gated cards are now FULL COLOR.** The old look-but-locked treatment (`opacity-80 saturate-[0.85]`, washed `lockedBg`/`lockedIconColor`, and `gv--locked` pausing the vignette until hover) is RETIRED on the hub. Gated cards render full-saturation art with a live ambient loop; the gate is carried entirely by the All-Access badge and a new `LockChip` ("Locked", `data-testid="lock-chip"`). `GameColor.lockedBg` and `lockedIconColor` were deleted from the type and all eleven entries as dead code. The hub no longer passes `locked` to `GamePreview`; the prop still exists on the component but has no hub caller. Locked cards remain tappable to `/upgrade` — never a dead end.

**Near-square tiles.** `GameCard` is `aspect-square` with `justify-between` and a `line-clamp-3` description, replacing the old content-height `h-full` card. Every card carries `data-testid="game-card-<id>"`; each group section carries `data-testid="games-group-<id>"` and the three shelves sit inside `data-testid="games-catalog"` (needed because the featured duplicate makes bare `getByText(title)` ambiguous). Difficulty chip kept; no energy or quick/deep chip was added, and a test pins their absence.

**Mobile clearance fix (`app/(app)/(tabs)/games/word-match.tsx`).** `GameBoard`'s root View is `flex: 1` and was the only one of the screen's three layouts missing the bottom clearance its siblings apply (topic picker line ~220, end screen style line ~738), so the grid stretched full-height and the last tile row rendered under the floating tab bar. It now sets `paddingBottom: TAB_BAR_CLEARANCE` and carries `testID="word-match-board"` so the clearance is assertable. The defect was NOT in the games hub — the mobile hub's FlatList already had the clearance.

**Test surface.** Web `games-hub.test.tsx` rewritten (group order, per-group curated order, ten-and-only-ten catalog, featured slot resolution and gate grammar, five/five split, all five gated cards locked AND tappable, full-color assertion, fail-closed pair, one-difficulty-chip, no energy chip, All-Access copy canon, vignette presence and stagger uniqueness). `games-hub-motion.test.tsx` updated for the new grammar: no vignette is ever `gv--locked`, the IntersectionObserver count is six (five grid vignettes plus the featured copy), and the step-in navigation pin moved to a free card (ticket-check) since Word Match is now gated. New mobile file `__tests__/word-match-clearance.test.tsx` (2 tests) reuses the `enterGameBoard()` pattern from `word-match-voice-cache.test.tsx` with a non-zero mocked `TAB_BAR_CLEARANCE`. Suites: web 68 files / 523 tests (was 68 / 507), mobile 77 suites / 520 tests (was 76 / 518).

### Build 35: mobile quick-game shell foundation (August 5, 2026, bolo-mobile only)

**Scope.** Foundation only — NO quick games are ported in this task. The shell and the roster ship so the five missing mobile games (the gap the Build 35 hub ruling above documents) can land against a fixed contract instead of each re-deciding its own timer, picker and persistence. No server, web, journey or `app.json` changes; `listen-and-pick.tsx` was NOT edited.

**Extraction, not translation (`components/games/QuickGameShell.tsx`).** The shell was extracted from mobile `listen-and-pick.tsx` rather than translated from web `pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx`, so it inherits mobile's real picker (SkeletonCard shimmer, `categoryIcon`, floor-disabled rows), end screen (Mascot pose ladder, stats grid, ChunkyButton stack), header exit and `confirmDiscardRun`. The web frame supplied only the round contract and the launch-context rules. Countdown and per-round timer are net-new — the extraction source has neither.

**What the shell owns, and what a game may not do.** Topic picker, 3-2-1 countdown (`COUNTDOWN_STEP_MS = 800`, gated on phrases actually being ready so a slow query cannot drop the learner into an empty round), the per-round clock and its `secondsLeft <= 2` urgent treatment, round progression, result accumulation, the result screen, and the SINGLE end-of-run POST. A game supplies only round UI via `renderRound` and reports through `api.submitRound`; games NEVER persist. `submittedRef` makes submit once-per-round and `finishedRef` makes the POST once-per-run, so hammering the final answer cannot double-post.

**Timeout contract (web parity).** At zero the shell sets `api.timedOut` and locks the clock; the ROUND is responsible for calling `submitRound` in response. The shell deliberately does not auto-submit a phantom answer — only the game knows what "no answer" means for its mechanic. A shell rendered with a round that ignores `timedOut` will sit at zero by design.

**Launch context is built in, not retrofitted.** `parseQuickLaunch()` is a pure exported function over `?cat=&ctx=&gap=` (route-param flavour: `useQuickLaunch()`). The rule that matters: a `signal` context is constructed ONLY when the gap is a valid non-negative integer — a signal launch with a missing or malformed gap is REFUSED and degrades to a plain hub launch rather than posting a signal the server rejects (server requires `contextRef` matching `^gap-[0-9]+$` whenever context is signal, and forbids it otherwise). `ctx=hub` also yields no context. Context keys are spread into the POST body conditionally, so a hub launch posts a payload byte-identical to one from a shell that knows nothing about launch context — asserted by pinning the exact key set.

**Pinned launches change the decline path.** `categoryId` from params skips the picker entirely (phase starts at countdown). Declining or finishing a pinned journey run returns to `/(app)/journey` instead of a topic list the learner never chose from; a pinned non-journey launch goes `router.back()`; only an unpinned run falls back to the picker. `phraseResults` carry `{phraseId, selectedPhraseId}` only — clients never self-report correctness (a test pins the absence of a `correct` key).

**Roster (`lib/quick-games.ts`).** Data only, no navigation wired. Ids, titles, floors (4/3/4/4/2) and `serverGame` match the web roster exactly so a signal offers the same game and the same scoring on both platforms; mobile adds `description`, `difficulty` and a Feather `icon`. `route` is a plain string, NOT a typed `Href`, because none of these screens exist yet and a typed route would fail typecheck until they do — convert when the first game lands. `gameForSignal()` mirrors web's deterministic index-over-eligible rotation.

**Only enumerated edit outside the new files:** `components/GameMuteButton.tsx` gained an optional `active?: boolean` prop for the audio-active green treatment (requirement F). Additive and opt-in — existing callers omit it and are unchanged; `soundOn && active` means a muted game never lights up.

**Test surface.** New `__tests__/quick-game-shell.test.tsx` (39 tests): launch parsing incl. five malformed-gap cases, roster invariants, picker skip and floor-disabling, round accumulation and ordering, clock reset/urgent/freeze-at-zero, single-POST and double-tap, Chai chip + wallet invalidation, the four payload shapes (hub / signal / closeout / refused-signal), and all four exit paths. Trap for future timer tests: the clock reschedules itself from an effect, so under fake timers one `advanceTimersByTime(4000)` yields ONE tick — advance one second per `act` block (`tickSeconds` helper). Suites: mobile 78 suites / 559 tests (was 77 / 520).

### Quick-game shell corrections: untimed mode + zero-based rounds (August 5, 2026, bolo-mobile only)

Both corrections came out of the Ticket Check port's Step 0, which stopped before building rather than working around the shell. Shell only — no games ported, and launch-context, single-POST, submit-guard and roster behaviour are all untouched.

**Untimed mode.** `secondsPerRound` is now `?: number | null`, mirroring the web frame's contract exactly. Absent or null means the shell runs NO per-round clock, NO timer chip, and NO 3-2-1 count-in, and `api.timedOut` can never become true; a pinned untimed launch starts in `playing` rather than `countdown`, and `handleTopicSelect`/`playAgain` branch the same way. The internal `timed` flag guards both timer effects, so an untimed game never schedules a tick at all — the chip is not rendered rather than rendered-and-frozen, so nothing on screen implies a clock that isn't running. When a value IS passed, behaviour is identical to `9fc9b07`. This matters because untimed is a design choice, not an absence: web Ticket Check omits the prop so its answer reveal rides the continue beat for BOTH outcomes and the learner sets the dwell time (auto-advance on correct was deliberately removed there). Three of the five games still to port are untimed.

**Game-facing round is now ZERO-BASED.** The shell used to hand games `round + 1` while web handed `roundIndex`, so `plan[api.round]` — correct on web — would have silently skipped round 1 and run off the end of the plan on mobile. `api.round` is now the raw zero-based index on both platforms and ported round code transfers with no off-by-one. The shell's own "Round N of M" display still adds the +1 itself; that is the shell's job, never the game's. `api.secondsLeft` widened to `number | null` at the same time.

**Test surface.** `__tests__/quick-game-shell.test.tsx` 44 → 50 tests: an `untimed games` block (no count-in / no chip / no clock / `timedOut` never fires, prop-omitted equals prop-null, full untimed run still single-POSTs, picker-started untimed run skips the count-in, and a pin that the TIMED path still counts in and shows the chip), plus a dedicated zero-based test asserting the game sees `round-0` while the shell displays "Round 1 of 5". Existing round-label and phrase-id expectations shifted down by one to match the new contract. Suites: mobile 78 suites / 570 tests (was 78 / 564).

**Third correction: RESOLVED RUN LENGTH (August 5, 2026).** `roundsPerRun` is now `?: number | ((phrases: Phrase[]) => number)`, mirroring web's `totalRounds(phrases)`. A number still fixes the length at mount, unchanged. A resolver is called with the fetched pool ONLY once that pool clears `def.floor` (web parity, so it never sees a half-loaded list), and its answer — `resolvedRounds` — is what every downstream consumer reads: round progression, the finish condition, `api.total`, "Round N of M", the progress bar and the end screen. The raw prop is read nowhere else. Without this, a game whose length depends on its data (a pairs board capped at `min(6, phrases.length)`) had to hardcode a number, and the picker admits any category at or above the floor: a floor-4 game hardcoding 6, given a 4-phrase category, builds a 4-pair board whose `nextRound` reaches 4 and never satisfies `>= 6` — `finishRun` never fires, so no POST, no result screen, and the learner's completed work is silently discarded. **Degenerate guard:** `roundsReady = poolReady && resolvedRounds >= 1`, so a resolver returning 0 (or a fraction) refuses to enter the run at all and shows its own `quick-no-rounds` message ("Not enough here for a full round"), distinct from the floor message — the pool DID clear the floor, and blaming the floor sends the learner looking for the wrong thing. Refusing is recoverable (pick another topic); entering is not. Test surface: 7 added, including the dead-run scenario end to end and the retired hardcoded shape kept as its witness. Suites: mobile 80 / 620 (was 80 / 613).

### Ticket Check ported to mobile (August 5, 2026, first quick game on the shell)

`app/(app)/(tabs)/games/ticket-check.tsx`. The filename is load-bearing: the hub navigates with `router.push(`/(app)/(tabs)/games/${game.id}`)`, so a game is a route file whose name equals its id plus a row in the hub `GAMES` constant. No server file was touched — `serverGame: 'listen-and-pick'` comes off the roster entry, so the frozen server game-id enum is untouched and correctness stays server-recomputed from `selectedPhraseId`.

**Planner.** `buildPlan` is exported, pure and module-level, and owns a local Fisher-Yates rather than reaching into the shell. Faithful to web: shuffle the pool ONCE and walk it with a cursor, reshuffling only when the cursor passes the end (this is what spreads anchors evenly over a short category — a random anchor per round repeats some phrases and never shows others). `correctIdx` is derived by `findIndex` AFTER the final shuffle and never carried through it; carrying it is how these planners rot into marking the wrong tile correct, which is silent and teaches the wrong answer. ROUNDS 8, CHOICES 4.

**Untimed.** `secondsPerRound` is omitted entirely, which is the whole reason the shell gained optional timing. The reveal rides an explicit "Tap to continue" for BOTH outcomes; web deliberately deleted the 700ms auto-advance on correct answers so the learner sets how long the romanized and meaning lines stay up. Do not add a clock here.

**Hub registration side effects, both by design and both fallback-safe.** The roster grades the game `Easy` but the hub's own scale is Beginner/Intermediate/Advanced, so it registers as Beginner. `GAME_COLORS` and `VIGNETTES` in `components/games/GamePreview.tsx` are keyed by game id with fallbacks: an unmapped game gets `FALLBACK_COLOR` (neutral slate) and `GamePreview` renders the `fallback` prop, which the hub supplies as the game's Feather glyph. So the tile shows a static icon rather than an animated vignette — correct, just not yet bespoke. Note that `__tests__/games-hub-vignettes.test.tsx` pins difficulty-pill COUNTS, so any new hub game fails it until the count is updated.

**Tests.** `__tests__/ticket-check.test.tsx`, 16 tests: planner invariants re-derive `correctIdx` independently over 200 runs rather than trusting the field, plus distinct-distractor, floor-pool and cursor-spread pins; the continue beat gated on both outcomes; and a full 8-round run through the real shell asserting no POST before the end, exactly one POST at it, and every picked tile's id travelling in the payload. Suites: mobile 79 suites / 586 tests (was 78 / 570).

### Trackside signals ported to mobile (August 5, 2026, bolo-mobile only)

The journey map's crossing encounters, ported from web. Zone closeout is NOT part of this — the map now seats signals, opens the encounter, waves through and launches a context-carrying quick game, and nothing else. No server file, no web file and no shared-lib extraction: `lib/tracksideSignals.ts` deliberately duplicates the web planner rather than hoisting it, because the two platforms must be able to disagree in a diff review.

**Seating is derived, never inserted.** `planTracksideSignals(total)` seats one signal after every ODD global stop with `stop < totalStations`, so the line never ends on a crossing and the terminus is always reachable. The gap number N is an identity, not a label: `gap-N` is the `contextRef` the launch carries and the server's grant ledger is keyed on, so `signalContextRef` is the single spelling and the planner is unit-tested on its own rather than through the map. Four things the port had to avoid, all of which are silent rather than loud: signal positions are a SEPARATE array derived off `stationPts` and are never pushed into `pts` (`segs` draws the rail between consecutive pts, so a signal point would route the actual track through the crossing); signals render as absolutely positioned Views over the ScrollView content, never inside the per-zone `<Svg>` slices (the map is sliced per zone for scroll perf, so a boundary signal would straddle two slices and be clipped by one); they sit in the same NON-parallax layer as the stations and rail, never the scenery layer, whose 0.03 parallax factor would drift a signal out of register with its own gap; and the card collision is really checked — mobile lays out its own stop cards, so the signal recomputes `side`/`boxLeft`/`boxWidth` with the station row's own formula, takes the flank opposite the card, and is pushed clear if the boxes actually overlap. A showroom (plan-locked) line seats no signals at all, so a map the learner has not bought can never advertise Chai the server would refuse to grant.

**State is one geometry, four states.** `components/journey/SignalGlyph.tsx` is a react-native-svg port of the web glyph, same palette, and the states differ only in arm rotation and lamp fill: upcoming (down/red, no halo), active (down/red plus the amber halo that marks the crossing blocking you NOW), waved (up/amber), cleared (up/green). Precedence when deriving it: server clears, then local clears, then server waves, then local waves, then `stopDone ? active : upcoming` — cleared is checked before waved on both sides, so a later clear always supersedes an earlier wave. `rewardChai` comes off the zone payload (`LessonGroupList.signals`, already served, no client change needed), never a constant.

**Memory layer is hydrated, not read at render.** `lib/signalMemory.ts`. Web reads sessionStorage/localStorage synchronously during render; AsyncStorage cannot, and reading at render time would flash every signal through a wrong state. So waves and stop-seen are in-memory only (an in-memory Set dies with the app process exactly as sessionStorage dies with the tab), and device-scoped clears are hydrated ONCE into a cache that render reads through synchronous accessors. `useSignalMemory` exposes `hydrated` plus a `version` that bumps on every mark to re-derive the map. Hydration merges rather than replaces, so a clear marked while the disk read was in flight is not thrown away. The local cleared mark is written only where the server actually granted — `QuickGameShell`'s success path, gated on `chaiGranted > 0` — so display state can never outrun the ledger and retire a claimable reward.

**Encounter.** `components/journey/SignalEncounter.tsx`, its own Modal in the journey's existing modal idiom (web keeps a second Dialog for the same reason: signal state and lock state are unrelated). The reward chip shows ONLY while the grant is unclaimed, so a cleared replay promises nothing; "Wave me through" disappears once cleared. Soft stop auto-opens the encounter once per signal per session when the run is actually held at that crossing, suppressed while any other dialog is open, and gated on `hydrated` so a genuinely cleared signal cannot burn its one auto-open showing a stale state; a manual tap counts as seen. Waving marks locally, POSTs `useRecordSignalWave`, re-derives, closes, and toasts without shame; the auto-wave case (no game fits the zone) has its own Green flag copy.

**Tests.** `__tests__/journey-signals.test.tsx` (22) covers seating purity and the never-on-the-terminus invariant, contextRef spelling, rotation including its null auto-wave case, the four glyph states, the reward-chip suppression rules, and the memory layer (session vs device scope across a simulated restart, per-language isolation, the hydration race, and the hook's `hydrated`/`version` contract). `journey-map.test.tsx` gained the soft stop, the wave POST payload with optimistic flip, the cleared-replay ledger rule, the launch params, and once-per-session; `journey-showroom.test.tsx` pins zero interactive signals. Both journey files use FULL-REPLACEMENT api-client mocks, so `useRecordSignalWave` had to be added to each or the file dies at render. Suites: mobile 80 suites / 608 tests (was 79 / 586).

**Known gaps, deliberate.** Web zeroes its rail comet at a held signal; mobile does not, because the spec did not ask and porting it would force a rewrite of the pinned comet-dot test whose fixture happens to hold a signal. The dialog's scene is engine + rule + signalman + glyph, matching web's scene order: `components/journey/SignalmanGlyph.tsx` is a coordinate-for-coordinate react-native-svg transcription of the web glyph (26×40 viewBox, same paint order, same brand fills, no motion because web has none), added so the wave-through copy does not name a character who is absent from the scene.

### Express Listening: round-pinned audio + an audio-required entry gate (August 5, 2026, web only)

Two defects in `pages/games/express-listening.tsx`, both in the audio path that IS the game — the four choices render `nativeScript` only, so the clip is the entire prompt.

**Round-pinning (was absent).** The playback path now copies mobile listen-and-pick's captured-index shape exactly: a `roundRef` mirrors `api.round`, the index is captured BEFORE the synthesis await, and the resolved clip bails before constructing the `Audio` if the round has moved on. Previously the only guard was the stop-previous at the top of the NEXT call, which cannot help a clip that resolves into a round that has already started — it played the old question's audio over the new one. The cache write happens BEFORE the bail on purpose: a late clip is still worth banking, it is only the playback that is stale. The bail also clears `isPlaying`, or the replay button stays disabled on a clip that never played.

**Cache key gained the voice.** `${phrase.id}:${ttsVoice}` (from `useGetAccount().data.preferences.learning.ttsVoice ?? "auto"`, `ttsVoice` in the `playPhrase` dep array), matching mobile's five audio games. On phrase id alone a mid-session voice change replayed the old voice's clip for the rest of the run. Express Listening is the FIRST web game to key on the voice; the other five web audio games still key on id alone and have the same latent bug.

**Audio-required entry gate (new frame capability).** `requiresAudio` on `QuickGameShell`, opt-in, Express Listening its only caller. It is deliberately NOT the inverse of mobile's `usesAudio`: that one declares a game silent so the mute button disappears, this one declares audio load-bearing so entry is refused while sound is off. Owner ruling: block at entry with a message, never mid-round. Mechanics: `audioBlocked` is evaluated ONCE per run entry (pinned launch at mount, Choose Topic, Play Again) and never re-armed, so muting mid-run leaves that run to finish and the gate catches the next entry; unmuting clears it immediately in that one direction only, making the header's existing mute button the way in. Nothing auto-unmutes and nothing writes the sound preference. The gate also has to suppress the clock: it feeds `roundsReady` (so the 3-2-1 count-in never starts) and the per-round timer effect, or a learner reading the message would be timed out behind it.

**Flagged judgement (mid-run mute).** The run continues silently, which is the least surprising thing and what the shell does naturally — but that run is effectively lost, since the remaining rounds show four unlabeled native-script tiles against a 7s clock. Killing the run instead would discard completed rounds with no POST, which is worse. If this proves wrong in the wild, the alternative is to let the learner finish the current round and end the run there.

**Tests.** New `src/test/express-listening.test.tsx`, 11 tests: entry blocked/allowed, unmute-clears-the-gate, mute-mid-run-does-not-interrupt, autoplay at 1.25x, replay-from-cache, the late clip dropped, the voice-bust, both advance beats (700ms auto / manual continue) and a full 8-round timeout run asserting every submitted id is the first non-target choice. Harness note: the frame's round clock chains one `setTimeout` per second and schedules the next only after React commits, so the seconds must be advanced one `act()` at a time — a single `advanceTimersByTimeAsync(8000)` ticks it exactly once.

### Ticket Check declared silent (August 5, 2026)

`usesAudio={false}` on `app/(app)/(tabs)/games/ticket-check.tsx`, one prop. Ticket Check speaks nothing — no synthesis, no playback, no `soundOn` or `setAudioPlaying` read; its only feedback channel is haptics — but it shipped as the FIRST game on the shell, before the silent-game declaration existed, so it inherited the `usesAudio` default of `true` and offered a live mute control over silence for four ports. The three later silent ports (Signal Lights, Wrong Platform, Luggage Match) each declared it at port time.

**Audit, all ten mobile game routes**: this was the only instance. The four shell games are now all declared correctly; the five audio games (listen-and-pick, word-match, speed-round, phrase-builder, bolo-quiz) predate the shell, own their own `GameMuteButton`, and really do speak; `script-trace` is silent but is also pre-shell and renders no mute button at all, so it has nothing to opt out of. The defect can only exist for a shell game, and the shape to check is: on the shell, no synthesis or playback import, no `usesAudio` prop.

### Luggage Match ported to mobile (August 5, 2026, fourth quick game, first PERSISTENT BOARD)

`app/(app)/(tabs)/games/luggage-match.tsx`, floor 4, UNTIMED, silent. Two racks of luggage tags — native script left, English right — paired up; exported `buildBoard(phrases)` and `pairCount(phrases)`.

**Run length is POOL-DEPENDENT**, the first ported game that is: `min(6, pool)` pairs via the shell's `roundsPerRun` resolver, one round per pair.

**The board is persistent, and that is the structural difference from the three earlier ports.** It is built ONCE per run (`useState` initializer) and rounds turn over underneath it, so there is no per-round advance beat and nothing is re-derived per round. The round therefore holds state for a whole run — a matched set and a first-wrong map — and resets NOTHING itself; it depends entirely on the shell's run key remounting it between runs.

**The first-wrong scoring model.** Only a CORRECT pair submits; a wrong attempt submits nothing. A pair solved first try submits the phrase matched to itself; a pair that took a wrong attempt first submits the counterpart it was wrongly paired with, and only the FIRST wrong attempt counts (`firstWrong` is a Map held in state and mutated in place on purpose — it must not trigger a render). Every id on the board is in-category, so unlike Wrong Platform there is no never-submit id; the invariant tested instead is that every submitted id is drawn from the board's chosen pairs and the submission count equals the pair count exactly.

**A wrong pairing tints both tags red for 450ms**, during which taps are dead, then clears both picks. Web names the state `shakeIds` but nothing actually shakes — it is a tint only, ported verbatim (owner-ruled; no shake invented for mobile). Tag treatment reuses word-match's matched/error colours. The pending 450ms timer lives in a ref cleared on round change and unmount.

**Web divergence, resolved.** Roster grades it `Easy` and the web hub hardcodes `Beginner` — these agree, unlike Wrong Platform. Registered FREE / Beginner / `briefcase` with the roster description; `__tests__/games-hub-vignettes.test.tsx` pill counts move to Beginner 4 / Intermediate 4 / Advanced 1. Static fallback tile, no vignette art.

### Wrong Platform ported to mobile (August 5, 2026, third quick game on the shell)

`app/(app)/(tabs)/games/wrong-platform.tsx`, floor 3, UNTIMED, silent. Odd-one-out: three phrases from the chosen topic plus one stray fetched from a second category by the round itself (first category that is not this one and has phrases), exactly as web does.

**The scoring model is indirect and is the breakable part.** The stray belongs to another category, so its id would fail the server's in-category validation and must NEVER be submitted. Each round therefore carries a unique in-category ANCHOR it is scored through: a correct spot submits the anchor matched to itself, a wrong pick submits the anchor matched to a different in-category id — including the case where the learner taps the anchor's own tile, which falls back to another local, because anchor-matched-to-anchor would score a miss as a hit. The planner keeps `anchor`, `locals`, `stray` and `options` as separate fields for this reason, and no correct INDEX is ever carried through a shuffle (the stray's tile position is re-derived at render).

**Two advance beats, deliberately not unified.** A correct spot auto-advances after 700ms (web's number — not Signal Lights' 650); a miss renders "Tap to continue" and waits, so the learner can study which tile was the stray. The pending 700ms timer is held in a ref and cleared on round change and unmount.

**Untimed means there is no timeout path at all.** `secondsPerRound` is omitted, so no clock, no chip, no count-in, and `api.timedOut` never becomes true — pinned by a test that sits on a round for 60s and asserts nothing moves, since the shell offers a clock and a future edit could switch one on unnoticed.

**Ported verbatim from web, both deliberate:** a one-topic language shows "This game needs phrases from a second topic. Play another game for now." rather than faking a stray from the same topic, and the board shows a spinner (RN `ActivityIndicator`, the equivalent of web's border spinner) while the second topic loads. Hub tile behavior is unchanged; gating is banked separately.

**Hub registration.** FREE (matching the web hub) and Intermediate, from the roster's `Medium` grade. NOTE a cross-platform divergence: the WEB hub lists this game as Beginner while the roster says Medium; mobile's only difficulty source is the roster, so mobile shows Intermediate. Static fallback tile, no vignette art (banked art-pipeline item). `__tests__/games-hub-vignettes.test.tsx` pill counts move to Beginner 3 / Intermediate 4 / Advanced 1.

### Silent games declare it; the shell drops the mute toggle (August 5, 2026, bolo-mobile only)

`components/games/QuickGameShell.tsx` gained `usesAudio?: boolean`, defaulting to TRUE so every shipped game keeps the toggle with no edit. A game passing `false` gets no `GameMuteButton` at all — not disabled and not dimmed, because a control that cannot change anything invites a press that does nothing and reads as a defect. The header is `space-between`, so the toggle's slot is replaced by a fixed 44pt ballast View (`styles.headerSlot`) or the title slides off centre. The round contract is unchanged: a silent game still receives `soundOn` and `setAudioPlaying`, it simply never uses them. Same shape as `secondsPerRound` — one per-game declaration, the shell owns the chrome. First and only caller: mobile Signal Lights, whose port carries no clip synthesis (web's round audio is deferred to build 35; flipping this back to the default is the whole edit when it lands).

## 10a. Recognizer language pinning and web min-duration guard (August 3, 2026)

- **Diagnosis (prod evidence, Aug 3):** `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe` treats the STT `language` param as a soft hint and can drift to another language entirely on short clips even when the hint is delivered (the server OpenAI client uses `OPENAI_API_KEY` directly with no proxy, and the hint has been live since July 24). Prod attempt 213 showed the mini pass in Russian Cyrillic while the HQ pass read the correct Devanagari; an Aug 3 nocatch sidecar shows the same family with a Perso-Arabic mini transcript. The conservative dual-pass chooser picks the farther transcript and the script-mismatch guard routes it to nocatch. That handling is correct (system miss, no penalty); the drift is a model limitation, not a wiring bug.
- **Fix 1 (server):** POST `/openai/pronunciation` requests without a `phraseId` previously carried no `languageCode` at all: no STT hint, no script-mismatch guard, full auto-detect. The route now resolves the session language server-side from `users.activeLanguage` (fail-open: a user with no recorded active language keeps auto-detect), and the `languagesTable` name/speechCapability lookup runs whenever a code is pinned, so the `speechCapability === "unsupported"` backstop now also applies on the no-phraseId path.
- **Fix 2 (web):** `practice.tsx` enforces `MIN_CLIP_SECONDS = 0.8` (the pilot rule from `qa/pilot-pronunciation-v2.mjs` `MIN_CLIP_DURATION`) right after `stopRecording()` resolves, using the recorder's wall-clock `getLastDurationSeconds()`. Short holds get the existing local "Didn't catch that one" card; no request is sent, nothing is scored or counted. Recorder test mocks must stub `getLastDurationSeconds` (all web recorder mocks were patched in one pass).
- **Banked / known debt:** `attempts.audio_duration_ms` is never populated by the live path (NULL on every prod row), so duration distributions are unmeasurable from the DB; nocatch sidecars carry no duration, mime, or user-agent fields. Server-side duration enforcement needs a request-shape addition (codegen) or ffprobe. Mobile has no 0.8 s clip guard (it has its own 300 ms min-hold). Safari capture was ruled out as a defect (the HQ pass transcribed the same clips correctly), and no pre-roll fix is supported by evidence (short-clip fragments contain the word onset).

## 10b. Referral R1: server-side referral codes and rewards (August 4, 2026)

**Scope.** api workspace only (plus lib/db, lib/api-spec docs). No web or mobile UI; offer codes, daily limits, and signal/game surfaces untouched.

**Schema (migration `drizzle/0040_referral.sql`).** `users.referral_code`: nullable text, unique via `users_referral_code_idx`, minted lazily on the first GET /referral (JIT, like the users row). `referral_redemptions` (`lib/db/src/schema/referralRedemptions.ts`): referrer_user_id, referee_user_id (UNIQUE `referral_redemptions_referee_idx`, once-ever per referee), code (stored normalized), created_at, activated_at, granted_at. R1 sets activated_at and granted_at together; they are separate columns so later slices can split activation from payout without DDL.

**Economy (`lib/tokenEconomy.ts`).** `REFERRAL_REWARD_REFERRER_CHAI = 25`, `REFERRAL_REWARD_REFEREE_CHAI = 25`; `TokenReason` gains `earn_referral_referrer` / `earn_referral_referee` (type-only; ledger reason column is text).

**Lib (`api-server/src/lib/referral.ts`).** Code alphabet `ABCDEFGHJKLMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789` (no 0/O/1/I), length 6. `getOrCreateReferralCode` retries on unique-index collision and read-backs a concurrent mint. `redeemReferralCode` is attribution ONLY; the already-redeemed check runs before the code lookup so a repeat attempt never leaks whether another code exists; unique-index race resolves to already_redeemed. `activateReferralIfPending` runs grants BEFORE the granted_at claim update so a crash between the two heals on the next call (grants no-op against the ledger unique key). Owner copy lives in `REFERRAL_COPY`, exact strings.

**Routes (`routes/referral.ts`, mounted for all authed learners).** GET /referral returns code, pendingCount, activatedCount (redemptions split on granted_at), chaiEarned (ledger sum, reason earn_referral_referrer, never a stored counter). POST /referral/redeem: 201 `{redeemed:true}`; 409 repeat, 400 self-referral, 404 unknown, each with the owner copy.

**Activation hook.** The server keeps no discrete practice session-complete marker; the Chai receipt rides POST /attempts. So activation is a Promise.all element there: the referee's first recorded attempt activates the pending redemption and grants both sides. Errors are warn-logged and never block the attempt response.

**RIDE-ALONGs.** (1) POST /game-sessions response now carries the server-authoritative `passed` (sessionPassed, the flag that gates signal/closeout grants); openapi.yaml documents it plus the two referral endpoints and three referral schemas. openapi codegen deliberately not run (server-only slice; the web slice owns client generation). (2) feedbackTts pending-join flake fixed test-side: the fake pending in `feedbackTts.test.ts` now writes the ttsCache row before resolving, honoring the production invariant (cache row lands before the pending entry clears).

**Tests.** `src/test/referral.test.ts` (one new file, lib/DB layer against the live dev DB, same harness pattern as signalWaves.test.ts).

**Debt.** None new. Known trap re-confirmed: dev-DB `drizzle-kit migrate` recorded the 0040 hash without executing the DDL; the committed SQL was applied by hand via psql and verified with to_regclass.

## 10b-2. Referral R2: the web slice (August 8, 2026)

R1 shipped the whole server side with nothing user-facing. R2 is web only (no mobile, no share card, no offer codes — referral rewards are Chai only, never subscription time). No R1 server changes: `pnpm --filter @workspace/api-spec run codegen` produced a zero diff, so R1's banked codegen step was already done and `useGetReferral` / `useRedeemReferral` were already in the generated client.

**Carry-through (`src/lib/referral-code.ts`).** No prior pattern existed for surviving signup: `/family/join?invite=` sits behind `Guard`, which bounces a signed-out visitor to `/` and loses the token (a latent family-flow bug, untouched here). The two sessionStorage precedents are unrelated one-bit flags. So R2 introduces a localStorage slot `bolo.referralCode` = `{code, savedAt, attemptedAt?}` with a 30-day TTL. localStorage rather than sessionStorage because signup is not guaranteed to stay in one tab (social sign-up leaves the origin; emailed verification can open a fresh one). `REFERRAL_REWARD_CHAI = 25` mirrors `REFERRAL_REWARD_REFERRER_CHAI`/`_REFEREE_CHAI` (no shared constants package exists); it is the only reward literal on the web side.

**One owner for the call (`src/components/referral-redeemer.tsx`).** A provider mounted above the router inside `QueryClientProvider` is the SINGLE caller of POST /referral/redeem, exposing `{status, message, attempt}`. Two callers (landing page + app-wide effect) would race and show the loser's 409 to a first-time referee. `startedRef` makes it single-flight within one mount; `attemptedAt` (written to storage *before* the POST) makes it single-flight across a reload mid-flight or a second tab — on a 409 where this browser had already attempted, the UI renders **redeemed**, not refused. Storage is cleared on every outcome so a refused code can never loop. Refusal text is echoed from the server's `error` body rather than duplicated client-side, so copy cannot drift from `REFERRAL_COPY`. Note R1's actual status split: 409 repeat, **400** self-referral, **404** unknown.

**Surfaces.** `src/components/referral-card.tsx` renders inside account.tsx's local `Section` (icon `Gift`, "Invite friends"): code, Joined/Pending/Chai earned, copy-link and share (`navigator.share` with clipboard fallback). It is a Chai glyph census site (web total 14 → 15). `src/pages/join.tsx` is the public landing at `/join/:code` and `/join`, deliberately NOT behind `Guard`. It stores the code exactly once per code (a ref guard — re-running would re-store a code the redeemer had just cleared) and never dead-ends: signed-out gets the invite plus a sign-up CTA, signed-in gets confirmed / refused, both with a working way into the app.

**`?redirect_url=` on the auth screens (`src/lib/auth-redirect.ts`).** New passthrough so the landing page can send the referee back to itself after signup instead of silently landing on home. Validated by canonical origin (`new URL(raw, origin).origin === origin`), NOT by prefix: `startsWith("/") && !startsWith("//")` waves through `/\evil.example`, which the URL parser reads as `//evil.example`. Percent-encoded `%5C` is a different, benign case (never re-decoded into a separator) and is pinned as same-origin.

**Tests.** `src/test/referral.test.tsx`, 17 tests. Web suite 625 tests / 78 files, 623 pass; the 2 failures are the pre-existing `games-hub.test.tsx` pair from the games reorder commit, unrelated.

**Debt.** (1) The `/family/join` guarded-invite token loss above. (2) `REFERRAL_REWARD_CHAI` is a hand-mirrored constant, same class of drift as the retired `TIER_PRICING`.

## 10c. Web prices come from Stripe, never from a constant (August 5, 2026)

The web paywall and the public pricing preview showed hardcoded strings, and the Family strings had drifted from Stripe: displayed `$19.99`/mo and `$139.99`/yr against live prices of `$24.99`/mo and `$174.99`/yr (the annual gap was a second, unreported drift; All-Access `$12.99`/`$89.99` did match). Both tiers are genuinely purchasable on web through Stripe Checkout, so the displayed price was a real misquote, not decoration.

Fix: no money string is written down in the client any more. `GET /pricing` (section 4) reports the amounts held by the exact price ids checkout charges, and `artifacts/gujarati-coach/src/lib/pricing.ts` renders them. That module now owns presentation only: `formatMoney` (Intl currency from minor units), the per-interval card copy, the annual monthly-equivalent line ("Just $7.50/mo, billed yearly.", Family "Just $14.58/mo for up to 4 people, billed yearly."), a `cadence` clause for sentences that already say "cancel", and a `Save N%` badge DERIVED from the two real amounts (both tiers currently compute to `Save 42%`, which is why the previously hardcoded badge looked right). `usePricing()` is a plain fetch plus a module-scope cache shared by every surface, deliberately NOT react-query (`upgrade-paywall.test.tsx` mocks `@tanstack/react-query` with `useQueryClient` only, so a `useQuery` call would break that mock). This supersedes the `TIER_PRICING` mention in the section 5 Task 997 paragraph; `TIER_PRICING` no longer exists.

Unavailable-catalog policy (money surface): never show an invented number. Price slots render a pulse placeholder, the annual "or $X/yr" line and the savings badge are omitted entirely, and fine print falls back to price-free phrasing ("7 days free. Cancel anytime before the trial ends and you won't be charged."). Checkout itself is unaffected; Stripe shows the real price.

Consumers updated: `pages/upgrade.tsx` (plan cards, interval badge, fine print), `pages/landing.tsx` (pricing preview, both tiers, monthly + annual), `pages/subscription.tsx` (Family upsell), `pages/family.tsx` (NoneView). Copy fix in the upgrade fine print: the trial sentence used to append "Cancel anytime before the trial ends..." after a `note` that already ended "Cancel anytime.", so it read as two cancel instructions; it is now one sentence, "7 days free, then $12.99/mo, billed monthly. Cancel anytime before the trial ends and you won't be charged."

`scripts/seedStripeProducts.ts` Family constants were also stale (1999/13999) and are corrected to 2499/17499; that script only seeds a NEW Stripe account and matches by `unit_amount`, so leaving them stale risked creating a second, wrong price on a re-run.

Tests: `src/test/pricing.test.tsx` (formatting, monthly equivalent, derived badge, absent interval, cached/fetch/error paths of `usePricing`) plus `src/test/setup.ts` seeding the live ladder from `PRICING_CATALOG` in `src/test/fixtures.ts` so component price assertions stay deterministic without a network call. Update that fixture when the Stripe prices change.

## 10d. chaiGranted receipt derives from the ledger insert, not a balance compare (August 5, 2026)

**Defect.** `POST /game-sessions` in `artifacts/api-server/src/routes/learning.ts` inferred its Chai receipt by reading `getOrCreateTokenState` before the grant and comparing to the balance `grantTokens` returned after it. `lib/tokenService.ts` documents that exact approach as unreliable: a concurrent unrelated earn (streak day, monthly allowance) landing between the two reads moves the balance and is indistinguishable from this session's grant, so the route could report Chai the session did not earn. `grantTokensDetailed` has exposed a `granted` boolean for this since Hotfix 3S, and the attempt-response Chai receipt (`attemptChaiEarned`, same file) already used it — game-sessions was the last caller on the compare.

**Fix.** Both grant paths on the route (signal and closeout) now call `grantTokensDetailed` and set `chaiGranted` from `granted`. `getOrCreateTokenState` is no longer called in this block; both it and `grantTokens` keep other callers in the file, so no imports changed.

**Deliberately unchanged.** Response shape (`chaiGranted` still omitted entirely when there was no grant), validation rules, amounts (`signalFirstClearChai`, `CLOSEOUT_FIRST_CHAI`), the pass gate, once-ever ledger idempotency, `tokenService`, and every client. Behavior differs only in the concurrent-grant case the compare got wrong.

**Tests.** `src/test/gameSessions.test.ts` gains a 7-test suite (item 9 in the file header) mirroring the route's derivation, as `validateContextRef` already mirrors its post-parse check — this codebase's api tests have no HTTP harness. Covers: receipt present on a real first clear; absent on a spent refId; absent for hub and for absent context; absent on a failed session with the refId left claimable for a later passing run; and a concurrency regression that carries the retired algorithm as an inline witness — it drives an unrelated streak-day earn into the window *between* the old algorithm's two balance reads, asserts the old compare DOES mis-report a receipt there, then asserts the shipped derivation reports nothing for the same already-spent refId. Landing the unrelated earn before the call instead of between the reads leaves the old algorithm passing, so that ordering is the whole point of the test; a first draft got it wrong and was caught in review.

**Suite state:** api 839 tests / 45 suites, 838 pass / 1 fail — the documented `PILOT_CAPTURE_USER_IDS` env trap, unrelated. Typecheck clean across all workspaces. Note the drift against older totals recorded in this doc: those were measured before the Hotfix 3S, Signal polish, Referral R1 and Pricing commits added api tests. Pre-change total on this HEAD was 832 / 44.

## 10e. Chacha-ji's Chai Stall — scene vignette + kulhad glyph (August 6, 2026)
