**Two-tier treatment, built once for both platforms (owner rulings).** (1) SCENE: the stall is a FULL-WIDTH BAND on home at the art's natural 1024/572 aspect, directly above the boarding pass so the pass reads as standing in front of the stall, and TAPPING IT OPENS THE CHAI WALLET — the same sheet the Chai stat cell opens, never a second wallet surface. It first shipped at wallet-vignette scale (56px, right-aligned above the stats banner) and decorative; at that size a detailed scene read as a stray thumbnail rather than a place, so the owner corrected scale and placement the same day. The scene is therefore no longer decoration: it carries an accessible name ("Chacha-ji's Chai stall — open your Chai wallet"). The components still support the decorative form — omit `onClick`/`onPress` and the box returns to `aria-hidden` + `pointer-events-none` (web) / a11y-hidden and unpressable (mobile) — but home passes a handler on both platforms. The band also NAMES ITSELF and shows the live balance (see the overlay paragraph below), which is what turns it from art-you-can-tap into a wallet surface. (2) GLYPH: the kulhad (clay chai cup) is Chai's inline mark and replaces the Coffee/`coffee` icon at every surface that shows a Chai AMOUNT. Ambient steam only: one slow looping plume over the kettle, layer-mapped from day one. Pour-on-earn is deliberately BANKED, not built.

**The scene band was cropped to its top-left corner on iOS (fixed August 7, 2026, mobile).** On device the band showed only the tarp and tree canopy — no kettle, no counter, no railway platform — while Expo web and the web twin were correct. Cause: the scene `Image` carried `style={StyleSheet.absoluteFill}` and nothing else. The four insets alone do not give an RN `Image` a layout size on iOS; it takes its INTRINSIC size (1024x572 **points**) anchored top-left, the band's `overflow: 'hidden'` crops away everything past the first ~350x196pt, and `resizeMode="cover"` scales nothing because the frame already equals the intrinsic size. The fix is `style={[StyleSheet.absoluteFill, styles.fillImage]}` — explicit `width/height: '100%'`, which is what web's `absolute inset-0 h-full w-full object-cover` always had and what the chachaji/steam layers in the same file already used; the scene was the only layer missing it. **RN-web maps the bare insets straight to CSS, so Expo web renders this bug perfectly and only a device or a geometry check catches it.** Diagnosed by cropping the source PNG to the predicted top-left 350x196 region and matching it against the owner's screenshot, not by reading the layout code. Also corrected in the same pass: the declared art constants disagreed with the actual files — the scene is 1024x**572** (both platforms said 574) and `steam.png` is **226x485** (mobile said 232/487). The scene number governs the aspect box that `cover` is measured against and that every KETTLE/CHACHAJI fraction is a fraction OF; mobile derives the plume's height as `width / STEAM_ASPECT`, so a wrong steam number is a wrong plume shape (web escapes it because `<img>` takes height from the intrinsic aspect). Tests on both platforms pinned the old `1024 / 574` and were updated with the source.

**Assets.** Four PNGs, delivered: `stall.png` (1024×574 scene), `kulhad.png`, `steam.png` (232×487 isolated plume), `chachaji.png` (386×520, see the Chacha-ji layer below). Web serves them from `artifacts/gujarati-coach/public/stall/` via a `STALL_ASSETS` BASE_URL path registry (the house delivery pattern — `chai-wallet.tsx`'s `VIGNETTE_SRC`, `brand-splash.tsx`'s `SPLASH_V2_ASSETS`); mobile can't read the web public folder, so the same three files are copied to `artifacts/bolo-mobile/assets/images/stall/` (mirroring `assets/images/mascot/`) behind a Metro `require` registry of the same name. Final art swaps in by editing the registries. The scene art replaced a `stall.jpg` with `stall.png`.

**Kettle layer map.** The plume's placement lives in ONE fraction map per platform — `KETTLE = { left 21%, bottom 46%, width 12% }` of the scene box, identical in `chai-stall.tsx` and `ChaiStall.tsx`. If the scene art moves the kettle, those three numbers change in both files and nothing else does. The scene is an aspect box (1024/572) filled by a `cover` image of the SAME aspect, so nothing is cropped and the fractions stay true at any size — which is why the jump from 56px to full width needed no re-mapping (pinned by a test on each platform rather than assumed). Web states the offsets as CSS percentages, so they are scale-free by construction; mobile needs real pixels for the plume box and the translateY amplitude, so the band reports its size via `onLayout` (`width: '100%'` + `aspectRatio`, Yoga derives the height) instead of the caller declaring a height — before the first layout the plume simply measures zero.

**Motion and reduced motion.** Web: pure CSS in `components/chai-stall.css` (7s `chai-steam-rise`, opacity + translateY/scale), so the global `prefers-reduced-motion` reset in `index.css` (`*:not(.animate-spin)`) collapses it for free. There is intentionally NO `animation-fill-mode`: with no fill the collapsed animation settles on the authored base declarations — a visible plume at rest, never a blank layer — whereas `forwards`/`both` would strand it on the last keyframe (and leave an identity transform behind, the containing-block trap). Mobile: Reanimated `withRepeat` on opacity + translateY ONLY, gated on `useReducedMotion`; the plume's absolute placement is a plain static style because animating layout props (top/left/bottom) inside `useAnimatedStyle` crashes Expo Go on the New Architecture. The reduced-motion decision is extracted into an exported `steamLoop(reduceMotion)` so the ruling is testable without reaching through worklets.

**Chacha-ji is a LAYER, never baked into the scene (August 6, 2026).** He renders as his own image over `stall.png` on both platforms, because the banked pour-on-earn moment has to be able to animate him and a painted-in figure makes that impossible. The art is the EXISTING greeting figure (`attached_assets/generated_images/chachaji-greeting.png`, a clean full-body figure on transparency), trimmed to its bounding box and resized to 386×520 — no new art. The wallet-vignette PNG (`public/mascot/chachaji-wallet-vignette.png`, still the wallet sheet's own image) was NOT usable here: it is isolated on transparency but hard-cropped at the forearms, so as a top layer it would read as a floating bust rather than a man standing at his stall. Placement is a fraction map in the KETTLE style — `CHACHAJI = { left 48.5%, bottom 17%, width 19.5% }` of the scene box, identical in both files, height derived from the art's own aspect (386/520 on mobile, intrinsic on web) so scale stays ONE number. He stands on the open dirt beside the stall, to the right of the awning support pole. **`bottom` is measured, not chosen:** the pole meets the dirt at b≈17 (its base, with the grass tufts), traced by scanning `stall.png` for the pole's dark span row by row, and that is the ground line at that depth; his soles sit on it. Two earlier passes picked a bottom by eye and both floated — out on the dirt at width 13.5%/bottom 19% he read too small on a device, and behind the counter at bottom 37.5% his feet landed partway up the dirt with nothing under them. `left` clears the pole entirely: the pole LEANS, x45.2% at its base to x48.3% at the top of its visible run, so 48.5% keeps his whole silhouette (including the raised hand) to its right. He clears the kettle (x19–30%), the plume, the hanging lantern (x50–56%, b75–88 — his head tops out at b≈63) and both ends of the overlay row. Verification is the plume's method plus a zoom: composite the real art at candidate fractions in a headless browser at native 1024×574, then crop the soles and the pole at 3× and look. Useful art geometry, measured off `stall.png`: counter surface x6–47% with its back edge b34–37; kulhad stacks x8–18% and x30–43%, up to b46; platform stone base meets the dirt at b≈24–26.

**Perspective note.** At this depth a strictly correct figure would be about 13.5% wide (the counter reads ~0.9 m over b18→b35, which puts a 1.75 m man near 31 points of frame height, against 45.7 for the 19.5% art). 19.5% is deliberate owner-chosen scale license — he reads as the proprietor rather than a distant bystander. Anyone re-deriving the map from perspective alone will get a smaller number and should not "fix" it. He sits UNDER the legibility scrim with the rest of the art, so the title and balance keep their guaranteed contrast. Decorative on both platforms — `aria-hidden` + `pointer-events-none` (web); on mobile a `View` carries the placement and `pointerEvents="none"` because `Image` takes no such prop, and the scene is already out of the a11y tree. The band still has exactly ONE tap target: a click on Chacha-ji opens the wallet, like a click anywhere else on the band.

**Overlay: title + live balance (August 6, 2026).** The band carries a bottom row — "Chacha-ji's Chai Stall" bottom-left (exported as `STALL_TITLE` on web, an identical local constant on mobile), the balance right-aligned with the kulhad glyph and the same small uppercase "Chai" unit the wallet sheet uses. THE BALANCE IS A PROP (`balance?: number`), never an internal query: both home screens already hold one `useGetTokens()` for the Chai stat cell, so the band reads that, and the two can never disagree after a spend (spends are server-authoritative, every surface refetches on change). Undefined renders the same `-` the stat cell and wallet show while the query is in flight. Legibility over bright photographic art follows the house pair rather than a dedicated component (none existed): a bottom gradient scrim (web `bg-gradient-to-t from-black/75 via-black/40 to-transparent h-2/5`; mobile the `LinearGradient` used by home's bottom fade and the pass shimmer) plus white text carrying the text-over-art shadow already used on the ticket (`drop-shadow-[…]` web, `textShadow*` mobile). The scrim spans the FULL WIDTH, so legibility does not depend on the art staying dark at one end, and stops at 40% height — below the plume, which starts at 46%, so the steam layer is untouched. Quiet by ruling: white on scrim, no accent fill, small sizes, so it does not fight the orange boarding pass directly beneath it. ONE tap target survives: the overlay is `pointer-events-none` text inside the existing button (web) / inside the already-unpressable scene View (mobile), and the mobile scene is now a11y-hidden UNCONDITIONALLY (it was `!onPress`) so a screen reader finds the Pressable and nothing else. The accessible name is unchanged and deliberately omits the balance — the Chai stat cell on the same screen already announces it.

**Glyph census — 8 web, 8 mobile (NOT 6/6).** Web (`<ChaiGlyph className=…>`, lucide `Coffee` dropped from each import): `components/chai-stall.tsx` (the band's own balance readout), `components/chai-wallet.tsx` ×2 (Equip · 5, Start · 10), `pages/home.tsx` (Chai StatCell), `pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx` (`chai-earn-beat`), `pages/games/speed-round.tsx` (+N Chai earned), `pages/journey.tsx` (`signal-chai-chip`), `pages/practice.tsx` (`session-chai-pill`). Mobile (`<ChaiGlyph size=…>`): `components/ChaiStall.tsx` (the band's own balance readout), `app/(app)/(tabs)/index.tsx` (Chai `GradientStatCell`), `components/ChaiWallet.tsx` ×3 (balance badge, Equip · 5, Start · 10), `components/journey/SignalEncounter.tsx` (`signal-chai-chip`), `app/(app)/practice/[id].tsx` (`session-chai-pill`). The asymmetry is real, not a miss: web's wallet balance row shows the Chacha-ji vignette where mobile shows an icon badge, and web's speed-round / quick-game-frame earn chips have no mobile twin that renders an icon. `GradientStatCell`'s `icon` prop widened from `keyof typeof Feather.glyphMap` to `| 'chai'`, which selects the glyph.

**Deliberately unchanged.** Chai amounts, earning, spending, wallet behavior, and every server route. The Utensils TOPIC icon stays Coffee on web (`lib/category-icons.tsx`) and `'coffee'` on mobile (`lib/ui.ts`) — it marks a phrasebook topic, not a currency, and both are pinned by a test so the exclusion doesn't look like an oversight.

**Tests.** `artifacts/gujarati-coach/src/test/chai-stall.test.tsx` (14) and `artifacts/bolo-mobile/__tests__/chai-stall.test.tsx` (15), plus a tap-opens-the-wallet test and a balance-parity test per platform in the home harnesses that already cover the Chai stat cell (`src/test/home-stats-banner.test.tsx`, `__tests__/home-quiz-gate.test.tsx`) — those assert the band opens the SAME `chai-wallet-sheet`, so a second wallet surface would fail. Both stall files assert the rendered art, the layered scene, the full-width band (web: `w-full` + the `1024 / 574` aspect box; mobile: `width: '100%'` + `aspectRatio`, no fixed height), the kettle map at the new scale, the decorative-vs-button split, the reduced-motion contract, and a CENSUS that compares a per-file COUNT map with `toEqual` (so a failure names the file) plus a platform total — a new Chai surface that forgets the glyph fails the suite rather than shipping a coffee cup next to a kulhad. Both also assert no Coffee/`coffee` icon survives on a censused surface. Mobile queries pass `includeHiddenElements` because the glyph and vignette are a11y-hidden. The overlay adds, per platform: the title renders, the balance renders the value it is given (and the loading `-` when it is not), the scrim spans the full width and stops short of the plume with shadowed white text, and the title and balance add NO second tap target (one button, one accessible name, a click on the balance still fires the handler). The home harnesses cross-check the band's balance against the Chai stat cell from one mocked token query, so a component that fetched its own would fail. CSS/source assertions strip comments first on BOTH platforms: the census sites now include the stall files, whose `ChaiGlyph` docstrings quote the very Coffee/`coffee` element they replaced, and web's CSS file explains why there is no fill-mode — prose must not satisfy, or trip, a check on what a file actually renders.

**Suite state after the overlay and the Chacha-ji layer:** web 70 files / 551 tests pass (was 70 / 545 before the overlay); mobile 85 suites / 728 tests pass (was 85 / 722, one of them the `quick-game-shell.test.tsx` "topic picker" load flake, which did not recur in either run). Typecheck clean across all 7 projects.

## 10f. Zone closeout ported to mobile — two beats, Chai payoff (August 6, 2026, bolo-mobile only)

**Scope.** The journey map's zone closeout, ported from web (`gujarati-coach/src/components/zone-closeout.tsx`). No server file, no web file, no signal-encounter change beyond the suppression interaction. The Chai itself needed nothing: `POST /game-sessions` has carried the closeout grant (`earn_closeout_first`, refId `${languageCode}:${categoryId}`, `CLOSEOUT_FIRST_CHAI = 2`, passing sessions only) since Chunk 6A, and `QuickGameShell` already parsed `ctx=closeout` and posted `context: 'closeout'`. Mobile learners previously earned nothing for finishing a fare zone.

**Two beats (`components/journey/ZoneCloseout.tsx`).** Beat 1 is the RESULT: confetti, cheering Bolo, "Zone N complete!", and a closeout-game CTA. Beat 2 is the PAYOFF: waving Bolo, Chacha-ji's Chai, and a door into the existing `ChaiWalletSheet` (now hosted by the journey screen). Every action, dismissal included, advances the machine; nothing gates the map. The overlay lives in the journey's Modal idiom, is rendered only when `!showroom`, and reuses the shipped `Confetti` (itself reduced-motion aware — it renders nothing under reduce motion, and the copy carries the celebration).

**Two DELIBERATE divergences from web, both owner-ruled.** (1) Beat 2 on web is the capstone conversation offer, gated on `scenarioId && !hasStamp`. Mobile has no scenario plumbing at all — no scenario map, `(tabs)/chat.tsx` takes no params, `useListZoneStamps` is unused there — so mobile beat 2 is the Chai payoff instead, and the capstone offer is left to the Zone 2-6 capstone work. Web's "silent done" branch (nothing to offer in beat 2) therefore has no mobile counterpart: the payoff beat always has content. (2) The free-rider game: web sends free riders to Express Listening, which has no mobile route (descoped from this platform's roster), so mobile sends them to Ticket Check pinned to the zone (`cat=<zoneId>&ctx=closeout`, same `listen-and-pick` server model Express Listening rides). Plus riders get `speed-round?ctx=closeout`, exactly as web. Plus fails closed (`isPlus === true && !isLoading`), so an unresolved entitlement gets the free game, never a locked one. The arrival fact is omitted on mobile — no `india-facts` port.

**Stage memory (`lib/closeoutMemory.ts`).** Web reads localStorage synchronously at render; that does not port, so this mirrors `lib/signalMemory.ts`: AsyncStorage hydrated ONCE into an in-memory cache that render reads through synchronous accessors, hydration MERGES rather than replaces, and nothing reads disk at render time. Key `bolo.zoneCloseout:${lang}`, value a zoneIndex → `"beat2" | "done"` map, revalidated on read (integer keys, the two literals) exactly like web. Writes persist read-modify-write, never blind — a stage written before hydration finished would otherwise persist only what the session knows and wipe zones already closed out on disk, the one way this display state could resurrect a celebration.

**First-sight seeding.** `unseeded` means this device has never written the key. On first sight the overlay writes every currently-`allDone` zone straight to `"done"` and returns without celebrating, so existing learners get no retroactive burst for zones they finished months ago. Unreadable storage reads as a missing key on purpose: seeding silently is the safe failure, celebrating everything is not. Per-zone completeness is derived on the map as `zoneAllDone` (stations non-empty AND every station `completed`/`tested_out`) — an unloaded zone is never "done".

**Suppression, both directions.** Direction one (web parity): the trackside soft stop holds while a celebration is owed, via `closeoutOwed(memory, zonesAllDone)` — extracted as a pure predicate so the rule is testable, and false before hydration, true while unseeded. Direction two is an ADDITION over web, which has no such guard: the celebration itself waits while the lock or signal dialog owns the screen (`blocked` prop). A third guard has no web counterpart either — web navigates away from the map to the closeout game, but a stack keeps the map mounted under it and a Modal is app-wide, so launching the game sets an `awaitingReturn` flag cleared by `useFocusEffect`; without it the payoff beat would re-open instantly on top of the game.

**The payoff claim is grant-only.** The stage machine is pure DISPLAY state (the ledger's once-ever refId is the real idempotency boundary), so "+2 Chai" is not derived from it. `QuickGameShell` records the server's receipt — `markCloseoutGranted(lang, categoryId, chaiGranted)`, session-scoped and in-memory, inside the existing `chai > 0` branch next to the signal mark — and the payoff beat renders the chip only for that zone id and only for the AMOUNT the server reported. A repeat run, a failed run, or a different category picked in the hub makes no claim. Session scope is deliberate: the receipt describes a run, not a device.

**Tests.** `artifacts/bolo-mobile/__tests__/journey-closeout.test.tsx` (21): stage-memory unit coverage (seeded vs unseeded, malformed values discarded, in-flight write survives hydration, seeding persists), the `closeoutOwed` truth table, first-sight seeding proving a pre-completed zone does NOT celebrate while a later completion does, both beats in order through a real launch-and-return, skip and "maybe later" paths, Plus vs free CTA (including Plus failing closed while loading), the grant-only chip (wrong zone and zero grant claim nothing), and both suppression directions. The Chai glyph census in `__tests__/chai-stall.test.tsx` gained the payoff chip: mobile total is now 7 (was 6), web unchanged at 7.

**Suite state:** mobile 85 suites / 718 tests pass (was 84 / 697). Typecheck clean across all 7 projects.

## 10g. Build 36 tiny-UI batch: Bolo plays the evaluating state, bare wallet glyph, square Word Match cards (August 6, 2026)

**Evaluating state — the throbber is retired on BOTH platforms.** Mobile ran an `ActivityIndicator` inside the 88x88 record button (visibly off-centre on the iOS build, with no offset anywhere in the style code — an `size="large"` intrinsic-frame quirk, moot now); web floated a `Loader2` spinner over the mascot's belly (`absolute bottom-[18%]`) and dimmed the mascot to 0.55 behind it. Both are gone, along with the dim, which only existed so the spinner could read. Bolo plays the state himself: he hangs upside down and swings, driven by the EXISTING pose/motion system on each platform, not a parallel one. Mobile adds `motion="flip"` to `MascotMotion` (`components/Mascot.tsx`), web adds `activity="evaluating"` to `MascotActivity` (`src/components/mascot.tsx`). Whole-image rotation only, so the canonical art rule holds; there is no perch in the canonical art, so he hangs in place rather than off a drawn branch. The record button keeps its mic icon at 0.45 opacity — the circle never empties or shifts under a holding finger (frame-stability contract).

**The 180 deg is a STATIC transform, never an animated value, on both platforms.** Mobile puts it in a plain `StyleSheet` style on an inner view (`testID="mascot-hanging"`); the shared value carries only the spring INTO the pose (`(flipIn - 1) * 180` cancels the static rotation until the spring lands) plus a +/-7 deg pendulum riding the idle loop. Web puts it in a `rotate-180` class on an in-flow wrapper with a CSS transform transition; framer only adds the pendulum. That split is what makes the pose survive with animations off — and it keeps the wrapper IN FLOW, because the practice `fill` chain has no definite ancestor height (an absolute box collapses the whole parrot zone).

**Reduced motion keeps a working state, not a frozen bird.** Reanimated 3 collapses `withSpring`/`withTiming` to their end value under the system switch, and the web reduce-motion CSS reset kills the transition, so on both platforms the bird simply IS upside down — no tip-over, no swing. A slow opacity breathe carries the "still working" beat instead (no movement): mobile via `withTiming(..., { reduceMotion: ReduceMotion.Never })` — it must opt out explicitly or reanimated snaps it to the end value and leaves the mascot permanently dimmed — and web via a framer opacity keyframe loop, which the CSS reset does not touch.

**Superseded August 9, 2026 (owner decision): the hang is now a zoom-out-and-spin.** Bolo no longer tips upside down while evaluating — he zooms out small (0.45) and spins in place, then zooms back in when the result lands. Everything above about WHERE the state lives still holds; only the transform changed. The state name changed with it: mobile `motion="working"` (was `"flip"`), web still `activity="evaluating"`, and the wrapper test id is `mascot-working` on both (was `mascot-hanging`). The static-transform split is unchanged in spirit: the SHRINK is the plain style (mobile `StyleSheet` scale, web a `scale-[0.45]` class on the same in-flow wrapper), and only the delta is animated. Web gets both halves for free — the wrapper's existing `transition-transform` turns adding and dropping the class into the zoom, and framer contributes a linear `rotate: 360` loop (1.4s; easing a full spin reads as a stutter). Mobile has no CSS transition, so a `zoom` shared value multiplies the plain scale: it springs from `1 / 0.45` down to 1 on entry (starting full size, cancelling the shrink) and from 0.45 up to 1 on exit, set through a `withSequence(withTiming(_, {duration: 0}), withSpring(1))` so the spring is guaranteed to start from the intended value rather than racing two writes. The spin is a separate shared value that unwinds to 0 over 220ms on exit, so he faces front as he grows back. Reduced motion is unchanged in shape: no zoom, no spin, the plain shrink holds and the opacity breathe carries the beat.

**Chai wallet balance badge (mobile).** The 52pt disc filled with `colors.primary` behind the terracotta kulhad is DROPPED, not warmed. It was the only plated Chai glyph anywhere: the home stat cell, the stall band, the spend buttons, the practice receipt, the journey signal/closeout payouts and every web surface all render `ChaiGlyph` bare. The glyph grows 26 -> 40 so the header keeps its anchor. `ChaiGlyph` now takes an optional `testID` (default `chai-glyph`) so individual sites can be pinned; it stays accessibility-hidden, which means RNTL queries for it need `includeHiddenElements`.

**Word Match card shape (mobile in-game board, NOT the hub).** The hub tiles were the reported suspect but measured out near-square already (~174x171 at 390pt); the slender surface was the in-game board, where `cardHeight` was the flex:1 grid's full height divided by the row count — ~84x134 at four rows and ~1:2.2 at three. Cards are now capped at square (`min(rowHeight, cardWidth)`) and `styles.grid` gained `alignContent: 'center'`, so leftover board height sits AROUND the rows instead of stretching them. Shape only: no hub restructuring, no layout rework, and the `TAB_BAR_CLEARANCE` contract is untouched.

**Test-harness note.** The hand-rolled reanimated mock in `bolo-mobile/jest-setup.js` had no `ReduceMotion` export, so any animation config carrying a reduce-motion policy threw on render. The enum is stubbed there now.

**Tests.** `bolo-mobile/__tests__/practice-evaluating-bolo.test.tsx` (5, new): the hang appears while evaluation is in flight, NO `ActivityIndicator` renders anywhere in that phase, the hang clears on the result, and `Mascot` hangs on `motion="flip"` only — including with reduced motion forced on, where the static transform is asserted directly. `gujarati-coach/src/test/mascot-evaluating.test.tsx` (3, new) mirrors it for web, and `practice-silent-mode-prefetch.test.tsx` gained a page-level pin (hanging mascot present, no `.animate-spin`) at the real evaluating moment. `chai-wallet.test.tsx` pins the bare 40pt glyph with no background or radius on its row; `word-match-clearance.test.tsx` pins card height == width and the centred grid.

**Suite state:** mobile 86 suites / 736 tests pass (was 85 / 728); web 71 files / 555 tests pass (was 70 / 551). Typecheck clean across all 7 projects.

## 10h. SignalGlyph redraw: a full level crossing, still one geometry (August 6, 2026)

**What it is.** The trackside crossing glyph gained the detail the reference art called for — a warning bell atop the mast, a proper crossbuck, a hooded lamp head, a striped arm with its own gate lamps, a bolted mechanism housing, and a concrete base with a footing and ground shadow — without changing the state model, the art form, or the rendered box (still `width=40 height=50 viewBox="0 0 32 40"`, so seating and hit targets are untouched). Flat brand-palette shapes only, no gradients, no raster, whole-glyph motion only: the same Inline-SVG character pattern TrainEngine and SignalmanGlyph follow. No new palette entries — the concrete base reuses `SLATE`, and the bell reuses `AMBER`/`AMBER_SHADE`, both already in the scenery module.

**The state model is untouched and still binding.** ONE drawing; `upcoming` and `active` are bar DOWN + red (active adds the halo), `waved` is bar UP + amber, `cleared` is bar UP + green. A state change remains a transform (`rotate(75 16.4 22.4)` about the pivot) and a fill, never a different picture. The arm — stripes, lamps, outline — is declared once and rendered either plain or inside the rotated group. **The gate lamps on the arm are deliberately fixed red in all four states:** a second colour-changing light would give the learner two things to read, and the mast lamp is the one that carries the state. The arm semantics stay ours (down while the crossing still owes you something), which is INVERTED from real-world crossings and from the reference art; the owner ruled explicitly against flipping it.

**Web/mobile parity.** Same coordinates, same palette consts, same draw order, same testids in both files — `gujarati-coach/src/components/journey-scenery.tsx` (`SignalGlyph`) and `bolo-mobile/components/journey/SignalGlyph.tsx` — differing only in renderer idiom (`transform="rotate(a cx cy)"` vs `rotation`/`originX`/`originY`, `<g>` vs `<G>`). Parity was verified mechanically, not by eye: a throwaway script extracted every geometry and paint attribute in document order from both sources, folded the two rotation idioms into one form, and diffed the sequences — 185 tokens each, zero differences. Re-run that check after any edit; hand-porting one file and not the other is the failure mode this drawing invites.

**Testids are now symmetric across platforms** (`signal-glyph`, `signal-lamp`, `signal-active-halo`, `signal-arm-down`/`signal-arm-up`, plus the new `signal-bell`, `signal-crossbuck`, `signal-housing`, `signal-base`, `signal-arm-lamps`). Web had none before; the four-state truth table was only pinned on mobile.

**Tests.** Both `journey-signals` suites now carry the same two glyph tests: the four-state table (arm testid, lamp fill, halo only on active) and a detail test asserting every new part is present in ALL four states — which is what stops the detail from quietly becoming a fifth state signal. Web additionally pins the raised arm's rotate transform, so a future edit cannot express "up" as a different shape.

**Suite state:** mobile 86 suites / 737 tests pass (was 86 / 736); web 71 files / 557 tests pass (was 71 / 555). Typecheck clean across all 7 projects.

## 10i. Games hub square tile is breakpoint-gated; Word Match has a real card back (August 6, 2026, web)

**The square was never conditional, the grid always was.** The hub redesign gave the grid card `aspect-square` unconditionally while the shelf grid only goes two-up at `min-[480px]`. On a 390pt iPhone the card is therefore full width, and a full-width square measured **358×358px** — most of the viewport, with `justify-between` stranding the icon at the top and the title and chip at the bottom of a mostly empty box. The square is now gated behind the SAME `min-[480px]` breakpoint as the grid, with a `min-h-[168px]` floor below it (and `min-[480px]:min-h-0` handing the box back to aspect-ratio above it). Measured after: **169.8–191px** at 390pt, **238×238 unchanged** at 520pt. Rule of thumb: an aspect ratio on a grid child is only meaningful alongside the column count that gives the child its width — gate them together or not at all.

**The featured card is not affected and never was.** `FeaturedCard` is a flex row, measured **162px at 390pt both before and after** the fix. If a hub screenshot looks top-heavy, the hero is not the cause.

**Word Match's face-down card** renders `WordMatchCardBack` (exported from the web game module) — the canonical waving Bolo on a tinted face, the same art the mobile game has always dealt. It previously drew a lucide `Link2` chain-link glyph at 40% opacity, which every viewer read as a broken image. It is ONE exported component precisely because the platforms have to be kept in step by hand, and its contents are pinned by test (mascot PNG present, zero `<svg>` on the back) so a placeholder cannot creep back.

**Verification note that matters more than the fix.** Both defects were invisible to the suites and always would have been: jsdom has no layout engine and never compiles Tailwind, so `aspect-ratio` never resolves and every element measures 0×0 — and no test asserted card shape at all, not even as a class string. `/games` is auth-gated, so the `qa/*.mjs` browser probes had never been pointed at it. Anything geometric on the games routes must be checked in a real browser with a Clerk sign-in ticket at a named viewport width; a class assertion is not an acceptance check for layout.

## 10j. Romanized reading is mandatory on game surfaces, with three named exemptions (August 6, 2026, web + mobile)

**The ruling.** Any game surface rendering native script to a learner during play must ALSO render its romanized form, always visible — not on reveal, not gated. It softens the script-recognition challenge in games like Word Match and Luggage Match, and that was accepted deliberately: a learner who cannot yet read Devanagari or Gujarati is otherwise guessing at shapes.

**Source is `phrase.romanized`, always.** It is a required string on the API phrase schema and already rides in every payload these games fetch — no new field, no new request, and NO transliteration helper ships to a client. The deterministic sanscript romanizer lives server-side and exists for free-form STT transcripts (the practice "We heard" line), not for catalog phrases. Per-word romanization does not exist anywhere in the schema or seed data.

**Empty string is a real case.** Several scripts have no romanization and their phrases carry `""`. Every surface guards with `romanized.trim() !== ''` and renders the script alone — never an empty line, never a placeholder.

**Treatment (identical on both platforms):** native script primary, romanized directly beneath it, quieter and smaller — the two-line stack mobile Word Match and the Speed Round card already used. Web: `text-xs font-medium text-muted-foreground` on cards and tags, `text-sm text-muted-foreground` on a full-size prompt. Mobile: `AppFonts.regular` at 11px on cards/tags and 13px on a prompt, 0.75–0.85 opacity. On Wrong Platform the reading sits between the script and the English meaning.

**Three exemptions, ruled by the owner, that must not be "fixed" later.**
- **Ticket Check** — its mechanic IS romanization → script: the prompt ticket shows the reading and the learner picks the matching script. Romanizing the choices reduces it to matching a Latin string to an identical Latin string, which is finished, not easier. Its reveal-only choice romanization stays.
- **Speed Round hard mode** — hiding the reading is the entire difference between normal and hard, and normal shows it. Opt-in challenge, not a trap.
- **Phrase Builder tiles** — per-word romanization exists nowhere, and the prompt already shows the full romanized phrase above the tiles.

**Test trap this created.** Choice buttons now contain two text nodes, so any test identifying a choice by the button's whole `textContent` breaks (Express Listening's helper did). Identify a choice by its FIRST line — the native span — not by the button's concatenated text.

## 10k. Chai buys ONE stop at a time, first zone only, in a language you have not bought (August 6, 2026, api + web + mobile)

**The ruling.** A Free learner may spend Chai to open an individual stop in a plan-locked language. The cap is the FIRST ZONE only — the zone that already hosts the free-taste stop — at 50 Chai per stop, once ever. It is not a new paywall boundary: it deepens the existing free-taste carve-out. Everything past that zone stays All-Access territory and is not for sale at any price.

**The ledger row IS the unlock.** `unlockStop` (`lib/tokenService.ts`) writes one `spend_stop_unlock` row whose refId is `stop:<languageCode>:<lessonGroupId>` (minted server-side in `lib/stopUnlock.ts`, never accepted from a client). Ownership is derived by reading that row back, so there is no unlock table, no device state, and a reinstall changes nothing. The ledger's unique `(user, reason, ref)` index makes a replay a no-op that returns `charged: false` and charges nothing.

**Two different stops are two different rows**, so the unique index cannot stop them from spending the same Chai. The money path takes a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the user's token-state row BEFORE reading the balance it spends against; the loser re-reads the debited balance and is refused. Any future sink that is not deduplicated by its refId needs the same lock.

**The cap is enforced by the server, not the UI.** `checkStopUnlockEligibility` accepts a stop only when it sits in the same category as the language's free first stop, is not that stop itself, and has at least one phrase-stage, non-premium row to serve. `denyLockedLanguage` then admits a bought stop wherever the caller identifies the requested resource (`firstStopGroupId` / `firstStopPhraseId`), which covers phrases, TTS, pronunciation and attempts without a per-route allowlist.

**Refusal registers are split on purpose.** Out-of-zone is a 402 `upgrade_required` through `sendLockedLanguageDenial` (it is an All-Access boundary, not a failed spend); an empty tin is the wallet's existing 409 `insufficient_tokens` with `{balance, cost}`; unsellable stops are 409 `stop_already_free` / `stop_not_unlockable`.

**Offer and sale share one predicate.** The journey listing marks `chaiUnlockable` with the same three filters the purchase route applies, and carries the price as `stopUnlock.cost` on first-zone showroom payloads only — clients never hardcode 50. A bought stop comes back `status: "unlocked"` + `chaiUnlocked: true` and serves through the same premium-filtered branch as the free stop, so a purchase can never surface Plus-library rows.

**Test trap.** The showroom envelope and per-group key sets are pinned byte-for-byte in `learning.lesson-groups-showroom.test.ts`, and the web Chai glyph census in `chai-stall.test.tsx` counts `<ChaiGlyph` per file; both must be updated whenever a Chai surface or listing field is added.

## 10l. Bolo's outfits: a permanent Chai sink worn on every mascot surface (August 7, 2026, api + web + mobile)

**The ruling.** A garment costs 25 Chai (`OUTFIT_COST`, `lib/tokenEconomy.ts`) — since August 8, 2026 that is a per-item price on the catalog row rather than the only price in the shop (see 10r) — is bought once and owned forever, and is permanent — not seasonal, never expiring, never re-charged. The first one is Navratri. Wearing and taking off are free; only the first purchase moves money.

**Two pieces of state, deliberately different.** Ownership is a ledger row — one `spend_outfit` row whose refId is `outfit:<id>`, minted server-side in `lib/outfits.ts` and never accepted from a client — so a reinstall or a fresh device changes nothing. What Bolo is WEARING is `user_token_state.equipped_outfit` (nullable, migration `0041_ordinary_daredevil.sql`), served on `GET /tokens` as `equippedOutfit` beside `stationPausesEquipped`. Unequipping clears the column and never touches the ledger, so it is not a refund and not a loss.

**Why its own routes.** `POST /tokens/spend` mints a `Date.now()` refId when the client omits one, so it cannot dedupe a replay. Outfits get `GET /outfits`, `POST /outfits/buy` and `POST /outfits/equip` (`routes/outfits.ts`, authed, not plan-gated), where the refId is derived from the outfit id. `buyOutfit` takes `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the token-state row FIRST and decides everything — ownership, balance, debit — against the view that lock serialises, then inserts the ledger row `onConflictDoNothing` and equips. A replay returns `charged: false`, charges nothing, and leaves the equipped choice alone. **Ownership must not be read before the lock**: a second request for the same outfit that read "not owned" and then queued on the lock wakes to the winner's debited tin and 409s for a costume the learner already owns (`outfits.test.ts` pins this with a held-lock witness).

**The shop is Bolo Bazaar (renamed and themed August 7, 2026).** The surface formerly called "Bolo's wardrobe" is a roadside cloth shop on both platforms: a striped awning with a scalloped hem, a marigold toran, a hand-painted enamel signboard reading Bolo Bazaar, and a timber counter the bird stands behind, with the preview mascot inside the storefront rather than in a separate card. Its palette is the FIXED `INDIA` palette (web `src/lib/india-palette.ts`, mobile `constants/india.ts`), NOT design tokens, for the same reason the chai stall's art is fixed: it is a painted scene and must read identically in light and dark mode. The awning and toran themselves are shared dressing (web `src/components/india-decor.tsx`, mobile `components/IndiaDecor.tsx`) so the bazaar and the wallet paint the same stall. Everything below the storefront (buy, wear, take off, rack cards) stays on the design system so contrast and dark mode keep working. The mobile nav row dropped its title text because the signboard carries the name; web keeps the `h1` inside the board. Decorative rows are `aria-hidden` on web and `accessibilityElementsHidden` on mobile. Entry copy in both Chai wallets now reads "Bolo Bazaar"; `data-testid`/`testID` values are unchanged (`outfit-shop`, `outfit-preview`, `outfit-balance`, `outfit-card-<id>`, plus the new `outfit-storefront`), and the ChaiGlyph count per screen stays at three so the chai-stall glyph-inventory tests hold.

**The Chai wallet is painted too (August 7, 2026, web + mobile).** The sheet was three white cards; every row now carries a 64px illustrated tile (web `src/components/wallet-art.tsx`, mobile `components/WalletArt.tsx`) plus a soft wash of that tile's colour bleeding across the row. Station Pause is the CANONICAL engine parked at a timber platform under a signal, bobbing; Bolo Bazaar is a stall front with the learner's OWN mascot (canonical `Mascot`, so it shows the outfit he is wearing) under the awning; Express Multiplier is the same engine driving on indigo behind speed streaks and a marigold 2× flag, switching from bob to drive only while a multiplier is actually running. The balance header was a bazaar-wall band under a toran; it is now struck across the stall photograph itself (see the next paragraph), keeping the `wallet-balance-band` id. The tiles are painted scenes, so they PIN the engine's ironwork colours instead of reading theme tokens: web sets `--color-foreground`/`--color-primary`/`--color-secondary`/`--color-card-border` on the tile wrapper, and mobile's `TrainEngine` gained an optional `palette` prop for the same purpose (omit it and it keeps reading theme tokens, which is what the ticket and rail markers want). Motion is the components' own reduced-motion-aware loops — no layout props are animated on mobile (Expo Go New Arch crash rule) — and every tile is decorative: `aria-hidden` on web, `accessibilityElementsHidden` on mobile. No testID/`data-testid` changed.

**The wallet opens ON the stall (August 7, 2026, web + mobile).** The sheet's first 132px are the painted chai-stall scene (web `public/stall/wallet-header.jpg`, mobile `assets/images/stall/wallet-header.jpg` — one flattened still cropped from the owner's artwork, NOT the layered composite the home band assembles), with a three-stop dark scrim over it and the sheet title plus the balance (`wallet-balance-band`, ChaiGlyph + count + "Chai") set in white on top. Web reaches it by dropping `SheetContent`'s padding to `p-0` and padding the row list itself; the Radix close button is forced white through `[&>button]:text-white` because it now sits on the art. Mobile does the same with `styles.header` + `styles.body` and `overflow: 'hidden'` on the sheet. The art is decorative on both platforms. Spend buttons left indigo for **bazaar green** (`INDIA.board` with `INDIA.boardDeep` as the pressed-into-it bottom edge, cream lettering): the kulhad glyph is terracotta and muddied against `--primary`. Bolo Bazaar is now reached by a real button like the other two rows rather than a worded link inside a wrapping `<Link>` — web navigates with wouter's `useLocation`, mobile closes the sheet then `router.push('/(app)/outfits')`, and `wallet-open-wardrobe` moved onto the button. Row copy fixed: **Bolo is female**, so "Buy once, his for good" is now "hers for good" on both platforms. A fourth row, FREE-TIER ONLY (`wallet-language-row`, hidden once entitlements say paid), pairs a new signpost tile (`LanguagesTile`: two enamel name-boards, one padlocked) with a `wallet-language-info` button that explains in place that Chai unlocks non-Hindi stops — web uses the house `Dialog`, mobile uses an overlay INSIDE the existing modal rather than stacking a second native `Modal`. Mobile's `ChaiWalletSheet` therefore reads `useEntitlements()` now, which throws outside its provider, so its test mocks the context (and `expo-router`, since the Browse button routes). The web wallet gained its first test file (`src/test/chai-wallet.test.tsx`); the web glyph census in `chai-stall.test.tsx` moves to 3 for the wallet (13 total) because the balance readout is a ChaiGlyph again. **The spend buttons are gradient enamel with a lit coin (August 7, 2026).** Flat board green was the wrong ground for the kulhad: terracotta on mid-green is two warm mid-tones side by side and the cup dissolved into the button. The face is now a vertical gradient (`#1E7357` -> `INDIA.board` -> `#103F31`) with an inset top highlight over the same deep-green pressed edge — RN cannot gradient a background colour, so mobile fills each button with an absolutely-positioned `SpendFace` `LinearGradient` and the button clips it (`overflow: 'hidden'`). The glyph sits in a shared `ChaiCoin`: a 24px cream disc with a marigold rim and halo (CSS radial gradient + box-shadow on web, `INDIA.gold` border plus shadow/elevation on mobile). Because both spend buttons now render ONE shared coin component, the Chai glyph census drops to 2 per wallet file (web total 12, mobile 10) — the count is of render sites in source, so factoring a glyph into a local component is expected to move it.

**The home stats figures are one door to progress (August 7, 2026, web + mobile).** Everything left of the Chai cell in the blue stats banner — Day Streak, Total XP, Mastered on BOTH platforms since Task #1057 removed web's Speaking Streak cell (August 12, 2026) — is wrapped in a single target (`stats-progress-link`: a wouter `Link href="/progress"` on web, a `Pressable` pushing `/(app)/(tabs)/progress` on mobile) instead of being inert numbers beside one tappable cell. The Chai cell stays OUTSIDE that wrapper: it opens the wallet, and it is a real `button`, which may not nest inside a link. The individual `StatCell`s are structurally unchanged (still plain divs), so the existing "static cells stay plain divs" pins hold.

**Refusals are 409, never 402.** An outfit is a Chai sink, not a plan boundary: an empty tin is `insufficient_tokens` with `{balance, cost}`, wearing something unowned is `outfit_not_owned`, and an unknown id is a 404. Nothing here is ever an upgrade prompt.

**Art resolution is pose + outfit, in one place.** Web `lib/mascot-outfits.ts` (`mascotAssetSrc`) and mobile `lib/mascotOutfits.ts` (`mascotSource`) own the mapping; both mascot renderers (`components/mascot.tsx`, and on mobile BOTH `components/Mascot.tsx` and `components/TalkingMascot.tsx`) call them, so all ~22 web surfaces and their mobile twins inherit the costume without knowing outfits exist. A pose an outfit does not ship falls back to canonical Bolo — a missing asset must never blank the bird. Mobile paths are literal `require()` strings because Metro resolves assets at build time. The CANONICAL ART RULE holds: an outfit is an owner-supplied alternate set of the SAME five whole-image poses, not new artwork.

**The equipped value is read from context, not fetched per mascot.** `EquippedOutfitProvider` (web `hooks/use-equipped-outfit.tsx`, mobile `contexts/OutfitContext.tsx`) rides the existing `GET /tokens` query and defaults to null outside the provider, so signed-out chrome and unit tests render canonical Bolo. Mutations must invalidate BOTH the outfits and tokens query keys, or the shop updates and the rest of the app stays undressed.

**The shop previews before it charges.** `/outfits` (web `pages/outfits.tsx`, mobile `app/(app)/outfits.tsx`, linked from the Chai wallet sheet on both platforms) shows the costume on the learner's OWN Bolo via the `outfit` prop override — the big preview is the bird herself, never a thumbnail (the rack's small per-item pictures came later, 10r) — with a back-out that restores what they actually wear. Prices, ownership and the equipped choice all come from the server; no client hardcodes 25.

**Test trap.** Both Chai glyph censuses (`chai-stall.test.tsx`, web and mobile) count `<ChaiGlyph` per file and must list the new shop. On web, the mascot CROSSFADES: mid-transition both the outgoing and incoming `<img>` are mounted, so a preview assertion must read the last image, not the first.

## 10m. The games hub is a bazaar wall of painted boards (August 7, 2026, web + mobile)

**Pastel tints are gone; each tile is a saturated enamel signboard.** The hub's ten games were `bg-<hue>-50/70` washes on the theme card — colourful in name only, and mostly white space. Every tile now carries a two-stop gradient in a saturated Indian hue (peacock, parrot green, turmeric, kumkum, jamun, terracotta, rani pink, deep teal, express indigo, signal green), a deeper edge of the same hue as its border, and a hard `0 5px 0` shadow in that deeper hue so the board sits on the page. Mobile expresses the same shadow as `borderBottomWidth: 5` (RN has no hard-offset shadow) and paints the face with `LinearGradient` inside an `overflow: 'hidden'` card. The page ground is a warm bazaar wall (`#FDF2DF`, dark mode keeps `--background`) and the shelf headings are terracotta with a gold tick.

**Colour lives on the tile, so tokens do not.** Text inside a board is white / white-85 and the badges sit on a scrim — the design-system foreground tokens are deliberately unused INSIDE a tile because they vanish on these backgrounds, the same rule the chai stall and Bolo Bazaar already follow. Everything outside the boards (page chrome, headings, nav) stays on the design system.

**Vignette ink is pinned, not inherited.** The preview vignettes are authored against the LIGHT theme and now sit on a cream medallion (`#FFF8EC`) inside a coloured board. Web pins `--primary`/`--secondary`/`--accent`/`--muted-foreground` on the medallion wrapper; mobile's `GamePreview` gained an optional `ink` prop (`VignetteInk`) for the same purpose — omit it and it reads theme tokens, which is what any on-card usage wants. This is the same precedent as `TrainEngine`'s `palette` prop.

**Icons scale with the tile; badges are stickers.** The medallion is 68px on a phone-width card, 80px at the two-column breakpoint and 112px at `lg` (where the square is ~306px and a small stamp read as an empty field); the fallback glyph and title scale with it. Padding dropped to `p-3` with `gap-2` and a `mt-auto` footer row. `AccessBadge` is now a solid enamel sticker — gold gradient with dark ink for All-Access, green for Free, both with a white ring and a hard shadow — and difficulty is a white uppercase label with a coloured dot on a `bg-black/25` scrim, because a tinted-text pill is invisible on a saturated board. The lock chip is a black scrim chip (mobile: a round black chip holding the lock glyph).

**A latent inline-link bug surfaced with the taller hero.** Both hub `Link`s wrap a block card, and wouter renders a bare `<a>` — inline. In the grid the `<a>` is a grid item and gets blockified, but the featured slot's link sits in normal flow, so it contributed only a line box and the catalog rode up over the hero (the "Listening" heading landing on the Luggage Match card). Both wrappers now carry `className="block"`. As with the 10i square, jsdom could never see this: it was found and confirmed fixed with a real-browser probe at 430px and 1280px measuring heading-vs-card rects.

**No decorative bloom.** A translucent corner circle was tried on each board and removed: on a square tile it clips through the badge row rather than sitting behind it.

## 10n. Bolo Bazaar: a curtained changing room, two doors per bolt of cloth, and a bird who is a she (August 7, 2026, web + mobile)

**Bolo is female. The shop copy said otherwise.** "Buy it once and it stays his" is now "stays hers", the shortfall line reads "N more Chai and she can wear it", and the back-out is "show my Bolo as she is". The resting caption under the bird ("Bolo, as he comes") is DELETED rather than reworded — with nothing being tried on there is nothing to name, and the line was the only thing gendering an empty stage.

**The awning is one cloth, masked — not a band plus a row of tabs.** The old hem was an independent flex row of `rounded-b-full` blocks under the striped band, so the two grids drifted apart and the hem read as loose red squares floating under the awning. Web now paints ONE striped div and carves the scallops out of it with a two-layer CSS mask (a solid rect for the body, a `radial-gradient` circle tiled at the stripe width for the hem); mobile builds fixed-width cells, each a stripe with its own half-round below it, in a row that clips the overflow. Both keep scallop diameter == stripe width, which is what makes every scallop sit centred on its own stripe. `Awning` no longer takes `scallops`/`hemHeight` — the hem is derived from `stripeWidth`.

**The changing room is a booth, and the curtains never leave it.** Swapping mascot art in place read as a glitch, so the preview sits inside a `DressingRoom` (`components/dressing-room.tsx`, `components/DressingRoom.tsx`): timber posts and floor, a plaster back wall under a warm ceiling glow, and a scalloped velvet pelmet — masked out of one cloth on web, one half-round cell per unit on mobile, the same construction as the awning because a separate row of tabs never lines up. OPEN MEANS TIED BACK, not slid off-stage: the panels are 52% wide and park at 13% behind the posts with a gold tie, so cloth is on screen in both states. An empty frame stopped reading as a booth and made the change look like two red rectangles flying in from nowhere. Panels are tucked UNDER the pelmet, or the bird's crest shows over the top of a shut curtain.

**The shut beat has a floor, and the open has a failsafe.** Bundled art resolves in the same frame, so gating the reveal purely on load made the curtain flash rather than change anything: the booth now stays shut for a minimum ~1.1s regardless, and opens on `max(load, minimum)`. `closed` is ALWAYS caller state, never an animation-end callback — web opens on load, on error, and on a 2.4s failsafe; mobile runs the flat beat because its art is bundled, not fetched. A curtain that can stick shut hides the product. Mobile animates `transform` only, through RN's own `Animated` on the native driver.

**Every bolt of cloth carries two doors.** The rack card used to be one big button whose whole-card tap silently previewed — safe, but nothing said so. Each card now renders **Try On** (marigold-outlined, previews only; reads "On the bird" while shown) and either **Buy Now · N** (bazaar-green enamel, disabled and reading "N more" when the tin is short) or **Wear this** / **Take it off** for something already owned. `outfit-card-<id>` survives as the container and still previews on click, so existing tests and the convenience tap both hold; the new buttons are `outfit-tryon-<id>`, `outfit-buynow-<id>`, `outfit-wear-<id>` and `outfit-takeoff-<id>`. Buy Now previews the outfit as it charges, so the curtain opens on what was just bought. The storefront's single action bar above the rack is unchanged.

## 10o. Ticket Check: the reading belongs to the answers, not the question (August 7, 2026, web + mobile)

The prompt ticket used to print the English meaning AND the romanized reading, while the answer tiles showed bare native script and only revealed their readings after the pick. That is backwards: the prompt handed over the pronunciation the learner is there to recognise, and the tiles withheld the script-to-sound pairing that is the actual lesson.

**Now:** the ticket being checked shows the meaning alone. Every answer tile shows its romanized reading under its native script from the first look. The post-answer reveal still adds the English meaning to the correct tile only, and the empty-romanization guard is unchanged — languages with no romanization render no empty slot, on both platforms. Mobile's prompt card gained `ticket-prompt` so a test can assert the absence of a reading on the question without tripping over the readings now living on the tiles.

## 10p. Streak repair: the one Chai sink that buys protection, and it writes no number (August 7, 2026, api + web + mobile)

**The ruling.** Streak repair is the ratified exception to the delight-only Chai spine. It costs 25 Chai (`STREAK_REPAIR_COST`, `lib/tokenEconomy.ts`, moved out of the unwired Slice 2 block) and buys back a streak lost to life happening — never an advantage, never speed, never content.

**A repair writes no streak.** There is no stored streak anywhere in this codebase: `currentStreakDays` is derived on every read from the days that have rows in `attempts`, bucketed into local days by `users.timezone` (`lib/progressMetrics.ts`). A station pause already "covers" a missed day so the ladder climbs through it, and a repair is that same cover bought AFTER the fact. So the repair inserts one ledger row and lets the ladder re-derive: every other day in the restored run is still real practice, and it is structurally impossible to buy a streak that never happened, because there is no field to write it into.

**One ladder, one accessor.** The ladder itself was extracted to `streakFromDayKeys(dayKeys, coveredDayKeys?, timeZone?, now?)` and `computeStreakDays` now delegates to it, so the number the offer promises and the number the progress summary later shows cannot disagree. `listPausedDayKeys` was RENAMED to `listCoveredDayKeys` and returns pause days (bare-date refIds) ∪ repaired days (`streak:<date>`); the old name is deleted deliberately, because a surviving pauses-only accessor would silently un-repair a paid-for streak wherever it was called. Callers: `routes/learning.ts` (HOOK 6) and `lib/badgeAward.ts`.

**Eligibility, and why the window is two days** (`lib/streakRepair.ts`, pure and separately tested). Scanning back from yesterday, the first day with neither practice nor cover is the only candidate. It is repairable when the day BEFORE it is present (otherwise this is an absence, not a slip — `break_too_long`) and it lies within `STREAK_REPAIR_WINDOW_DAYS = 2` (`window_expired`); an unbroken run to the 60-day scan horizon is `no_break`. Two days is the number that makes AT MOST ONE break repairable at any moment, so repairs cannot be chained backwards through history — at three, an every-other-day learner could buy two in one sitting and walk a dead streak back to life. Eligibility is user-level across all languages, matching the pause cover it buys, which is deliberately generous for the same reason.

**Nothing about the purchase comes from the client.** `POST /tokens/repair-streak` takes NO BODY: the server re-derives which day is repairable and composes the ledger key itself (`streak:<YYYY-MM-DD>`, reason `spend_streak_repair`). This is why it is not `POST /tokens/spend`, which mints a `Date.now()` refId when the client omits one and therefore cannot dedupe a replay. `GET /tokens/streak-repair` serves the offer (`eligible`, `missedDay`, `restoresStreakDays`, `cost`, `balance`). No migration was needed — ledger `reason`/`ref_id` are plain text with a unique `(user, reason, ref)` index.

**Lock ordering, as with outfits.** `repairStreak` takes `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the token-state row FIRST and only then reads whether the day is already repaired. The inverse ordering makes the request that loses a race wake to the winner's debited tin and refuse a day the learner has already paid for; the correct ordering makes it a free `charged: false` replay. `routes/streakRepair.test.ts` pins this with a held-lock witness AND carries the retired ordering inline, so the guard cannot quietly stop discriminating.

**Refusals are 409, never 402.** `insufficient_tokens`, `no_break_to_repair`, `break_too_long`, `repair_window_expired`. A broken streak is not a plan boundary and must never become an upsell.

**The surface is the Chai wallet, on both platforms** (`wallet-streak-repair`, button `wallet-repair-streak`), because that is where every other Chai sink already lives and the balance is on screen. It is a CONDITIONAL row: shown only when the server says a repairable break exists, and otherwise absent entirely — no greyed-out "mend your streak", which on an unbroken streak is a small daily reproach. Copy is warm and never shaming ("Mend the line" / "Tuesday got away from you. Cover it and your 9-day streak rides on."), the price comes from the payload rather than a client constant, and the tile (`StreakMendTile`) is a gap in the rail with a marigold fishplate bolted across it — the missing sleeper stays missing. Settling invalidates tokens, the offer, and the progress summary, since the restored streak is derived server-side.

**Test trap.** Any mobile test that renders a screen able to open the wallet must mock `useGetStreakRepair`/`useRepairStreak` in its `@workspace/api-client-react` factory; the full-replacement mocks in the home and journey suites broke on this exact hook (see `.agents/memory/api-client-mock-factories.md`).

**Contextual offer on home tab (August 7, 2026, web + mobile).** `HomeStreakRepairBanner` (local component in `home.tsx` and `index.tsx`) sits between the stats banner and the main content. It renders only when `useGetStreakRepair` returns `eligible: true`, and is absent entirely otherwise — no greyed-out "mend your streak" on an unbroken streak. The button copy is "Mend · {cost}" (cost from payload). On settle it invalidates tokens, the offer, and the progress summary (`queryKey: ['/api/progress/summary']` prefix match). testIDs: `home-streak-repair-offer` (container) and `home-repair-streak` (button). Not dismissible: the 2-day eligibility window is its natural expiry. Mobile: repair notice auto-clears after 4 s via `useEffect`; web: inline notice replaces the copy row on settle.

## 10q. The games-hub access badge owns a row; it was never "clipped" (August 7, 2026, bolo-mobile only)

Reported as "not enough padding around the badges on iOS". The badge sat hard against the card's top-right corner with no breathing room, and on smaller iPhones it also overlapped the medallion.

**Absolute insets resolve against the PADDING BOX.** The badge carried `position:'absolute', top:0, right:0` with a comment claiming that landed it "inside the 12px card padding". That is wrong, and it is the whole bug. RN 0.81 runs Yoga 3, which is CSS-spec compliant: the containing block for an absolutely positioned child is the parent's padding box, so `top:0/right:0` puts it flush against the inside of the card's 2pt border. A parent's `padding` never insets an absolutely positioned child — only its in-flow siblings. The medallion and body text ARE inset by the 12pt padding, which is exactly why the badge looked misaligned by precisely that amount.

**The badge does not fit beside the medallion at any inset, so the inset was not the fix.** Measured from the bundled TTF rather than estimated: "ALL-ACCESS" at 10pt `Inter_800ExtraBold` with `letterSpacing:0.4` is 69.6pt of text, and the pill adds 20pt of chrome (2pt border + 8pt padding, both sides) plus 13pt for the star and gap — **~103pt**. A two-column card is `(screen - 32 - 10) / 2` = 174pt at 390pt wide, leaving a ~146pt content box of which the 64pt medallion claims the left 64. At `right:0` the badge already overlapped the medallion by 8.6pt at 390pt and 16.1pt at 375pt; the card's `overflow:'hidden'` disguised the collision as clipping. Any inset large enough to look padded pushes it further in — even a 10pt inset only clears the medallion on a 430pt screen, and by 1.4pt. Shortening the label is not available: "All-Access", never "Plus", is copy canon.

**Fix: `pillAbs` is deleted and the badge is an in-flow, right-aligned row (`pillRow`) placed above the medallion.** It now takes the card's full 12pt padding on all four sides at every width and cannot collide with anything, at the cost of ~30pt of card height (badge row + the card's 8pt gap). This also unblocks growing the medallion later, which a pinned badge would have fought. The Free badge (46.3pt, never collided) moves with it for consistency. Web is untouched — its cards are wide enough and `AccessBadge` is already in flow.

**Measuring bundled-font text offline.** `opentype.js` throws on Inter's GSUB (`substitutionType : 62 lookupType: 6 - substFormat: 2 is not yet supported`) from any shaping API (`getAdvanceWidth`, `forEachGlyph`). Bypass shaping: sum `font.charToGlyph(ch).advanceWidth / font.unitsPerEm * size` per character, add `getKerningValue` between pairs, add `letterSpacing * length`, then add the element's own border and padding (RN counts both inside an auto width). `ot.loadSync` returned undefined here; parse a Buffer with `ot.parse`.

**Debt:** none introduced. `games-hub-gate` and `games-hub-vignettes` pass unmodified (6 tests) — no test pins badge position, so nothing in the suite would have caught either the flush corner or the medallion overlap. Both are device-visual properties; the numbers above are the reference if that ever needs a regression pin.

## 10r. The rack sells accessories too, at their own price, shown as pictures (August 8, 2026, api + web + mobile)

**The shop is no longer one flat price.** An accessory is one thing added to the bird rather than a whole redress, and prices accordingly: `ACCESSORY_COST = 10` beside `OUTFIT_COST = 25` in `lib/tokenEconomy.ts`. The price travels on the catalog row (`lib/outfits.ts`) and `buyOutfit` reads it there itself via `outfitCost(outfitId)`. **Price is deliberately NOT a parameter of the money path**: a cost argument — even one defaulting to `OUTFIT_COST` — lets a caller pair an id with a price that disagrees with it, which for a 10-Chai accessory means silently charging 25. One id in, one price, one place. **A client never sends a price.** `outfits.test.ts` seeds the accessory buyer's tin BELOW `OUTFIT_COST` on purpose: if the flat price ever creeps back into the money path the purchase 409s instead of going through, so the witness cannot quietly stop discriminating. Lock ordering in `buyOutfit` is untouched.

**The catalog says what a thing is and how to picture it.** `OutfitCatalogItem` gained two required fields: `kind` (`garment` | `accessory`) and `preview` (`full` | `head`). The rack groups by `kind`, so a new kind of stock is a server entry, not a client release. An unrecognised `kind` (newer server, older app) falls into the FIRST section rather than through the floor — unknown stock stays shoppable. Empty sections print no heading.

**Thumbnails are the item worn, not new artwork.** `OutfitThumb` (`components/outfit-card.tsx`) renders `mascotAssetSrc("wave", id)` inside an aspect-square tile: accessories `scale(2.3)` about `53% 26%`, garments `scale(1.04)`. The wave master frames the bird identically for every item, so one origin serves all of them. This costs ZERO extra assets per item and makes a preview that disagrees with what the learner gets impossible. A pagdi in a full-body crop is a few unreadable pixels, which is why the catalog, not the client, decides the crop.

**Stocking an item is a catalog row plus five pose files** per client (`public/mascot/outfits/<id>/`, `assets/images/mascot/outfits/<id>/`). Nothing else: price, ownership, grouping and the thumbnail all derive from the row, and a pose a client has not shipped still falls back to canonical Bolo.

**The web rack is a grid now.** A vertical list of text-only cards read fine with one bolt of cloth and does not survive a growing catalog. Cards moved to `components/outfit-card.tsx` (a 2-col / 3-col grid, picture-first, ownership badge on the art) and keep every `data-testid` including both doors. That move carried two `<ChaiGlyph>` sites out of `pages/outfits.tsx`; the glyph census in `chai-stall.test.tsx` enumerates FILES, so it had to learn the new one — the web total is unchanged at 15.

**The first accessory is the Marigold pagdi** (10 Chai), composited over untouched canonical PNGs at per-pose anchors and measured per-pose rotations. The CANONICAL ART RULE holds: no Bolo pixel is redrawn. See `.agents/memory/mascot-accessory-compositing.md`.

**Debt.** (1) There is ONE equipped slot, so the pagdi and the Navratri choli cannot be worn together; a second slot means layer order and per-accessory anchors, which is real engineering, not a field. (2) The mobile rack is still a text list — no thumbnails, no sections — so the two platforms' shops now differ. (3) The pagdi hides Bolo's indigo crest entirely, and its fold clips the top arc of her eye on `thumbsup` and `cheer`; a ~15px raise is available and unruled.

## 10s. Garments are composited, not drawn: five more outfits on the rack (August 8, 2026, api + web + mobile)

**The rack now sells seven items.** Five garments were added beside the hand-drawn Navratri choli and the pagdi: `kediyu`, `anarkali`, `kurta` (25 Chai each, `OUTFIT_COST`) and `sherwani`, `saree` (40 Chai, the new `PREMIUM_OUTFIT_COST` in `lib/tokenEconomy.ts`). The shop is a three-rung ladder now — 10 / 25 / 40 — and every rung is read off the catalog row by `outfitCost(outfitId)`; nothing about the money path changed, and no new field was needed on `OutfitCatalogItem`.

**A garment is not new Bolo artwork.** The CANONICAL MASCOT RULE forbids redrawing her, and the handoff assumed ten garments therefore meant fifty hand-drawn pose redraws that nothing in this workspace can produce. It does not. `scripts/gen-mascot-outfits.mjs` composites ONE flat piece of cloth over the only region safe to cover — her teal belly, bounded above by a measured per-pose chin line — and then restacks every non-belly canonical pixel on top. That last step is the whole trick: it is what keeps her wings in front of the cloth (a wing crosses her chest on ALL FIVE poses, not just two) and her feet in front of the hem. A garment now costs the same as an accessory: one generated flat object, zero Bolo pixels touched.

**Her body is `#0CA6A0`, not the brand teal `#0D9488`.** Keying on the brand hex captures about a fifth of her. The failure is quiet and badly disguised: the bounding box still lands in roughly the right place, so the placement logs look correct while the mask itself is a speckle — which lets her body redraw over the cloth and turns a dress into a bib. Related trap in the same pipeline: the obvious `-transparent TEAL -alpha extract -negate` route also whitens the transparent background, so the mask must be keyed positively (`-fill white -opaque`) and multiplied by her own alpha.

**Two placement numbers are pinned, not measured.** (1) `thumbsup` width: a wing folds across her chest and splits the visible teal down to 500px, so measuring would tailor that one pose two sizes too small — the cloth is cut to the body that is there, since the wing redraws in front of it anyway. (2) The horizontal anchors are the approved kediyu pass, frozen in a table: the live centroid is a good estimator but it shifts whenever the mask recipe moves by a hair, which would silently re-place art the owner has already signed off. `--live-centre` recomputes for measuring a new pose set.

**Generated garment art comes out portrait; Bolo is squat.** Real clothes are taller than wide and the model draws them that way even when told not to, so all but the kediyu (whose source art was re-cut wide) are `squash`ed to a multiple of the belly height. The script's `ITEMS` table is the record of which source art and which cut produced the art now in the asset dirs — without it, regenerating one garment means guessing the parameters back. **The source art is committed at `scripts/mascot-garment-art/`, deliberately not in `attached_assets/`**, which is gitignored: a generator whose inputs vanish on clone only looks reproducible.

**Owner rulings from this pass.** A Western everyday set (denim jacket, hoodie, retro track jacket, puffer vest) was generated and rejected outright — do not regenerate it without asking. Closed-front re-cuts at `gar-<id>-b.png` were not shipped as-is either; what ships for kurta/sherwani/saree is the **recut `gar-<id>-c.png`** derived from them (see below). `gar-<id>.png` and `-b` remain on disk as the older cuts.

**Every leak is an art hole, not a placement error (August 12, 2026).** Measuring the kurta/sherwani/saree `-b` cuts pose by pose refuted the placement hypothesis: pixels falling outside the cloth's horizontal cut are zero at every row, so `CX` and `WIDTH`/`wfrac` are not the cause. The teal that survives is where the *cloth itself is transparent* — each piece's collar tapers in over its top ~24%, covering barely half its own width where her shoulders need all of it. `scripts/recut-garment-yoke.mjs` fixes the art rather than the geometry: it extends each top-band row sideways to the full piece width using a colour sampled from *behind* the row's contour stroke (every piece is drawn with a 2-3px near-black outline; sampling the first solid pixel paints black wings onto her shoulders), and paints over that now-interior stroke so no seam is left. Output is `gar-<id>-c.png`; the `-b` inputs stay intact. Kurta additionally gets its churidar leg gap filled into one panel. Result: sherwani and saree go clean or near-clean in all five poses. The kurta needed a third pass, because it is the odd garment structurally — short sleeves mean it tapers a second time *below* the sleeve cuff and again toward the ankles, which no top-band job and no knob reaches, so its art is widened FULL height (`--hem`) with interior gaps filled from the yoke down (`--gaps`).

**A widened lower band must be clipped, not hung (August 12, 2026).** Widening the kurta's lower rows the same way only works at `freefrac` 1.0. Below the hem line cloth is unclipped by design (that is what lets a saree flare), so at `freefrac` 0.95 the widened rows hang past her legs as a white slab through her feet — worse than the leak it fixes. Clipped all the way down, with interior gaps filled from the yoke down, the kurta measures 0-46px of residual teal per pose with no detached cloth — that is the shipped configuration. **`squash` 1.05 is load-bearing on all three, kurta included.** At 1.0 the cloth stops ~11px above her feet and a teal band shows between hem and foot (cheer 695px, tryagain 457px) — the exact failure the recut existed to close, and the reason the shipped `ITEMS` row for kurta reads 1.05 rather than the 1.0 in the ruling's table. `freefrac` is 1.0 for kurta (nothing hangs free: churidar are trousers), 0.78 sherwani, 0.5 saree.

**The six coverage rules, the standing grading contract for ANY garment (recorded August 12, 2026; they existed only in an old contract and had to be recovered).** A piece is judged on all five poses, never on `wave` alone:
1. No teal band reaching her body edges (x293 / x725 in `wave`, the equivalent x positions in the other poses).
2. Residual teal only as a single centred scoop, roughly symmetric, at or under the kediyu reference scale (~5,200px). **The kediyu calibration reference is that scoop at x399-604, y500-533**, and it should be quoted whenever rule 2 is applied, because it is the only thing that makes the rule measurable rather than a matter of taste. Report the scoop's bounding box, not just its area, or there is nothing to compare against the reference.
3. Neckline no lower than the bottom of the beak.
4. Hem reaches the feet with no teal band between hem and foot, **in all five poses**. `cheer` is the strict case and the one that fails first.
5. Wings and feet restack in front; no cloth floating outside her silhouette.
6. No teal wedge between garment legs.

**Trim finding: art briefs do NOT need edge-to-edge framing or a matched aspect ratio (August 12, 2026). This supersedes the earlier instruction to specify them,** so do not quietly re-revert it in the next brief. `buildItem` runs `magick([art, "-trim", "+repage", trimmed])` as its first act, so every pixel of background margin is discarded before the art is used; and for any item with a `squash`, `dressPose` builds `["-resize", `${gw}x${Math.round(squash * bh)}!`]`, where the `!` forces exact dimensions and throws the source aspect ratio away entirely (width comes from the `WIDTH` table, height from `squash` × belly height). Margin and aspect are therefore both irrelevant for a squashed piece; only the cloth's INTERNAL proportions matter, meaning where collar, shoulders, hem and cuffs sit as fractions of the trimmed box. The one carve-out: the no-squash branch is `["-resize", `${gw}x`]`, which preserves aspect, so source proportions do reach the output for `kediyu` (the only item with no `squash`).

**A speck in the margin is a placement bug, not a cosmetic one (August 12, 2026).** Because `-trim` is the first thing that touches the art, a single stray pixel anywhere in the background drags the trim box out to meet it, and the piece is then cut, scaled and centred by that inflated box. Measured on the new sherwani flat: three backdrop-noise pixels in the bottom-right corner (just outside the key threshold) stretched the box from the garment's real 541x1076 to 719x1176, padding it with ~178px of empty column, which visibly shifted the coat left and shrank it. Keyed art must therefore be despeckled AND emitted tight-cropped to the cloth, so the generator's trim is a no-op; `scripts/key-garment-magenta.mjs` does both. Note that `-trim` reacts to one unit of alpha and to the RGB under fully transparent pixels, so a despeckle threshold of "visible alpha" is not enough.

**Recutting a review candidate needs `--src` (August 12, 2026).** `recut-garment-yoke.mjs` could only ever read ids listed in its own `ITEMS` table, and it writes `gar-<id>-c.png` unconditionally, which for sherwani, saree, kurta and anarkali IS the installed art: a trial recut destroys what ships. The additive `--src <path>` flag recuts an arbitrary piece that has no `ITEMS` row, and it insists on both `--id` and `--out` so the result cannot land in the art dir. It also has to turn job B on itself, because `legs` is declared per `ITEMS` row and a candidate has none, so under `--src` it is `--gaps` that enables the leg fill. With no `--src` every existing invocation behaves exactly as before. The default path is still destructive and is not fixed by this.

**Debt.** (1) The cloth is one flat cut that does not re-drape per pose, so it will not match hand-drawn art for craft — most visible where a garment meets a folded wing. (2) The mobile rack is still a text list, so the two shops diverge further with every item added. (3) `navratri` (choli) and `kediyu` are both Navratri garments; nothing in the catalog expresses that they are the same occasion, and nothing tags items masc/fem/neutral as the expansion handoff intended. (Debt item "one equipped slot" was closed by 10t.)

## 10t. Two slots: a hat and an outfit are worn at the same time (August 8, 2026, api + web + mobile)

**Owner ruling.** A hat and clothing go on together. The head and the belly never interact, so the constraint that made them exclusive was the single `equipped_outfit` column, not the art.
