**Server: one column per slot, and the catalog decides which.** `user_token_state.equipped_accessory` (nullable, migration `0042_mixed_ultimates.sql`) joins `equipped_outfit`. `equipOutfit(userId, outfitId, slot?)` writes ONLY the slot named — the catalog row's `kind` is the authority, never the client's word — and `slot` is consulted only to decide what to clear. **Omitting `slot` with a null id undresses both slots**, which is deliberate: an older client's bare "take it off" must keep meaning what it meant. `GET /outfits`, `POST /outfits/buy`, `POST /outfits/equip` and `GET /tokens` all carry `equippedAccessory` beside `equippedOutfit`. No data migration moves an existing `equipped_outfit = 'pagdi'` into the new column: a learner wearing the pagdi keeps wearing it out of the garment slot until they touch it again, and the first equip re-homes it.

**Clients stack two layers; they do not bake a second composite.** Pre-rendering every garment×accessory pair multiplies with the catalog. Instead the accessory ships an extra transparent `overlay-<pose>.png` in the same 1024 frame as the pose art, so a renderer draws the garment base and lays the overlay over it at the same box with NO per-pose maths at the call site: `accessoryOverlaySrc(pose, accessory)` (web `lib/mascot-outfits.ts`) / `accessoryOverlaySource(...)` (mobile `lib/mascotOutfits.ts`), both returning null when there is nothing to stack. `Mascot` (both platforms) and mobile `TalkingMascot` take an `accessory` prop beside `outfit`. **Web:** the overlay is an absolutely-positioned `motion.img` inside the existing relative wrapper — the base img stays IN FLOW, because the practice fill-chain collapses any absolutely positioned current img to ~0 height (10-fill rule, §"mascot.tsx"). **Mobile:** the overlay Image carries explicit width/height; a bare `absoluteFill` Image takes its intrinsic size on iOS and ignores `resizeMode`, and `pointerEvents` is not a valid `Image` prop.

**Wearing state is a pair, not a string.** `useEquippedOutfit` / `OutfitContext` expose `{ garment, accessory }`. The shop keeps ONE `previewed` id and derives which slot it lands in from the item's `kind`, so trying a hat on leaves the garment on the bird. Every per-item take-off sends its slot; only the bare "Take it all off" button (shown when EITHER slot is filled) sends a slot-less null. Pinned on both platforms in the outfits tests (web `src/test/outfits.test.tsx`, mobile `__tests__/outfits.test.tsx`): both slots at once, previewing one slot leaving the other, and the exact equip payloads including `slot`.

**Copy says so, in two places.** The Bazaar header on both platforms reads "Buy once, keep forever. Bolo wears your pick everywhere in the app, and a hat and an outfit go on at the same time." (No em dashes in user-facing copy: standing owner rule, recorded in `replit.md`.) — the two facts learners cannot discover from the UI (it is worn app-wide, and the slots combine). Separately, `lib/parrotChat.ts` answers "are you a girl or a boy?" the same way in BOTH prompts (`BOLO_PERSONA_PROMPT` and `buildSystemPrompt`): never pick a gender, say playfully that it is a bird that can be dressed from the Bolo Bazaar, in the target language, and carry on. Older UI strings still say "she"; a pronoun sweep was cancelled and is not done.

**Debt.** (1) The art-parity test enumerates `mascot-<pose>.png` on both platforms but not `overlay-<pose>.png`, so a missing overlay still ships silently. (2) The stacking is not verified on a real iPhone. (3) The re-cut sherwani (`freefrac: 0.78`) has still never been looked at.

## 10u. Bolo Bazaar: a wardrobe filter, a sticky dressing room, and the mobile picture rack (August 8, 2026, web + mobile)

**The dressing room stops scrolling away.** On both platforms the storefront (awning, signboard, balance, previewed bird, counter) plus the single action button are pinned and the RACK is the only thing that moves — comparing seven items used to mean scrolling the bird out of view. Web wraps them in `sticky top-0 z-20 -mx-4 bg-background` inside the page's own scroller (`data-testid="outfit-dressing-room"`); the negative margin is what lets the block cover cards sliding under it edge to edge, and sticky (not fixed) means no height maths. Mobile achieves the same by moving that block OUTSIDE the `ScrollView` (`testID="outfit-dressing-room"`) — the scroller now starts at the shop copy. The preview mascot shrank 180→150 on mobile to keep the pinned block near half the screen; measured at 402×874 the web block is 477px of 874 and the probe asserts it stays under 68%.

**Owned stock has its own door.** The purchase button on an owned card now reads **Dress Bolo** (was "Wear this"; `outfit-wear-<id>` and the take-off testID are unchanged), and a two-chip filter above the rack (`outfit-filters`, `outfit-filter-all`, `outfit-filter-owned`, labelled "Everything" / "My wardrobe · N") narrows the rack to purchased items. The count on the chip is derived from the same payload the rack renders, so the two can never disagree; an empty wardrobe renders `outfit-filter-empty` rather than a bare rack. Filtering is client-side over the `GET /outfits` payload — the server keeps returning the whole catalog with `owned` per row.

**Mobile got the web picture rack.** `app/(app)/outfits.tsx` replaced its text list with the two-column grid of square previews web already had: local `RACK_SECTIONS`/`groupOutfits` twins (unknown `kind` falls into the first section), an `OutfitThumb` drawing `mascotSource('wave', id)` — the item ON Bolo, never separate art — and an OWNED/WEARING or price badge pinned over the picture. Card width is computed once from `Dimensions.get('window').width` minus the 20pt gutters and a 12pt column gap, and the thumb is that minus the card's own padding, so the picture is a true square on any handset. **RN has no `transformOrigin`**, so the accessory crop (`preview === 'head'`) centres the head instead of pivoting on it: with `scale` first in the transform array the translate is expressed in PRE-scale units, i.e. `(0.5 - HEAD_X) * size` and `(0.5 - HEAD_Y) * size`. Verified by rendering the same CSS the RN-web layer emits over the real pose PNG.

**The station master's cap is the second accessory (August 8, 2026).** `station-cap` ("Station master's cap", accessory, head preview, accessory price) is navy and red with a brass engine badge, and it fits the journey's railway framing. Its source art is committed at `scripts/mascot-accessory-art/station-cap.png` with per-pose reference layers beside it, because the first cut of this cap was reviewed and approved out of `/tmp`, which was then wiped: the montage survived as an attached asset, the pose files did not. Rebuilt with `node scripts/gen-mascot-accessories.mjs --id station-cap --fudge 0.78 --install`. Two things about generating a SECOND hat: the reference layers can be seeded from an existing accessory's approved placements (they encode "where a hat goes on this pose", and `--fudge` rescales), and `NUDGE_X` in that script carries owner-approved per-pose horizontal corrections, currently +20px right on `thinking` and `tryagain`, whose heads are rolled about 19 degrees. Everything else about it is ordinary: catalog row, five pose files plus five overlays per platform, both asset maps, and `PRICE_LIST` in the api outfits test.

**Harness.** `qa/outfit-equip.mjs` buys the cap as a signed-in learner, wears it with a garment, then reads the mascot `<img>` srcs on home and journey to prove the bird outside the shop agrees with the bird inside it, and that taking the hat off leaves the garment on. Any rack button it clicks goes through its `tap()` helper: the dev-preview banner and the shop's own sticky dressing room both sit exactly where playwright parks a scrolled-into-view element and swallow the click. `qa/bazaar-shots.mjs` (chromium + a Clerk sign-in token, same pattern as the other qa probes): checks the rack renders, that the dressing room stays on screen and stays short enough after a 900px scroll, that owned cards say Dress Bolo, and that the wardrobe chip's count matches the filtered card count; shoots mobile/desktop/scrolled/owned. `THUMBS_ONLY=1` re-renders just the mobile thumb crop with no Clerk token burned. Note the probe needs the API workflow running CURRENT code — a stale API server served a two-item catalog with no `kind` and made every check look like a client bug.

## 10v. Dark mode: a semantic text token over a hardcoded white card (August 8, 2026, web)

**The failure was one class, repeated 90 times.** Text on the affected surfaces already used tokens (`text-foreground`, `text-muted-foreground`), so it correctly turned near-white in dark mode, while the surface under it was a literal `bg-white` that did not turn. Chat bubbles, the composer, the journey "Wave me through" and "Test out" CTAs, progress cards, the friends list and the plan pages were all white-on-white at about 1.05:1. In light mode `--card` IS white, so the sweep of bare `bg-white` to `bg-card` is a no-op there; only `bg-white/<opacity>` (a scrim over colour) and two knob-style dots on coloured tracks were deliberately left literal.

**A token can also fail on its own.** Dark `--primary` at 68% lightness sat just under AA in both of its jobs (indigo text on a card, and near-black text on an indigo fill), so it moved to 75%. Tailwind opacity modifiers on already-dim tokens are the other trap: `text-muted-foreground/50` is a soft touch on white and nearly invisible on navy, so the dim nav labels take a `dark:` bump. `--destructive` cannot serve both jobs at once, since the lightness that makes red readable AS TEXT on a dark card is too light UNDER white button text; the token stays tuned for text and the solid destructive button carries `dark:bg-red-700`.

**Harness.** `qa/dark-contrast.mjs` signs in, forces `bolo.theme` before first paint, walks the app and reports every visible text run below its WCAG threshold against the background actually painted behind it, opening dialogs listed in its `OPEN` map so dialog-only copy is covered. Two things it gets right that a static class scan cannot: it measures composited colour, and it catches token-level failures. Do not parse computed colours with a regex, though: Tailwind opacity modifiers serialize as `oklab(L a b / alpha)` and a naive number-grab reads the lightness as a red channel, which reports pale grey as black; the probe paints onto a 1x1 canvas and reads the pixel instead. Known remaining reds, both unchanged from light mode and therefore design decisions rather than dark-mode bugs: white on the lime boarding-pass hero on home (about 3.1:1) and the Bazaar signboard's gold-on-green eyebrow (4.44:1).

**Mobile was already sound.** `bolo-mobile` themes through `useColors()` palettes rather than a `.dark` class, and its hardcoded colours are white-on-accent CTA text and deliberate paper stock (the journey postcard, the signal Chai chip), so no text is unreadable. Those two surfaces stay light in dark mode by design, like the bazaar and chai-stall art.

## 10w. A content typo needs a correction pass, and the result buttons need a measured fold (August 9, 2026, api + web)

**"jay shri krushna" is now "jay shri krishna"** in the Gujarati greetings lesson, in the scenario opener the chat uses, and in the progression spec table. Fixing the curated library is NOT enough on its own: `seedLesson` leaves an already-seeded lesson's rows alone and `topUpLesson` only INSERTS what is missing, and the dedup key is native script plus English gloss, so a respelt romanization matches the existing row and is skipped. Dev and production would have kept the typo forever. `topUpLesson` therefore ends with a correction pass that rewrites `romanized` when the curated entry for that (native script, English) pair disagrees, logging each change. Romanized is the only field it rewrites: native script and English identify the row, and everything else (premium, sort order, elo, source) is environment state, not library content.

**The practice result buttons were under the fold, and the bird was not the whole story.** `qa/practice-fold-probe.mjs` drives a real signed-in attempt (Clerk sign-in ticket, WebAudio `getUserMedia` shim, the coach's own clip piped back into the mic so the attempt lands a genuine result state) and then measures, at three phone sizes, whether the Retry / Next row sits above the fold, plus the height of every block competing for the column. It tears the dev preview banner out first: that banner is 71px of workspace chrome the published app never shows, and leaving it in poisons every fold number. Measured at 390x844, the row's bottom was 32px under the fold; the compact result-state parrot band went from 110px to 72px (and to 52px on windows shorter than 720px), the result panel's spacing went from `space-y-3 mt-2` to `space-y-2 mt-1`, and the score card from `p-4` to `p-3`. 390x844 and 430x932 now clear the fold with room. 375x667 (iPhone SE) still needs about 135px of scroll, because the score card alone is 454px: the band ladder, the transcript, the coach's line and the tip. Shrinking the bird further cannot close that gap; the card itself would have to get denser on short viewports.

## 10x. Every game can say what you got wrong, and each game words it itself (August 10, 2026, web + mobile)

**Two affordances, one review.** At the end of a run the score card itself becomes the way in — its caption swaps from "Score" to "See misses" in the primary colour and the card turns into a button (`<game>-score-card`, `quick-score-card` for the five games on the shared frame) — and a secondary "See what you missed" CTA sits under the primary Play Again button. A perfect run renders NEITHER: the card stays a plain non-interactive card and the CTA renders nothing at count 0, so there is never a dead button promising a review of nothing. Both open the same list: `components/game-miss-review.tsx` (web, radix Dialog) and `components/GameMissReview.tsx` (mobile, RN Modal), deliberate twins that must be changed together.

**The games describe their own misses; the shell never derives them.** A `GameMiss` is `{ prompt, promptSub?, answer, correct, answerLabel?, correctLabel? }`, supplied per wrong round. Deriving it generically from the round's phrase ids would be wrong for at least two games: Wrong Platform SCORES through the anchor phrase while the right answer is the stray (an id-derived row would name the wrong phrase), and Luggage Match submits the counterpart a tag was first mis-paired with rather than an "answer" at all. `answer: null` is the lapsed round and renders as "nothing — the round ran out", which is why a timeout path must still supply a review. The two label overrides exist for games that are not answered in words: Script Trace reads "Your best 32 out of 100 / Pass mark 40", not "You said".

**What a miss means, per game.** Quick frame (Ticket Check, Wrong Platform, Luggage Match, Express Listening, Signal Lights): the wrong pick, worded as the round presented it — the audio games name the clip by meaning since there was no visible prompt. Word Match has no score at all, so a miss is a mismatched flip pair and the Time card is the reviewable card. Bolo Quiz kept only a numeric score; the review is derived at completion from the existing questions + answers arrays using the same correctness call the score used, so the list matches what the score docked. Script Trace has no wrong answer — a miss is a character that never reached the pass mark, carrying its best accuracy, and a character skipped past reads as "not traced". Collected misses reset on every replay path (Play Again, Choose Topic, chapter Replay); the once-daily quiz has no in-mount replay.

**The review obeys 10j too: script never appears without its reading.** `GameMiss` therefore carries `answerSub` / `correctSub` alongside the two values, rendered directly beneath them in the same quieter two-line stack the game cards use, and the games supply them from `phrase.romanized` (no transliteration helper ships to a client). Where the reading used to be stacked under a prompt that did NOT contain the script — Ticket Check, Listen and Pick, Phrase Builder, Bolo Quiz's listen and order rounds — it moved down to the line whose script it actually reads, so nothing is stated twice. Two consequences worth knowing: Speed Round shows the reading in the review even in hard mode (the run is over, so the review is a study list, not part of the challenge, and hard-mode OPTIONS are native script, hence the reading now rides on each option), and Signal Lights leads its sub line with the reading before "It means …". Phrase Builder's submitted answer and Bolo Quiz's picked script stay reading-less because per-word romanization exists nowhere and only the correct choice's reading is in the payload. Empty romanization renders nothing, on both platforms, on both lines.

**Trap (miss review).** The web quick frame renders the score card as a `<button>` only when reviewable and as a `<div>` otherwise, so a test asserting on the non-reviewable card cannot use `toBeDisabled()`. On mobile, a results screen that a test renders directly needs its `misses` prop defaulted, or the direct-render tests crash on `misses.length`.

## 10y. A signal offers itself once, ever — after that it is the learner's to tap (August 10, 2026, web + mobile)

**The bug was scope, not logic.** The soft stop was already "once per signal", but "once" meant once per SESSION: web kept the seen marks in sessionStorage and mobile kept them in a process-memory Set. A learner whose journey resumes at a held signal — which is exactly where a learner who stopped at one resumes — was met by the same "Signal ahead" dialog on every sign-in and every app launch, including after they had already played the game or waved through. Seen marks are now device-scoped, like clears: web localStorage under the same `bolo-signal-stop-shown:{lang}` key, mobile AsyncStorage under `bolo.signalStopSeen:{lang}`. Nothing else moved — the mark is still written at auto-open AND at a manual tap, so closing the dialog with X never re-opens it either, and the signal itself stays tappable in every state.

**Waves stay session-scoped on purpose.** Do not "fix" that for symmetry: waving is never permanent, so an unclaimed first-clear Chai grant must stay claimable on a later visit. The three scopes are now: waves = session, seen = device, clears = device (+ server payload as cross-device truth for waves and clears).

**Mobile hydration is the load-bearing part.** `useSignalMemory`'s `hydrated` now gates on BOTH disk reads (`Promise.all` of the cleared and stop-seen hydrations), because the auto-open effect reads seen synchronously during render; gating on the cleared read alone would let a signal re-offer itself in the window before the seen set landed. Both hydrations merge rather than replace, so a mark written while the read was in flight survives.

**Test trap this created.** RN tests that previously isolated with `resetSignalMemory()` alone now leak between cases: the in-memory caches are cleared, hydration reads the previous case's marks back off the AsyncStorage mock, and every auto-open assertion silently goes quiet (the failure reads as "unable to find signal-dialog-title", not as pollution). Any suite touching signals must `await AsyncStorage.clear()` in `beforeEach` too. On web the same isolation lives in the file's own cleanup, which now removes the localStorage key rather than the sessionStorage one.

## 10z. A phrase that earns nothing comes back before the session ends (August 10, 2026, web + mobile)

**The rule.** A practice attempt that earns NO XP puts its phrase back in the session: once the base list is exhausted, every zero-XP phrase returns, in the order it was missed, and keeps returning until it earns something. Three zeros release it. Owner-ruled that EVERY kind of zero counts a strike, nocatch included — otherwise a dead mic or a phone in a noisy room would loop the learner forever in a session that can never end. Earning XP settles the debt immediately; strikes are kept as history and are never reset, so a phrase that already burned two strikes still gets only one more if it lapses back to zero.

**Zero is read from the server, not inferred.** The trigger is `xpAwarded === 0` on the evaluation response — the same field the XP chips use. Do not switch on band: `retry` and `nocatch` are the usual zeros, but the ladder is display-only and a future band could award nothing without being either of those.

**The cursor moves backwards, so forward progress has to change owner.** Both platforms carry an `inEncore` flag. Once it flips, `handleNext`/`next()` may no longer advance with `currentIndex + 1` — the whole tail of the list would replay. Advances come from the queue alone, then test-out, then the summary. Test-out is exempt end to end (one take per phrase, judged as a batch), and manual prev/next navigation stays free and ungated as before.

**Two attempts inside one render pass.** The strike counter is mirrored in a ref alongside its state, because two evaluations landing in the same pass would both read strike 0 off state and queue the phrase twice.

**Keying differs by platform, deliberately.** Web keys the queue and strikes by phrase ID (its `sessionResults` is ID-keyed, so a revisit overwrites the old result). Mobile keys by phrase INDEX, matching `bands`/`xpData`/`sessionFeedback` — which also means a mobile revisit overwrites the band and accumulates XP, exactly as a retry already did.

**Naming the detour matters.** Web shows an "Another go" chip beside the phrase counter; mobile appends " · another go" to the header label. Without it the counter reads like the session went backwards. The result card carries the same news in words: "comes back at the end of the session", or "That's three goes" when the phrase is released. Mobile's forward button had to learn about the queue too — it read "Finish" on the last phrase while an encore was still pending (`hasNextStop`).

**Test fallout worth knowing.** Any existing test that scores zero XP and then expects the summary now detours. Two did (web phrase-navigation, mobile streak-toasts) — one was given real XP because its subject is navigation, the other now burns all three strikes because its subject IS the zero. Also: RNTL's `toHaveTextContent` matches the WHOLE string, so assert the full sentence.

## 10aa. A glitched listener does not get to quote itself (August 10, 2026, server)

**Field report:** a Gujarati attempt on `જય શ્રી કૃષ્ણ` came back as `We heard: "вучит крашна."` — Russian Cyrillic — under the copy that says our listener glitched and it's on us. The learner read it as the app mishearing them in a language they were not speaking.

**The hint was never missing.** `POST /openai/pronunciation` pins `language` from the phrase row (or, with no `phraseId`, from `users.activeLanguage`) and names the language in the STT prompt. `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe` treats `language` as a SOFT hint and drifts on short clips anyway — the same Cyrillic family documented in the Aug 3 diagnosis. Everything downstream then behaved correctly: the transcript is in a script the target cannot be compared against, so `applyScoreGuards` returned `script-mismatch-nocatch`, and nocatch is a system miss (no score, no penalty, no streak break).

**The defect was that the response still carried the drifted transcript.** The sibling nocatch path (empty / uncorroborated passes) has always returned `transcript: ""`; the guard path returned the foreign-script string, and every client renders `result.transcript` verbatim as the "We heard" line (web practice, mobile practice, mobile review). The guard path now blanks it too, for exactly the reason its `transcriptRomanized` was already blank: a transliteration of a glitched reading is noise, and so is the reading itself. One server change fixes web and mobile at once — no client edit.

**Only the incomparable case is blanked — `targetSim.comparable ? transcript : ""`.** Nocatch has two causes. Guard 1b (a LATIN transcript for a non-Latin target with romanized sim < 0.45) is comparable, readable, and may be exactly what the learner said, so it keeps its "We heard" line; that behaviour is pinned by the S1 dual-pass test that expects `hello world` back. Only the incomparable-script cause is withheld. Blanking both is what the first attempt at this fix did, and it broke that pin.

**Evidence is not display.** The real transcript still rides the signed evaluation token (and from there the attempt row) and the nocatch diagnostics sidecar, which is where drift gets measured. Anything analysing drift must read those, never the response body.

**Not changed, and deliberately so:** when one pass drifts and the other reads the target correctly, the S1 conservative chooser still picks the farther (drifted) transcript and the attempt resolves nocatch rather than being scored off the pass that happens to match. Preferring the script-matching pass would score learners off a reading the recognizer only half-believes; that is a scoring-honesty change and needs an owner ruling, not a bug fix.


## 10ab. The web icon set is OWNER-SUPPLIED art, installed byte-for-byte (August 11, 2026, web only)

**The old tab icon was the whole wave pose scaled down** — a small bird in wide margins with a raised wing, reading as a teal smudge at 32px. It was replaced not by an in-repo crop but by five finished PNGs the owner supplied: `favicon-16.png`, `favicon-32.png`, `favicon-48.png`, `favicon-180.png`, `favicon-512.png`, all in `artifacts/gujarati-coach/public/`. **These files are owner art and must be installed unmodified** — no re-encoding, no re-crop, no optimizer pass; verify by checksum against the source rather than regenerating them. (An earlier in-task attempt did generate a head-and-shoulders crop of `mascot/mascot-wave.png` for the 32/48; it was superseded and overwritten by the supplied files.)

**Only three link tags exist and all three point at the supplied set.** `index.html` carries `icon` 32x32 → `/favicon-32.png`, `icon` 48x48 → `/favicon-48.png`, and `apple-touch-icon` 180x180 → `/favicon-180.png` (repointed from `/apple-touch-icon.png`). There is NO web manifest in this app and no 16px link tag, so `favicon-16.png` and `favicon-512.png` ship as assets with no reference yet — do not add tags for them without a brief. `public/apple-touch-icon.png` still exists but is now unreferenced. OG image, sitemap and meta tags untouched.

**`public/logo.svg` is gone.** It drew the Indian tricolour as a speech bubble — wrong for a product covering India, Pakistan, Bangladesh, Sri Lanka and Nepal — and a repo-wide search found zero references (no link tag, no manifest, no component, no doc). It was never the favicon. The same mark still ships in the Play Store listing graphics; see the §8 debt row.

## 10ab. Cached phrase audio is now listened to, not trusted (August 11, 2026, server)

**Field report:** on production, `સાચવીને જજો` played back as "Saachvine" — the second word was simply absent. The suspicion was the English meaning clip cutting it off; it was not. The meaning clip only starts from the phrase clip's `onended` plus 400ms, and splitting the cached MP3 settled it: the production clip's second half transcribes to nothing, while the development row for the same phrase (a different take, synthesized a day earlier) says "jajo" clearly. `gpt-4o-mini-tts` had dropped a word, and the cache made that take permanent for every learner from 2026-07-28 onward.

**No cheap heuristic can find this.** The bad take runs 2.45s against the healthy 2.62s — the model said one word slowly — so duration, byte size, and a bytes-per-character audit over all 10,333 cached clips all look normal (that audit only ever flagged long sentences). The only signal that distinguishes a truncated clip from a good one is what it says. Verification therefore transcribes the clip.

**`src/lib/phraseAudioVerify.ts` judges by phonetic length, not wording.** Expected letters come from the phrase's `romanized` column (falling back to transliterating `nativeScript`); heard letters come from the transcript, normalized through the same `normalizeLatin` folding. `coverage = heard ÷ expected` must land in `[0.7, 2.2]`. Exact word matching was rejected deliberately: recognizers return the same good clip as Gurmukhi, as Cyrillic, or with different word splits, and a strict comparison would reject healthy audio wholesale. Dropped content is what survives that noise — the reported take scores 0.54 where healthy takes of the same phrase score ~1.0.

**Every ambiguous outcome passes.** Untransliterable transcripts, recognizer outages, and phrases under 6 comparable letters return `unverifiable` with `ok: true`. Discarding a good clip strips a learner's audio; keeping a doubtful one only preserves the status quo.

**Languages the recognizer cannot hear are never verified.** A dev sample flagged Santali (empty transcript) and Manipuri (unrelated Latin) — both of which the per-language speech probe already classifies. `isSpeechVerifiable` gates on `languages.speech_capability === "supported"`, so `degraded`/`unsupported` languages short-circuit to `unverifiable` before any API call. Without that gate the sweep would have evicted and re-synthesized every clip in those languages, forever, on no evidence.

**Nothing enters the cache unheard, except on the playback path.** `synthesizeVerifiedPhraseAudio` (`src/lib/phraseAudioSynthesis.ts`) synthesizes, verifies, and retries up to 3 takes, returning the best-covered take if all fail; the startup pre-warm goes through it. `POST /openai/tts` cannot wait — a learner is listening — so it serves its take, then `verifyServedTakeInBackground` listens after the cache insert resolves (chained on the insert, or the delete would race its own row) and drops the row if the take fails twice. All three synthesis branches are covered — gpt-audio, `gpt-4o-mini-tts`, and ElevenLabs; leaving any one out would have made a provider switch quietly reopen the hole.

**Every write is a compare-and-swap on the exact take that was judged.** The sweep updates `WHERE cache_key = ? AND audio_base64 = <the audited take>`, and the post-serve verifier deletes under the same condition. Without it, two concurrent cache misses (only one wins `onConflictDoNothing`) let the loser's verdict delete the winner's row, and a slow verifier of an old take can wipe a freshly written replacement. A failed synthesis now leaves the old row alone: a clip that says half the phrase still beats silence, and the next sweep retries. Batches also take a Postgres advisory lock so only one sweep runs per database — each batch spends transcription and synthesis calls, and parallel callers would both race the same rows and double the bill.

**Two failed listens before anything is deleted.** A dev sample of 150 clips showed first-listen coverage as low as 0.30 on clips that pass cleanly on a second listen — recognizer noise is real and the double-check absorbs it. The sweep also overwrites the row with a verified replacement rather than deleting it, so a false positive costs one synthesis and never leaves a phrase silent.

**An overwrite is the only irreversible act, so three brakes sit in front of it.** (1) A replacement that itself fails verification is written ONLY if its coverage beats the cached clip's; otherwise the original stays and the phrase is counted `unfixable`. Swapping one unjudged take for another with no evidence it is better is churn, and it destroys the old audio. (2) The condemned take is copied to R2 first (`src/lib/ttsAuditBackup.ts`, key `tts-audit-replaced/<lang>/<phraseId>-<runAt>.mp3` plus a `.json` sidecar carrying the verdict); the backup throws when R2 is unconfigured or the upload fails, and the row is then left alone — a WARN-swallowed tee here would make "restorable" a fiction. (3) Writes are capped (`maxWrites`, default 25/batch, and a run-wide budget the driver decrements via `--max-writes`, default 100), and `WRITE_FAILURE_BREAKER` consecutive write failures stop writes for the rest of the batch. The cap slot is reserved BEFORE the synthesis await and released when no write happens, because the pool runs phrases concurrently and a post-await check would let every in-flight worker through a cap of one. Delete and overwrite are asymmetric: a deleted row is just a cache miss that re-synthesizes, an overwritten row's previous take is gone — and a production database is not covered by workspace checkpoints.

**The sweep runs where the cache lives.** Development and production hold different takes of the same phrase, so auditing here fixes nothing there. `POST /tts-audit/batch` (`src/routes/ttsAudit.ts`, mounted in the PUBLIC section above `requireAuth`) walks a phrase-id cursor a batch at a time and is driven by `scripts/auditTtsCache.ts` (`pnpm --filter @workspace/api-server run audit-tts-cache`), locally or against a deployment. Authorization is the cron convention — a constant-time compare against `TTS_AUDIT_SECRET` (falling back to `SESSION_SECRET`), failing closed when unset, checked before any work. The cursor is persisted per run because long sweeps do not survive their shell.

**Known gap:** roughly a third of clips come back unverifiable — mostly phrases under the 6-letter floor, plus recognizer drift into scripts we cannot transliterate. The sweep is blind there by design.

**First production run: Gujarati, dry run, August 12, 2026 — the detector catches the defect it was built for.** `--dry-run --languages gu --remote https://bolo-india.app`, driven in four foreground chunks (`qa/tts-audit-prod-gu-chunk{1..4}.json`, merged into `qa/tts-audit-prod-gu-report.json`). **532 of 532 Gujarati clips were cached and heard; 0 uncached; 384 verified, 143 unverifiable, 5 flagged, 0 evicted, 0 replaced.** All five flags are `short` — no `long`, no `empty` — so on this language truncation is the only failure mode the detector sees. `સાચવીને જજો` (3975) **was flagged**, coverage 0.58, heard "Sačvine": the reported take was still live in production at the time of the sweep and the sweep finds it (it was repaired later the same day — see below). The kshama cluster (`ક્ષમા કરો`, 3981) was **not** flagged and is genuinely healthy — 0.90 on three independent listens, transcript "Kšamákoro" carrying both words.

**Truncation is a single clip, not a systemic Gujarati bug — and the acoustic envelope, not coverage, is what proves it.** `qa/tts-audit-prod-gu-envelope.mjs` decodes each clip and counts spoken bursts and speaking rate. (Its silence threshold differs from the later general-purpose `qa/tts-envelope.mjs` described below, so the two report slightly different speech-seconds for the same 3975 take; the signature — one burst, long trailing silence, implausible rate — agrees.) 3975 holds **0.58 s of speech then 1.29 s of silence** in a 2.45 s file, one burst for a two-word phrase, at 22.4 romanized letters per speech-second against 10.8–15.4 for every other clip; the same two words occupy ~1.06 s of speech inside the healthy sentence clip 5954. The other four flags do not show that signature: 69 `શુભેચ્છા` (0.64) is the recognizer answering in **Bengali script**, which transliterates shorter than the romanization; 226 (0.54) had the recognizer degenerate into a repeated vowel mark and **cleared on 2 of 3** independent re-listens; 3967 (0.63) cleared on 1 of 3; 128 (0.68) drops only the final `છે` in the transcript while its rate and burst structure are normal. Coverage alone cannot separate these from a real defect — burst count and speech-seconds can.

**The 0.70 pass mark sits on the shoulder of the distribution, not in a quiet gap.** Over 389 length-checkable Gujarati clips: min 0.53, p1 0.60, p5 0.77, p25 0.92, median 1.00, p95 1.14, max 1.41. In 0.1-wide buckets: 0.5→3, 0.6→6, **0.7→21**, 0.8→47, 0.9→88, 1.0→187, 1.1→31, 1.2→3, 1.3→2, 1.4→1. Twenty-one clips sit in the first bucket above the line and nine below it, so moving the threshold in either direction trades false positives against misses one clip at a time. This is why a repair pass must not run unattended on coverage alone. Of the 143 unverifiable, 110 are the under-6-letter floor and 33 recognizer script drift.

**Reading production clip bytes needs no deployment change.** `executeSql({ environment: "production" })` is a read-only replica: `tts_cache.audio_base64` can be pulled in `substr` chunks and written to local mp3 files (`qa/tts-audit-prod-gu-clips/`), which is how the flagged clips were listened to. The production playback route was deliberately not used — an uncached key there synthesizes and writes. Cache keys are reproducible offline: `sha256(text ␀ provider ␀ model ␀ voice ␀ languageName ␀ "phrase:v2:<instructions digest>")`, currently `gpt-4o-mini-tts / gpt-4o-mini-tts / nova / Gujarati`.

**The first production repair ran on August 12, 2026, and it was a single clip.** Phrase 3975 (`gu`, `સાચવીને જજો`), the take from the original field report, was condemned twice (coverage 0.667 then 0.583, heard "Sačuvane") and overwritten with a verified replacement, confirmed good by ear. Targeting one phrase needs the cursor window, not an id: `ttsCacheAudit` accepts explicit `phraseIds`, but the HTTP endpoint never parses them, so a single-clip repair is expressed as `afterPhraseId: <id - 1>` with `limit: 1` after checking that the next id in that language really is the intended one. Always dry-run the identical window first; it costs one transcription and proves both the parameters and the verdict before anything is written. The condemned take is at `tts-audit-replaced/gu/3975-2026-08-12T15-28-57-198Z.mp3`.

**Nothing can read those backups back.** The backup half of the contract works; there is no reader, no restore script, no endpoint, and the workspace has no production write channel, so restoring a wrongly-replaced take today requires shipping code and publishing. Acceptable only while the replaced clip is known-worthless. Deliberately not fixed yet.

**`qa/tts-envelope.mjs` measures a take's shape without a recognizer.** It decodes with ffmpeg and reports burst count, speech seconds, leading and trailing silence, and romanized letters per speech-second. Its value is separating a real truncation from recognizer noise: the bad 3975 take showed one burst, 0.74s of speech and 1.27s of trailing silence at 17.6 letters/speech-second (implausibly fast for the full phrase), while its verified replacement runs 1.54s of speech at 8.4. Burst count alone is not a verdict, because the 200ms merge threshold folds a short inter-word gap into one burst; the rate and the trailing silence are the discriminating numbers. Use it to size a suspicion, then listen.

**Driver caveat for chunked runs:** totals reset per process, so each invocation overwrites `--report` with only its own chunk. Chunked runs must write per-chunk report paths and be merged afterwards; the cursor (`artifacts/api-server/qa/.tts-audit-prod.cursor`, not configurable) is what carries across.


## 10ac. Noise robustness bench: cleanup measured, cleanup dropped (August 11, 2026, measurement only)

**`qa/noise-robustness-bench.mjs` re-scores the frozen pilot corpus under controlled noise, and its verdict is that audio cleanup does not work here.** 2,640 scoring runs / 5,280 transcription calls over a seeded, stratified 80-clip sample (`qa/pilot-results/noise-bench/sample.json`, committed) at clean / 20 / 12 / 6 / 0 dB SNR, against four pre-transcription treatments — passthrough, a generic speech chain, `afftdn` profiled from the clip's own 0.6 s noise-only opening, and profile→clean→trim. **No chain produced a reproducible gain at any level in either noise character.** The one significant win (babble @20 dB, profiled, +15.2 ± 6.1) did not survive an independent re-run (+4.5 ± 4.6); the trim's harm did (−30.2 → −18.7 ± 6.4). Cleanup on clean audio leans negative (generic −3.3 ± 2.5, clean-audio no-scores 4% → 9%) and, more tellingly, *shuffles* it: 10-12 of 63 clips get worse and 6-7 get better under every chain. Full findings, tables and reproduction commands: `docs/specs/noise-robustness-bench.md`; regenerate the table with `--report`. Nothing in the live route, mobile app, or web app was touched.

**Three numbers that outlive the cleanup question.** (1) **The scoring path has a ±3.8-point measurement floor** on an 80-clip sample and **flips the displayed band 18% of the time** when the identical clip is transcribed twice — established by re-running the whole clean baseline as a variance control, and the reason several first-pass "effects" evaporated. Any future scoring claim must be sized against this. (2) **Speech babble is the whole problem; steady broadband is nearly free** — babble costs 16 points at 20 dB SNR and 63 at 0 dB, while broadband costs nothing measurable until 6 dB. Detect babble, not loudness. (3) **Dual-pass STT disagreement climbs 38% → 90% under babble but holds near 50% under broadband**, making it the best available "this recording was compromised" signal — and it costs nothing extra, because both passes already run on every attempt.

**Bench conventions worth copying.** Runs are append-only JSONL keyed by runId so an interrupted run resumes (long jobs do not survive this environment; drive it with `--max-minutes` in foreground chunks). It imports the REAL scoring modules through the tsx loader (`chooseConservativeTranscript` → `compareToTarget` → `applyScoreGuards` → honesty cap → `bandFromScore`) rather than copying them; the single substitution is the stochastic gpt-5.4-mini judge, replaced by a deterministic `round(sim × 100)` proxy so deltas measure noise rather than judge variance — absolute scores in the bench are therefore NOT learner-visible scores. Score deltas are computed over `native`/`american_accent`/`subtle_error` only, per the corpus label protocol; `wrong_attempt` gets its own column. Standout cells are re-run before they are believed — with ~20 comparisons at a 2-SE bar, one false positive is expected, and one is exactly what showed up. Raw results (`results.jsonl`) are gitignored: they hold learner transcripts.

## 10ac. Every attempt now says how noisy the room was, and why it failed (August 11, 2026, server + reader)

**What was already answerable, and what was not.** The failure rate itself was never missing: every attempt, including one that failed to score, writes an `attempts` row carrying its band (`nocatch` = system miss) and `stt_disagreement`. What nobody could answer was WHY an attempt failed and HOW NOISY the recording was — the cause existed only inside the write-only nocatch diagnostic files, gated to the pilot allowlist, with no reader. This change adds the two missing derived values and the reader; per the plan it adds NO column for disagreement or band, because both already exist.

**Two nullable columns on `attempts`** (`lib/db/src/schema/attempts.ts`, migration `0044_brief_gunslinger`): `audio_snr_db` (real) and `nocatch_cause` (text). Null means "not measured / predates instrumentation" everywhere, and the reader reports that as its own bucket rather than folding it into a rate. Both travel from scoring to storage exactly like the dual-pass fields — as OPTIONAL claims on the server-signed evaluation token (`snrDb`, `nocatchCause` in `src/lib/evaluationToken.ts`), so a token issued before this change simply carries neither and still records an attempt.

**The measurement reads the room tone every hold-to-talk clip already opens with.** `src/lib/audioNoise.ts` walks the RIFF chunk list into 16-bit PCM, takes 20ms frame RMS, and reports `20·log10(loudest tenth ÷ noise floor)`, clamped to [-20, 60] dB. The floor is `min(first 300ms, 10th-percentile frame)`: the opening is the intended sample, and the percentile both rescues an all-speech clip (which would otherwise have no floor) and stops a click in the opening from masquerading as a noisy room. Degradation is deliberate and total — silence reads 0 dB, all-speech reads near 0, under 40ms reads null, non-PCM reads null, and **`measureAttemptSnrDb` never throws or rejects**, so a measurement failure is indistinguishable from a scored attempt's point of view.

**It costs the learner nothing.** The call rides in the SAME `Promise.all` as the two STT passes in `POST /openai/pronunciation`, so it overlaps a ~1s network wait. Measured on a 3s clip: 0.6ms median when the pipeline already produced WAV (no conversion), 135ms median / 405ms worst via the existing `convertToWav` helper for webm — well inside the STT window, and it reuses that helper rather than adding a second decoder. Note `ensureCompatibleFormat` normally passes webm/mp4/m4a/ogg through UNCONVERTED, so the ffmpeg path is the common one; there is no PCM lying around to reuse.

**The cause label is now recorded for everyone; the diagnostic files are not.** Cause classification moved OUT of the `isPilotCaptureUser` branch at all three nocatch exits (`unsupported_language`; `empty_audio_or_silence` / `dual_pass_uncorroborated`; `latin_low_sim` / `no_match_after_bridge` / `script_mismatch`). Only the LABEL is ungated. The transcript-bearing sidecars in `src/lib/nocatchDiagnostics.ts` stay on their existing allowlist, untouched. **Privacy position, checked against `docs/specs/voice-data-program.md` while building:** that spec governs RAW RECORDINGS (consent, retention, deletion, access to audio) and is silent on derived measurements; this change retains no audio and no new transcript content, and the attempts table already stores transcripts for all learners, so a dB number and a cause label introduce no new category of data.

**Platform had to be invented, and it reuses `flags`.** Nothing in the codebase recorded a client platform, and the plan forbids client changes, so `src/lib/clientPlatform.ts` buckets the User-Agent of the `/attempts` request (same device, seconds later) into `ios_app | android_app | ios_web | android_web | web | unknown` and appends it to the existing comma-separated `flags` column as `platform:<label>` — no new column, and `latency_missing` keeps its exact prior value. An unrecognised or absent UA adds no tag.

**The reader is `pnpm --filter @workspace/scripts run noise-baseline`** (`scripts/src/noiseBaseline.ts`, `--days=N`, `--languages=N`). It prints window + instrumentation coverage, failure rate, cause breakdown (with a "not recorded" bucket), the SNR distribution, failure rate by noise bucket, and splits by platform and language. It is useful BEFORE the columns fill — band and disagreement history answer immediately. Two things it must keep doing: excluding the games routes' phantom attempts (`native_script <> ''`, else every rate is diluted), and reading platform out of `flags` via `substring(flags from 'platform:([a-z_]+)')`. Baseline at ship time (dev data, 164 spoken attempts): 9.8% failed to score, 25.6% dual-pass disagreement, hi 14.4% vs gu 4.2%.

## 10ad. Noise adaptation, cut to what the bench supports: explicit web mic processing and a higher mobile bitrate (August 11, 2026, web + mobile)

**The plan was an eleven-step automatic noise-adaptation feature; the bench had already killed most of it, so it shipped as two changes.** Task 1029 asked for server-side cleanup of noisy clips, a client rolling room estimate with hysteresis, adaptive capture on web and Android, a bitrate lever gated on that estimate, an extreme-noise nudge, and a fairness rule. It also said, in the plan's own words, to stop and raise it if the bench found cleanup did not help. It had (§10ac). What survived the owner's ruling: the web microphone-constraints fix and the mobile bitrate. **Dropped, with reasons worth keeping** — cleanup (no chain reproduced a gain; the only replicating effect was harm); the room estimate and everything gated on it (no benched threshold exists, and the bench's finding is "detect babble, not loudness", which is all a client meter can see, so building it meant inventing the constants the plan forbade); the extreme-noise nudge (same loudness gate, same absence of signal); and the fairness rule.

**Why the fairness rule was dropped, since it looks obviously fair.** Dual-pass disagreement is the best compromised-recording signal available, but on CLEAN audio the two passes already disagree **44%** of the time (50% broadband half / 38% babble half; 25.6% in the production baseline). And disagreement only separates from that clean baseline under BABBLE (38% → 90%); under broadband it never leaves the 33–58% band at any level, including 0 dB. The only gate available is `measureAttemptSnrDb`, a loudness ratio, which cannot tell babble from broadband — so "noisy AND disagreeing" would have fired on ordinary attempts carrying no signal. The second reason is decisive on its own: **a no-fault miss already awards zero XP, and zero XP is what drives the encore strike counter** (§10z, `xpAwarded > 0` on both platforms, band never consulted). Converting a partial-credit attempt into a no-fault miss therefore converts a no-strike outcome into a strike, and three of them eject the phrase from the session. The rule would have punished the learner it meant to protect. No code changed for this; the existing nocatch → zero-XP → strike → release-after-three behaviour is untouched and still pinned by `practice-zero-xp-encore.test.tsx` on both platforms, whose third zero is a nocatch.

**Web: the microphone is now requested with explicit processing settings.** `useVoiceRecorder` asked for `{ audio: true }`, which leaves the choice to the browser, and every major browser enables noise suppression and automatic gain control by default. Both fight the scoring path: suppression smears the aspiration and fricative energy the rubric reads, and AGC rides the level between words, lifting the very room tone the server profiles from each clip's silent opening. The hook now exports `SPEECH_AUDIO_CONSTRAINTS` — `noiseSuppression: false, autoGainControl: false, echoCancellation: TRUE` — and uses it at all three acquisition sites. **Echo cancellation is deliberately kept on**: it subtracts a KNOWN playback reference (the coach's own voice through the speakers) rather than guessing at the learner's audio, practice lets learners barge in while the coach is talking, and speaker bleed is what invalidated most of the pilot corpus. Bare constraint values are "ideal" per the Media Capture spec, so a browser lacking one ignores it instead of failing acquisition. This was a standing defect independent of noise adaptation — quiet-room learners were getting worse audio than they should have — and it is unconditional, because the bench found no room condition in which extra processing earns its place.

**Mobile: 96 kbps, unconditionally, and the reason it reaches iOS.** `RECORDING_PRESET` went 32 → 96 kbps. Applied unconditionally rather than gated on a room estimate, since no such estimate exists in this scope and the bench measured +2.4 on clean audio, i.e. nothing to protect a quiet room from. The trap: `bitRate` must stay a top-level preset field, because expo-audio's `createRecordingOptions` spreads the common options first and the platform block second — `RecordingPresets.HIGH_QUALITY.ios` happens to carry no `bitRate`, which is the only reason the value reaches `AVEncoderBitRateKey`. `__tests__/recording-preset.test.ts` drives that real flattener rather than restating the rule. **Read the whole change against the ±3.8-point floor** (§10ac): it is a cheap directional bet on the owner's ruling, not a proven win, and the confirming ~250-clip run and the before/after production comparison both remain undone (§8).

## 10ae. A refused mend says which refusal it was (August 11, 2026, web + mobile)

**The home streak-repair banner answered every refusal with one sentence, and it was the wrong one.** Web said "Couldn't mend right now. Open the Chai wallet to try again." and mobile said "Couldn't mend right now. Try from the Chai wallet." — for all four server refusals, including the common one, an empty pocket. That copy sends a learner who cannot afford the mend to the wallet, where the same button refuses them a second time with no new information. The server has always been specific here: `POST /tokens/repair-streak` answers 409 with `insufficient_tokens` (plus `balance` and `cost`), `repair_window_expired`, `break_too_long`, or `no_break_to_repair`, and the Chai wallet's own repair row already spoke all four. Only the home banner threw the code away.

**One mapper per platform, shared by both surfaces.** `artifacts/gujarati-coach/src/lib/chai-errors.ts` and `artifacts/bolo-mobile/lib/chai-errors.ts` export `repairErrorMessage(error)`, moved out of the two wallet components (which now import it) and adopted by the two home banners. The sentences are word-for-word across platforms. The empty-pocket line changed while it moved: it is now "Not enough Chai to mend. You have N, mending costs M. Keep practicing to earn more." — it names the gap, and it points at the only way to close it today. The generic spend mapper (`spendErrorMessage`, still local to each wallet) is untouched and keeps its "this costs" phrasing. RULE: a 409 refusal is never a paywall moment — never map one to 402 copy or a Plus upsell.

**Where the purchase screen plugs in.** When in-app Chai purchases exist, the `insufficient_tokens` branch is the entry point: it already carries the balance and the cost, which is exactly what a purchase screen needs to open pre-filled on the shortfall. Marked in both files.

**A second, quieter bug: the web notice never expired.** The banner renders the notice INSTEAD of the offer row, so while a notice is up there is no Mend button. Mobile cleared it after 4s; web never cleared it at all, so a refused learner lost the button until the page remounted — including the learner who is 3 Chai short and will have them within the hour. Web now mirrors mobile's 4s expiry. Pinned by `home-stats-banner.test.tsx` ("the notice expires and hands the Mend button back") and by the twin in `home-quiz-gate.test.tsx`.

**Test note worth keeping.** The mobile home mock is a full replacement of `@workspace/api-client-react`, so it has to export its own `ApiError` class or `instanceof` fails and every refusal falls through to the generic line — a green-looking test that proves nothing. Web's shared `api-client-mock` passes non-hook exports through, so it gets the real class for free.


## 10af. The two result buttons hold their places, and advancing has to be earned (August 11, 2026, web + mobile)

**STANDING RULING — supersedes the Aug 2 revert recorded above. Do not revert this a third time.** On the practice result card the two actions used to swap sides: band `retry` put "Next phrase" left and "Try again" right as the filled primary; every other band put them the other way round. Both buttons are equal halves of the row, so the swap was invisible — the learner tapped the same pixels and got the opposite action. The owner hit this in production on web (tapped the blue right-hand button expecting another go, and the app moved on). The fix shipped once (Aug 2) and was reverted the same day along with the order assertion that would have caught the regression. The owner overruled that revert.

**Position is constant, emphasis is not.** "Try again" is ALWAYS the left slot and "Next phrase"/"Finish" ALWAYS the right one — every band, every state, both platforms, including test-out, error, and the ear-training compare stage. Labels are constant too: the left slot always reads "Try again" (the mobile unlabeled icon-retry is gone), the right always "Next phrase", reading "Finish" only on the last stop of a run. Which slot carries the filled treatment still moves with the band: after a weak take the retry is primary, after a good one the advance is. One shared component per platform renders the row — web `ResultActions` in `src/pages/practice.tsx` (review is the same component under `mode="review"`), mobile `components/ResultActions.tsx` (used by both `app/(app)/practice/[id].tsx` and `app/(app)/review.tsx`). Both render a `result-actions` row containing `try-again-button` then `advance-button`. The single-primary rule: the retry is primary when it is active AND (the card is an error card OR the band is below good); otherwise the advance is.

**The soft advance gate.** The advance is rendered but inactive until the learner scores good-or-better, or has made 3 attempts on that phrase — on the third go it opens whatever the band, so a broken mic or a brutal phrase can never strand anyone (three no-catches open it too). Attempts count regardless of band. Inactive means disabled and dimmed (`aria-disabled` on web, `accessibilityState.disabled` on mobile), no press, no explanatory toast. Exemptions: test-out is ungated and its RETRY slot is the inactive one (one take per phrase is a server-side batch rule); the error card's advance is always inactive (no band, no token, nothing to advance from) and its retry is the primary recovery action; the compare/ear-training stage is ungated because no band is ever produced there. Capture mode is untouched — it never renders the result card.

**The attempt tally rides in the encore map, not on the server.** The existing per-phrase zero-strike map was widened to `{ attempts, zeroStrikes }` and both counters are incremented at the one existing write site (web keys by `phrase.id`, mobile by list index — deliberately NOT harmonised here). The server's `Phrase.attemptCount` is cumulative across past sessions and stale mid-session; using it would open the gate on attempt 1 for a returning learner. Encore carry-over is deliberate: a phrase returning in the encore keeps its attempt count, because it has already cost three goes.

**Why the two 3s cannot collide.** The encore's `ZERO_XP_STRIKE_LIMIT` counts only zero-XP attempts (bands `retry` and `nocatch`); the gate counts every attempt. Strikes are therefore always <= attempts, so the encore can never release a phrase while the gate is still shut. On an all-weak run both fire on the same render and reinforce each other. The only divergence is `almost` (earns half XP so no strike, below good so no gate opening) — harmless, and the reason tests that need a phrase to stay queued past three goes use a ZERO -> half-credit -> ZERO sequence.

**Test consequences worth knowing before you write one.** (1) The band ladder's bottom rung ALSO reads "Try again", so a text query for it is ambiguous — address the controls by testID (`try-again-button` / `advance-button`) or scope the text query to the row. (2) Order is pinned with real order assertions, not presence: `compareDocumentPosition` on web, an ordered child query on the `result-actions` container on mobile (RNTL has no DOM-order primitive), across every band on both platforms. (3) Any test that scores weakly and then advances must now either score good, take three goes, or use the deliberately ungated phrase-strip chevron — walking past a weak take is exactly what the gate stops. (4) `describeEvaluationError.action` is no longer passed to the button (the varied wording stays in the card body).

## 10af. Chai packs: buying Chai with money, shipped dark (August 11, 2026, web only)

**Web only, and deliberately so.** Chai packs are sold through Stripe Checkout on `gujarati-coach`. Mobile is untouched: not a line of `bolo-mobile` changed, and NOTHING in the iOS app may mention or link to web purchases (App Store rules). iOS packs will be StoreKit consumables in a later build.

**One flag, and it gates the SURFACE only.** `CHAI_PACKS_LIVE` in `artifacts/gujarati-coach/src/components/chai-packs.tsx`, shaped exactly like `APP_STORE_LIVE` in `components/app-store-badge.tsx`: an exported constant, overridable per render by a `live` prop so tests exercise both states. It is `false` today, so `ChaiPackShop` renders nothing and the Chai wallet looks unchanged. What it does NOT gate: the pack catalog, `POST /api/stripe/chai-checkout`, the Stripe session, the webhook credit, or the ledger write. All of that is live and tested, so flipping the flag is a display change rather than the first run of untried code. The `?chai=` return banner (`ChaiPurchaseReturn`, mounted in `pages/home.tsx`) is deliberately ungated too — a purchase in flight when the flag flips off must still be acknowledged.

**The ladder, and the no-literals rule.** 50 Chai / $1.99, 150 / $4.99, 400 / $9.99, defined once in `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/chaiPacks.ts` (`CHAI_PACKS`) and seeded into Stripe by `scripts/seedStripeProducts.ts` (now also creates one-time, non-recurring prices — `findOrCreateOneTimePrice`; the original helper assumes `recurring`). Nothing inlines a pack size or price at a call site. The `cents` field EXISTS ONLY TO SEED STRIPE: what a learner is shown and charged is the live Stripe amount, read through `lib/pricingCatalog.ts` (new `packs` key, mirrored by web `lib/pricing.ts` `buildPackOffers`) exactly like a plan price. Price ids come from `STRIPE_CHAI_PACK_{SMALL,MEDIUM,LARGE}_PRICE_ID` via `getChaiPackPriceId` (`lib/stripePricing.ts`); an unconfigured pack is simply not offered, and the checkout route answers 503 rather than guessing. Packs alone never satisfy the "no Stripe price ids configured" check — the paywall is what that guard protects.

**The webhook is the only thing that credits.** `POST /stripe/chai-checkout` creates a `mode: "payment"` session with metadata `{ kind: "chai_pack", userId, packId }` on both the session and the PaymentIntent, and credits nothing. `middlewares/stripeWebhook.ts` gained a `checkout.session.completed` case that runs `chaiPackCreditFromSession` (a pure, exported, separately tested mapper) and then `creditChaiPack`. Three guards, each load-bearing: the session must be ours by `kind` (a subscription checkout raises the same event), it must be `payment_status === "paid"` (async payment methods complete a session before the money lands), and the pack id must resolve. **The Chai granted is read off the catalog by pack id, never from anything the client or the metadata states.** Because the browser is not involved, a learner who closes the tab the instant Stripe charges them still gets their Chai.

**refId = the Stripe transaction id, and ONLY the PaymentIntent id.** The ledger row is `(userId, "purchase_chai_pack", <payment_intent id>)`. A payment-mode session carrying no intent is refused rather than keyed on the session id — the review round caught that a session-id fallback would let two deliveries of the same payment disagree about the key and credit the pack twice. The ledger's unique index is the entire idempotency story: a Stripe retry, a duplicate delivery, a redelivery after our own 500-for-retry, and an out-of-order event all carry the same id, so the second insert conflicts and credits nothing. `grantTokensDetailed` reports whether THIS delivery was the one that credited; the webhook logs it either way.

**No automatic refund clawback, and a zero floor.** A Stripe refund does not reverse Chai — it may already be spent, and un-spending it would mean un-buying a stop or an outfit in use. A reversal is a deliberate act: `applyChaiAdjustment(userId, refId, delta)` in `lib/tokenService.ts` (reason `adjust_manual`), which locks the state row before reading the balance and floors at zero, so a reversal larger than the balance lands on 0 and a learner can never "owe" Chai (every affordability check in the app compares against `balance`). The ledger still records the full delta the owner asked for; `balanceAfter` records what happened. Idempotent by refId. There is deliberately NO route for it — it is a console primitive.

**The monthly All-Access allowance dropped 50 → 15** (`TOKEN_ALLOWANCE_ALL_ACCESS_MONTHLY`, `lib/tokenEconomy.ts`). Server-side and granted through the ledger, so one constant covers web and mobile with no client release. The refId is the UTC month, so a month already granted at 50 keeps it and the new amount starts next month. The number appears in no user-facing copy on either platform (audited), so no copy changed.

**Stripe account note.** `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` is a LIVE key, so the seeder created three real live products/prices. They are inert while the flag is off, and the three price ids are shared (non-secret) env vars, set alongside the existing `STRIPE_PLUS_*`/`STRIPE_FAMILY_*` ids.


## 10ag. The web suite's three standing failures were two stale fixtures, and the "slow tests" were file wall time (August 11, 2026, web tests only)

**Neither timeout existed.** The two cases carried into this task as load-dependent timeouts were neither timeouts nor load-dependent, and no timeout value moved. `games-romanized.test.tsx` fails **one run in four on a fair coin**: Luggage Match `quickShuffle`s the topic pool and plays only the first six of the eight fixture phrases, so `getByText("n1")` was naming a phrase the board deals out 75% of the time. Reproduced 3 green / 2 red in five solo runs, which is also why a full-suite run sometimes shows it and sometimes does not — nothing to do with contention. The two Luggage Match assertions now assert over whatever the board dealt (`getAllByText(/^n\d+$/)`, each tag's own `r{n}` beneath it), which is both deterministic and closer to the test's own name; eight consecutive runs green. `practice-streak-xp.test.tsx`'s UNSTOPPABLE test measured **784 ms under full-suite load** and **1.4 s under 8-way CPU oversubscription**, against an 8 s `waitFor` ceiling and a 30 s per-test override — never within an order of magnitude of either. The quoted "7.9 s solo / 15.8 s under load" are the FILE's wall time (8.4 s solo, 13 s under burner load), which is transform + import + jsdom environment + 18 tests, not that test. Its ten scoring cycles cost ~41 ms each (derived from the 5-cycle and 10-cycle tests in the same file), spent re-rendering the practice page; no real-timer constant is reachable, because these tests run with `bolo.silentMode` on and the meaning segment off, so neither the 8 s `FEEDBACK_AUDIO_TIMEOUT_MS` guard nor the meaning pause is ever armed. **Nothing was changed in that file.**

**The games-hub failure was a stale fixture, and the app is right.** The hub renders vocabulary → listening → building; the fixture still listed listening first. That reorder is deliberate and documented at the source (`pages/games/index.tsx`: "Vocabulary leads so Luggage Match … appears in the top-left slot — matching the mobile hub's ordering", commit 9d94f65). Both failures were the same staleness seen twice — the hardcoded three-group array and `GRID_IDS`, which is derived by flattening `GROUPS`. The fixture was reordered; the app was not touched.

**Suite cost, measured.** 83 files / 671 tests, **~43–47 s end to end** on this machine (`pool: 'forks'`, `maxForks: 2`), unchanged by this work. The three slowest files are **`script-trace-scoring.test.ts` at ~40 s**, `speed-round-combo.test.tsx` at ~11 s, and `practice-streak-xp.test.tsx` at ~6–7 s. The first number is the one that matters: a single file holds a worker for ~40 s of a ~43 s run, so the whole suite is now that one file's runtime plus scheduling, and every other file is free. Suite wall time cannot fall below it, and shaving anything else changes nothing until it does.

## 10ah. A broken reading is not a farther reading (August 12, 2026, server only)

**The anti-flattery rule was scoring recognizer failures.** Every attempt runs two STT passes, and when they disagree the route scores the transcript FARTHER from the target so a pass that normalizes a wrong pronunciation into the target cannot win. The distance ladder in `chooseConservativeTranscript` (`artifacts/api-server/src/lib/pronunciationGuards.ts`) ranks a NON-COMPARABLE transcript (wrong script, `-1`) below every comparable similarity, so whenever the mini pass drifted into Cyrillic or Perso-Arabic while the high-quality pass read the learner correctly, the garbled pass won, `compareToTarget` found no comparable space, and a correct attempt died as `script_mismatch` → nocatch.

**Owner ruling (Aug 12, 2026): exactly one non-comparable pass is a glitch, not distance.** When both passes carry content and exactly one of them is comparable, the comparable pass is taken and scored normally. A wrong-script transcript is not a kinder reading of the learner's speech, it is a broken one, so excluding it does not weaken the rule. Everything else is byte-for-byte unchanged: two comparable transcripts that disagree still score the farther one (the case the rule exists for), two non-comparable passes still resolve as before, and an empty pass is still the farthest rung with its own uncorroborated system-miss path — an empty pass is never a glitch rescue.

**Measured before the change, from 170 production attempts carrying both transcripts.** Exactly-one-non-comparable fired on **14 of 170 (8.2%)** and **every one of the 14 landed nocatch**; in 9 of them the surviving pass matched the target exactly (sim 1.000), i.e. a perfect attempt thrown away. It was ALWAYS the mini pass that broke (14/14) — the high-quality pass never drifted. Gujarati is worse but this is not a Gujarati-only fault: **11/106 gu (10.4%)** against **3/64 hi (4.7%)**. Both languages' drift went to Cyrillic, Perso-Arabic, Gurmukhi and once Japanese kana.

**The rescue is countable afterwards without a new store.** `DualPassChoice.glitchRescue` rides the signed evaluation token as the optional claim `sttGlitchRescue`, and `buildAttemptFlags` (`lib/clientPlatform.ts`) turns it into the `stt_glitch_rescue` tag inside the attempt row's existing `flags` column, alongside `latency_missing` and `platform:*`. No schema change, no new sidecar. A distinct log line ("STT recognizer glitch: one pass was unverifiable script") makes the rate greppable for non-allowlisted learners too. Tokens issued before the claim existed simply omit it and record no tag.

**Known gap, deliberately left.** On the `script_mismatch` nocatch path the diagnostic sidecar records `rawSim`, `bridgedSim` and `finalSim` as null — by construction, since an incomparable pair has no similarity to record. The sidecar shape was not changed here; the honest fix is to record the OTHER pass's similarity, which belongs to whatever task next touches the diagnostics shape.


## 10ai. Review carries the lesson audio menu, minus the item it has no code for (August 12, 2026, bolo-mobile only)

**Review is a practice screen, so it now has the practice header's controls.** `artifacts/bolo-mobile/app/(app)/review.tsx` gained a rightmost settings gear and a display-only language chip, both from the new shared component `artifacts/bolo-mobile/components/LessonSettingsMenu.tsx` (exports `LessonSettingsMenu`, `LanguageChip`, `LessonSettingsItem`). The component is a bottom-sheet `Modal` on the house pattern — `transparent` + `onRequestClose` (Android back), a full-screen backdrop `Pressable` that closes, an inner `Pressable` with a no-op `onPress` to swallow sheet taps, and each item closing the sheet on select. It holds NO preference state of its own: every item's `enabled` and `onToggle` are supplied by the screen, so the menu can never drift from the control it mirrors.

**The menu had exactly two items on review — `Autoplay phrase` and `Spoken feedback` — with `Speak meaning` absent, not disabled. SUPERSEDED Aug 12, 2026 by #1046, which gave review its own meaning segment and therefore the third item; the paragraph below is otherwise unchanged and still current.** The three label strings are owner-approved verbatim and live in one place, `LESSON_AUDIO_LABELS` in the shared component; a screen passes them through and never writes its own string, so the two lesson screens cannot word the same control differently. The two-item ruling rested on review having no meaning-audio code path at all, so a greyed-out or stand-in item would have advertised a control that did not exist; once the code path landed, the item became real on the same terms as practice's. `Phrase` is backed by a new `silentModeUI` state mirroring the `bolo.silentMode` preference the screen was already reading fresh at all three coach-autoplay sites, so a flip applies from the very next phrase without restarting the session — review previously honoured that preference with no way to set it from the screen. `Feedback` points at review's existing `spokenEnabled`/`toggleSpokenFeedback`, which is the same value the result-card mute (`spoken-feedback-quick-toggle`) writes, so the two stay in sync in both directions by construction rather than by syncing. The mute button itself is untouched.

**The chip is inert by ruling.** `LanguageChip` renders the uppercased active code in a fixed three-character slot (`minWidth: 40`) so switching between two- and three-letter codes never reflows the header and `sat` is never truncated to `SA` (which reads as Sanskrit). It is a plain `View` + `Text` with `accessible={false}`: no press handler, no button role, not focusable. Only the phrase-card header variant receives the gear and chip; the loading, empty and summary variants keep their 44px spacer, matching how practice treats its own non-lesson header states.

**Menu toggles confirm in the practice screen's words.** Because selecting an item closes the sheet, a flip would otherwise be silent, so both items fire review's existing `MilestoneToast` with a local copy of practice's `TOGGLE_TOAST` strings (same deliberate-duplication pattern as the local `BAND_LABEL` copy in the same file). Mobile suite after the change: **831/831, 93 suites**, including 12 new tests in `__tests__/review-header-settings.test.tsx`.

**Standing note: the practice-side half of this parity was NOT PRESENT in this repl when review shipped.** The practice header still renders loose icon-only toggles, and a search of the full history — all 468 branch tips plus `git log --all -S` — finds the approved label strings in no commit other than this one. The practice work is not merely unmerged into `main-repl/main`; the commits recorded against it do not exist as objects here at all. Until that is resolved, treat review as the reference implementation: when practice adopts it, import `LessonSettingsMenu`/`LanguageChip` and `LESSON_AUDIO_LABELS` rather than build a second construction.

## 10ai. The speaking streak is tracked, not displayed (August 12, 2026, web + mobile, display only)

**Owner ruling: speaking streak does not earn a permanent tile.** The web home stat bar was five cells (Day Streak, Speaking Streak, Total XP, Mastered, Chai) and the mobile Progress-tab grid was five stats; the crowding was the complaint, and the ruling does not stop at a screen boundary, so BOTH went. Web home is now four cells; the mobile Progress grid is four stats, which finally matches the four-card `SkeletonCard` loading state that was already there. The mobile home stat bar never carried it and is untouched.

**Nothing behind the display moved.** `computeSpeakingStreakDays` (`artifacts/api-server/src/lib/progressMetrics.ts`), its `progressMetrics.test.ts` block, the `speakingStreakDays` field on `GET /progress/summary`, and its `openapi.yaml` description are all byte-for-byte unchanged, and no client was regenerated — the response shape did not change. Re-surfacing the number later is a UI-only job. Confirmed at removal time that the field had no other consumer anywhere: not badges, not `streakRepair.ts`, not reminders, analytics, encore, test-out, or any server-side gate.

**The web bar rebalances by flex weight, not by shrinking type.** The progress-figures group (`stats-progress-link`) went `flex-[4]` → `flex-[3]` so the three figures inside it stay the same width as the `flex-1` Chai cell sitting outside the link; drop the weight change and the remaining three would each widen to a third of four units and the bar would come out lopsided. Dividers are one per gap (three now, was four). Entrance-spring delays on the surviving cells are unchanged (0.16 / 0.24 / 0.32 / 0.40 — the removed cell's 0.20 simply leaves a gap in the sequence, which is invisible), as are the reserved-height/`invisible` pending treatment, the link target and the Chai button behaviour. No type size was reduced. `Mic` is no longer imported by `home.tsx`.

**Pinned by:** the three web `home-stats-banner.test.tsx` cases that enumerate the bar now assert four labels plus `queryByText(/speaking streak/i)` null, and a new `progress stat grid` block in `artifacts/bolo-mobile/__tests__/progress-history-retake.test.tsx` mounts the Progress screen with a summary that DOES carry `speakingStreakDays` and asserts the four labels render, the label is absent, the value never appears, and only one `mic` icon remains in the grid.

## 10ai. Home leads with the pass, and asks for a friend at the bottom (August 12, 2026, web + mobile)

**Order of intent on home is practise → progress → spend.** Chacha-ji's stall used to render directly above the boarding pass inside the shared entrance wrapper on both platforms, which put a spend surface ahead of the primary "start practising" action. The two swapped places inside that same wrapper — one entrance animation, same idle-breathe on the ticket alone, same tear hand-off, same wallet sheet behind the stall. Nothing else on home moved. Order is now pinned by a test on each platform (`home-stats-banner.test.tsx` compares document position of `journey-pass-card` vs `chai-stall-vignette`; the mobile twin in `__tests__/home-quiz-gate.test.tsx` walks the rendered tree collecting testIDs in order — `JSON.stringify(screen.toJSON())` throws on the circular fiber, so the walk is the way).

**One module builds every referral link, on both platforms.** `lib/referral-link` is a new private workspace package shaped like `@workspace/api-client-react` (`type: module`, exports the TypeScript source, no build step and therefore no stale-`dist` trap for either Vite or Metro). It owns code normalization, the `/join/<code>` path, link construction from a **caller-supplied origin plus base path**, and `REFERRAL_REWARD_CHAI`. Web's `src/lib/referral-code.ts` kept its call sites but now re-exports the shared constant and delegates link building; mobile's new `lib/referral.ts` follows the `lib/legal.ts` precedent — read `EXPO_PUBLIC_DOMAIN` once at module load, return `undefined` when it is absent. No second place in the repo constructs a `/join/` URL.

**The hand-mirrored reward constant is now guarded, closing the 10b-2 debt item.** `sharedConstants.contract.test.ts` reads `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/tokenEconomy.ts` off disk and asserts both `REFERRAL_REWARD_REFERRER_CHAI` and `REFERRAL_REWARD_REFEREE_CHAI` equal the shared `REFERRAL_REWARD_CHAI`. Home copy renders that constant, never a literal, so the copy cannot advertise a number the ledger will not pay; moving the server number without moving the shared one fails the web suite.

**The home card is deliberately not the settings card.** Gift icon, "Invite a friend, earn Chai", "You both get {N} Chai when they finish their first practice.", one "Share invite" button — no code, no URL text, no Joined / Pending / Chai earned row, no Copy link. Those omissions are pinned by test on both platforms. It renders nothing at all while the referral query is loading or failed, and mobile renders nothing when no web domain is configured (same graceful absence as the privacy link). The settings card is untouched visually and behaviourally; its share/copy internals were lifted into `src/lib/referral-share.ts` so the two cards share one share invocation instead of duplicating it, and `referral.test.tsx` pins Copy link → "Copied!", Share → sheet, and Share-without-a-sheet → clipboard fallback.
