**Two test-double traps met here.** `userEvent.setup()` installs its own `navigator.clipboard`, displacing a stubbed one — clipboard assertions have to go through `fireEvent`. And on mobile, `jest.isolateModules` re-requiring a component yields a SECOND React instance with a null dispatcher, so the no-domain case lives in its own test file with its own module-level env, not in an isolated block.

## 10aj. The lesson header's audio controls moved behind a gear, and the header finally says which language (August 12, 2026, web + mobile, client only)

**Three loose pills became one gear menu, and the two platforms now present the same three items.** Web's practice header carried Phrase / Feedback / Meaning as three labeled `aria-pressed` pills; mobile carried only two of them in the header, icon-only, with Feedback stranded on the result card. Both now show a single settings gear as the rightmost header control, and all three items live inside it with **text labels** — `Autoplay phrase`, `Spoken feedback`, `Speak meaning` — plus an explicit `On`/`Off` word and a check indicator. Owner ruling: the labels ARE the feature (icon-only was rejected), and mobile gets text labels here for the first time. A second ruling settled the wording after checking each handler: all three are genuine persistent preferences (each one `load*`s at mount and `save*`s on flip — `bolo.silentMode`, `bolo.spokenFeedback`, `bolo.meaningAudio`), not actions, so all three legitimately carry a checkmark and an On/Off word. `Autoplay phrase` says autoplay because turning it off does not remove the ability to hear the phrase — the learner taps "Hear it" instead — and `Play phrase audio` read as an action button. `Speak meaning` beats `Show meaning` because the translation text is always visible on the card; only the audio is being gated, and "show" would imply the toggle hides the text. Nothing about what the three controls DO changed: same preference keys (`bolo.silentMode`, `bolo.spokenFeedback`, `bolo.meaningAudio`), same `TOGGLE_TOAST` confirmations, same Meaning-disabled-while-coach-voice-off rule. Placement only.

**Reused primitives, no new dependency.** Web uses the shadcn `dropdown-menu.tsx` that had been sitting in `components/ui` unimported (its Radix dep was already declared) — `DropdownMenuCheckboxItem` gives `role="menuitemcheckbox"` + `aria-checked` + `data-disabled`, and outside-click / Escape / close-on-select for free. Mobile has no menu, sheet, or popover component and no bottom-sheet dependency, so the house pattern applies: a React Native `Modal` styled as a bottom sheet (same shape as the chat language picker and `PhraseReportButton`), where `onRequestClose` is what wires the Android back gesture.

**Spoken feedback has two doors and one state.** The mobile result-card mute (`spoken-feedback-quick-toggle`) is a moment-of-playback control and STAYS where it is; the menu's Feedback item is a second entry point onto the same `spokenEnabled` / `toggleSpokenFeedback` pair passed down into `PracticeHeader`. Neither holds a copy — `practice-spoken-feedback.test.tsx` drives each door and reads the other.

**The language chip is inert by ruling, and its slot is fixed at three characters.** Immediately left of the gear, the active code renders uppercased with **no press handler, no button role, and no focusability** — the language cannot be changed mid-lesson, and this must not become a second language switcher. Codes are NEVER truncated: six of the 22 are three letters (`brx`, `doi`, `kok`, `mai`, `mni`, `sat`) and `sat` clipped to `SA` collides with Sanskrit. The slot is a fixed width (web `w-9`, mobile 40pt) so switching between a two- and three-letter language never reflows the row. Web adds an `sr-only` "— practising <language>" so the code is not the only cue; the chip is deliberately NOT `aria-hidden`. Web `/review` renders this same header and therefore inherits both the gear and the chip — intended, review is a practice screen. Mobile review is a separate screen, left for a follow-on. The practice loading / empty-state header variant passes no toggles and stays bare: no gear, no chip.

**The 320px probe was re-aimed, not retired.** `qa/header-pills-320-probe.mjs` used to assert exactly three `header button[aria-pressed]` pills; there are none now. It measures the chip + gear (with the chip forced to a three-character `SAT`, the worst case) and asserts zero stray pills, then opens the menu and measures the three items. All PASS at 320 light/dark/normal/large-text and 375 light: chip right edge 264/320 normal, 250/320 at 125% root text — the pair is markedly narrower than the three pills it replaced, so this fit got easier, not tighter. Mobile shots via the new `qa/task1044-header-shots.mjs` (Expo web, 412×824@2x, provisions its own `+clerk_test` user through the Clerk Backend API because the email-code path needs an account that already exists, and deep-links to `/practice/1` — the home hero's entrance animation never settles enough for a stable Playwright click).

**Test traps this created.** Radix opens its menu on **pointerdown**, not click, so every jsdom helper is `fireEvent.pointerDown(trigger, { button: 0, ctrlKey: false, pointerType: "mouse" })`; and because the menu closes on select, each item press is open-then-click-then-wait-for-close. Two tests in `practice-band-audio.test.tsx` read the Meaning control by `getByTitle(/meaning aloud/i)` — the pill's `title` is gone, so they read the menu item's `data-disabled` instead. **Toast assertion ORDER is load-critical and was got wrong once:** the confirmation toast self-dismisses after 1800ms of REAL time, and `MilestoneToast` uses `AnimatePresence mode="wait"`, so a replacing toast enters only after the previous one's exit animation — it can be neither read synchronously (you get the OLD message) nor waited for at leisure. Putting the close-the-menu `waitFor` between the click and the toast assertion burnt the whole dwell window inside the helper, and `practice-toggle-toasts.test.tsx` passed solo but failed under a concurrent three-suite validation run. The toast assertion must sit immediately after the click, with the menu-close wait after it — which is the adjacency the pre-menu tests had for free. `qa/task1003-meaning-probe.mjs` still drives the retired Meaning pill by `aria-pressed` and is now stale; it was left untouched (out of scope) and needs re-aiming before its next run. Suites after the change: web **707 / 85 files** green, mobile **829 / 92** green.

## 10ai. A topic has a ceiling, a tap has a lock, and the daily meter is gone (August 12, 2026, api + web + mobile)

**Two defects, one surface.** "Add more phrases" ran with no concurrency control and no ceiling: two taps (or a tap racing a background top-up) generated against the same phrase snapshot, so each batch passed its own duplicate check and each insert duplicated the other's rows. Separately, the only cost guard on manual appends was the retired daily lesson meter, which returns null for everyone, so a Free learner could append a topic without limit.

**Manual appends now take the SAME advisory lock the background replenisher uses** (`topicLockKey(lang, categoryId)` with the shared default prefix `phrase-replenish`, both exported from `lib/phraseReplenisher.ts`). The lock is a session-level `pg_try_advisory_lock` held on a dedicated pooled client and released in `finally`; the loser of the race gets 409 `append_in_progress` rather than waiting, because the winner's phrases arrive on the next refetch anyway. The snapshot of existing phrases is read INSIDE the lock, so no batch can be generated against a stale view. **Every writer to a topic now shares that one lock.** The Free background path used to pass its own prefix `phrase-replenish-free`, which meant a Free top-up and a manual append could still generate against the same snapshot, so the prefix override is gone from both call sites and survives only as a test seam. The startup regroup backfill still holds its own lock, which is why the insert-time group assignment keeps its retry-then-unassigned fallback.

**Ceilings are per plan and counted in VISIBLE rows** (`lib/phraseCeilings.ts`): 20 for Free and One Language (One Language buys languages, not a bigger library), 60 for All-Access. Family needs no entry because family membership resolves to plan `plus` per request and inherits 60. The counting basis is the important part: a Free learner's topic can hold 40 rows of which 8 are visible, so counting raw rows refused every Free append on a live topic. `countVisiblePhrases` counts `stage='phrase'` rows the caller can actually see (premium rows only for `plus`), and the background replenisher now takes the same `plan` option so the paths cannot disagree about how full a topic is. Background top-ups CLAMP to the remaining headroom exactly as manual appends do, and the Plus path passes its 60 ceiling too: a ceiling a background writer can overshoot is not a ceiling, and the clients now show the number.

**Near the boundary the request is CLAMPED, not refused.** A learner with 2 rows of headroom asking for 5 gets 2. Only zero headroom refuses, and the refusal splits by whether an upgrade would help: 402 `phrase_ceiling` (a new `UpgradeReason`) when it would, 409 `topic_full` on All-Access and Family when nothing would, so the top tier is never shown an upgrade prompt for a ceiling it already paid to lift. A separate bound caps 10 manual appends per rolling hour per user on every tier (`MANUAL_APPENDS_PER_HOUR`, 429 `append_rate_limited` with `retryAfterSeconds`), counted across all topics and languages so switching topics does not reset it. That bound is a READ, so it runs under a second advisory lock keyed on the learner (`phrase-append-user:<userId>`), taken before the topic lock and always in that order so the two cannot deadlock. Without it a learner could tap several topics at once, have every request count the same nine rows, and pay for every one of them; a concurrent second tap now gets 409 `append_in_progress`. Every refusal is evaluated before any AI call, so a blocked tap costs nothing.

**Appends are their own generation kind.** `lesson_generation_kind` gained `manual` (migration 0045), and the replenisher's cooldown query now excludes manual rows. **Migration 0045 is effectively irreversible.** `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` cannot run inside a transaction block, so it is applied on its own outside the usual wrapped batch, and Postgres offers no way to remove an enum value once added. Rolling this back means recreating the type (new type, column swap, drop old) with a hand-written migration, so treat any future addition to this enum as permanent and name values conservatively. Lessons are cached globally per topic, so counting one learner's tap in the cooldown would freeze background top-ups for every other learner on that topic for a full day on the Free cadence. `countLessonGenerationsToday` still counts `initial` rows only.

**The daily meter is deleted, not repointed.** `FREE_DAILY_NEW_LESSON_CAP`, `dailyNewLessonLimit` and `dailyLessonCapDenial` are gone from the server. Two things were kept on purpose: the `dailyNewLessons` wire field still emits a literal null for installed builds that read it, and `daily_lesson_limit` stays in the `UpgradeReason` union annotated as retired and never server-emitted, because both clients still pass that exact string as a paywall deep-link param to lead with the free-trial CTA.

**Clients read the ceiling from the server, never a constant.** `/categories` now carries `phraseCeiling` alongside `phraseCount` (which is already the visible count, so no second field was added). At the ceiling both platforms hide the add button in favour of a "topic is full" line, and the web error block is three-way: upgrade link on 402, the server's refusal message with no upgrade prompt on 409/429, generic retry otherwise. The false "check back tomorrow" copy is gone from both platforms, along with a mobile code comment that described the same retired cadence.


## 10aj. A topic cannot hold the same phrase twice, and the library was never the cause (August 12, 2026, db + api)

**Production was clean; only the dev database held duplicates.** The five pairs the plan enumerated (bn/6, brx/3, brx/6, sa/4, ur/1) exist in dev and in dev only — production has zero normalized duplicates in either stage, checked before and after the work. Nothing was deleted in production and nothing needed to be.

**The stated cause was wrong, and the correction matters for the survivor choice.** These are NOT append-race duplicates. Curated seed top-ups de-duplicate on native script AND English gloss, so when a curated gloss was corrected the next boot read the pair as new content and inserted a SECOND row carrying the same native text. The fingerprint is visible in dev's `brx` numbers lesson, which holds both the old shuffled numerals and the corrected ones. In every one of the five pairs, exactly one copy matches the CURRENT curated library, and it is always the higher-id row. Deleting the library-matching copy would have been undone by the very next seed run — and would then have crashed against the new index.

**Survivor rule: most learner history wins, highest id breaks the tie** — expressed generically in SQL, not as a list of ids, so the same migration is a safe no-op anywhere the data differs. None of the ten dev rows carried attempts, reports or scheduler state, so the tie-break decided all five, and it selected the five library-matching rows. Learner history is REPOINTED, never dropped (`attempts.phrase_id` has no FK, so a delete would silently orphan it); `user_item_memory` repoints only where the learner has no row on the survivor. Positions are renumbered contiguously per affected group in a negative-then-flip two-phase update, so ordered playback has no gaps.

**Consequence worth knowing: two topics lost a free row.** For bn/6 and sa/4 the pair straddled the free/premium boundary and the survivor is the PREMIUM copy, so dev's free starter set for those topics went from 10 to 9. That is the correct outcome, not a regression: the free copy was stale pre-correction content, production's free set never contained it, and dev now matches production and the library exactly. Nothing silently JOINED a free set.

**The constraint is a unique index on normalized text** — `phrases_topic_stage_text_unique` on `(language_code, category_id, stage, lower(regexp_replace(btrim(native_script), '\s+', ' ', 'g')))`, added by migration 0046 together with the cleanup DML. Raw-column uniqueness would miss the case and whitespace variants a buggy writer actually produces, which is why the expression mirrors the application's own comparison. **`normalizePhraseText`, the index name, and `isDuplicatePhraseTextError` all live in one place** (`lib/db/src/phraseText.ts`, also exported from the `@workspace/db/phrase-text` subpath so tests can import them without opening a pool). The index expression and that function must be changed together or the database and the application stop agreeing about what a duplicate is.

**Violations are recognised BY CONSTRAINT NAME, never by bare 23505.** The replenisher's insert ladder legitimately depends on catching position and group unique violations to retry; treating every 23505 as "duplicate text" would swallow those. On a text-index violation the background path logs and returns 0 added, and the manual append route answers `200 []` — an AI producing a near-duplicate is expected, not a fault, and the learner tapped that button deliberately.

**Both seeders now check normalized native script per stage as well as the gloss pair** (`seedContent.ts` top-up and `backfillPremiumPhrases.ts`), logging rather than inserting when a curated gloss was corrected. This closes the actual duplicate source, and it means a corrected English gloss will now be SKIPPED rather than applied — applying gloss corrections to already-seeded rows needs its own path (see the correction-pass row in the debt table). The initial lesson build and the sentence insert in `learning.ts` de-duplicate their batch through the same helper before writing.

**Fixture fallout is expected and was fixed, not suppressed.** Two suites seeded several placeholder rows with identical `native_script` inside one topic (`teaserGating`, `openai.pronunciation.gating`); their helpers now derive distinct text per row. Real content never had that shape. `learning.phraseTextUnique.test.ts` covers the index itself (case and whitespace variants rejected; same text in another topic or the other stage still allowed) and both append paths under a racing writer that inserts the phrase after the caller took its snapshot.

## 11. House Patterns

One-line registry of reusable components and patterns. Verify the contract at the path before reuse; paths are under `artifacts/gujarati-coach/src/` unless noted.

- **Quick-game frame**: `pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx`. QuickGameShell owns picker/countdown/rounds/result and the POST; rounds implement `QuickRoundProps` and report via `api.submitRound`, never their own persistence.
- **Game miss review**: `components/game-miss-review.tsx` (+ mobile twin `artifacts/bolo-mobile/components/GameMissReview.tsx`). `GameMiss` records supplied BY THE GAME, `MissReviewCta` (renders nothing at count 0) and the dialog/modal; end screens own the open state and the tappable score card.
- **Branded toast**: `components/ui/milestone-toast.tsx`. Milestone celebration toast used by home and practice; use it instead of raw sonner for branded moments.
- **Chai stall kit**: `components/chai-stall.tsx` (+ mobile twin `artifacts/bolo-mobile/components/ChaiStall.tsx`). `STALL_ASSETS` registry + `KETTLE` fraction layer map; `ChaiGlyph` is Chai's inline mark everywhere an amount is shown, `ChaiStallVignette` is the ambient-steam scene.
- **Layer-map scene kit**: `components/brand-splash.tsx`. `SPLASH_V2_ASSETS` path registry + `useComposedReveal` + ready-signal hold with the `--splash-max-hold` failsafe (a stuck signal can never trap the user).
- **cssTimeMs**: `lib/utils.ts`. Minifier-safe parsing of computed CSS time vars to milliseconds (prod rewrites 8000ms as 8s; bare parseFloat reads 8).
- **FactStrip**: `pages/journey.tsx`. Compact labeled fact row used in journey dialogs.
- **Earn chips**: `pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx` (`chai-earn-beat`) and `pages/practice.tsx` (`session-chai-pill`). Amber earn pills; render only when the server actually granted.
- **StatCell**: `pages/home.tsx`. Uniform stat tile for dashboard grids.
- **Countdown capability**: `pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx` `secondsPerRound` prop. Opt-in 3-2-1 pre-round count plus a timer pill that goes urgent inside the final 2 seconds.
- **Audio-required gate**: `pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx` `requiresAudio` prop (web). For a game whose PROMPT is the clip: entry is refused while sound is off, evaluated once per run entry and never mid-round, cleared by unmuting. A gate must suppress `roundsReady` and the round timer too, or the clock runs behind the message. Not for games that merely speak.
- **Run key**: `artifacts/bolo-mobile/components/games/QuickGameShell.tsx` `runKey` (mobile; web's equivalent is `gameKey` in `pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx`). The round subtree is keyed on a run counter bumped on Play Again AND Choose Topic, so a round holding its own state for a whole run (a persistent board) starts every run clean. A Fragment key, not a wrapper View: the remount adds no layout node. Per-round-derived games are unaffected.
- **Game testIDs**: `{game-id}-{element}`, with `-{idx}` or `-{phraseId}` appended for repeated elements (`wrong-platform-option-0`, `luggage-match-left-10`, `ticket-choice-0`, `word-match-card-3`). Established across the four ported quick games; follow it rather than inventing a per-game scheme.
- **Romanization on game surfaces**: the always-visible-reading ruling still holds everywhere EXCEPT Listen & Pick (both platforms, Aug 12, 2026): the clip is the question there, so choices show native script + English meaning and never `romanized`, otherwise the round is solvable by matching Latin letters to sounds. Pinned by `src/test/listen-and-pick-choices.test.tsx` (web), the choice-card block in `__tests__/listen-and-pick-voice-cache.test.tsx` (mobile), and a carve-out note in `src/test/games-romanized.test.tsx`. Express Listening has the same shape and still shows readings — not yet ruled on.
- **Silent-game declaration**: `artifacts/bolo-mobile/components/games/QuickGameShell.tsx` `usesAudio`, and since Aug 12, 2026 the same prop on web's `src/pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx` (web silent games: Ticket Check, Luggage Match, Wrong Platform; pinned by the "silent games hide the mute control" block in `src/test/quick-game-frame.test.tsx`). Web games that own their header rather than the shell (Script Trace) render no mute control anyway. Per-game opt-out that removes the mute toggle entirely for a game that speaks nothing; defaults on, so existing games need no edit — which is also its one trap: a silent game that predates the prop keeps a live mute control over silence until it declares. Every shell game must state it deliberately, and its test should pin `queryByTestId('game-mute-btn')` either way.
- **Audio-active treatment**: `pages/games/quick-game-frame.tsx` `setAudioPlaying`. The mute control wears the active (green) treatment while a clip is playing.
- **Session-memory helpers**: `lib/quick-games.ts`. Per-language keys: waves are session-scoped, cleared marks device-scoped, plus stop-seen and closeout-stage memory.
- **Scenery planners**: `components/journey-scenery.tsx`. Deterministic per-zone scenery planning; seed-stable so the map never reshuffles between renders.
- **Inline-SVG character pattern**: `components/journey-scenery.tsx` (TrainEngine, SignalmanGlyph). Hand-drawn flat shapes from brand palette consts; no gradients, no raster, whole-glyph motion only.
- **Traffic-light state model**: `components/journey-scenery.tsx` SignalGlyph. One geometry, states upcoming/active/waved/cleared expressed by arm rotation and lamp fill only.
- **Soft-stop auto-open**: `pages/journey.tsx` + `markSignalStopSeen`. Encounter auto-opens once per session; a manual open counts as seen, so nothing re-opens on the learner.
- **First-sight seeding**: `lib/quick-games.ts` `seedCloseoutStages`/`closeoutStateUnseeded`. Already-done zones seed straight to done on first sight so nothing retro-celebrates.
- **Two-beat overlay**: `components/zone-closeout.tsx`. Celebration overlay staged as two beats (result, then reward) instead of one dense card.
- **Expiry-refetch hook**: `components/chai-wallet.tsx`. One timeout keyed on the expiry timestamp refetches token state when a multiplier lapses; no polling.
- **UpgradeCard + PlusPill**: `components/plus.tsx`. The only sanctioned upsell card and Plus badge; reuse them rather than composing ad-hoc upgrade UI.
- **Spend contract**: `components/chai-wallet.tsx` wrapper over `useSpendTokens`. Server-authoritative spends; the client never decrements a balance locally, it refetches on success.
- **Displayed prices**: `lib/pricing.ts` `usePricing()`. Any surface quoting a plan price reads the live Stripe catalog and omits the amount when it is unavailable; never hardcode a money string.


## 10ak. Review speaks the meaning too, so all three audio controls are real everywhere (August 12, 2026, bolo-mobile only)

**The last surface without a meaning segment now has one.** `artifacts/bolo-mobile/app/(app)/review.tsx` carries the practice screen's meaning-audio path verbatim in behaviour: after the coach phrase clip ends, a `MEANING_SEGMENT_PAUSE_MS` (400ms) beat, then the English meaning in an English voice (`languageName: 'English'`, `languageCode: 'en'`). Web review always had it for free — it renders the same component as web practice — and mobile practice had it since #1003; mobile review was the one gap, left out deliberately at the time and banked.

**The text rules are not re-implemented.** The line comes from the shared `meaningSpeechText` in `lib/meaning-audio.ts`, so the "means X" prefix rule (skipped for sentence-final punctuation or 6+ words) is one implementation on both mobile screens. Review calls it with NO `sentence` option, and that is correct rather than an omission: `GET /review/phrases` returns the `Phrase` schema, which has no stage field, so the helper's own heuristics are the only signal available. Practice keeps passing `{ sentence: isSentences }` because its route param tells it.

**Same gates, same token, no new plumbing.** The segment reads `bolo.meaningAudio` through the same lazy-ref pattern practice uses (`meaningPrefRef` starts null, `readMeaningPref` awaits the stored value once, later reads are synchronous), so a saved "off" is never raced by the first autoplay and a toggle applies to the very next phrase. The coach-voice master gate (`coachVoiceRef`) suppresses the whole chain at `playCoach`'s first line, so it needs no separate check. Playback rides the existing `playbackRef`/`playTokenRef` and `coachPlaying` spans both segments — which is what makes barge-in and iOS earpiece routing cover the meaning clip with zero extra code. Per-session `meaningCacheRef` is keyed by phrase id alone (the meaning is always English, so the coach cache's `id:voice` key shape does not apply), and the prewarm that overlaps the phrase clip resets its handle on failure so `playMeaning` retries fresh. Every failure path falls back silently to phrase-only.

**The menu is three items now, and 10ai's two-item ruling is superseded.** Review's `settingsItems` gained `meaning`, labelled from `LESSON_AUDIO_LABELS.meaning` (`Speak meaning`) like the other two, disabled when coach voice is off — the same rule practice applies to its own meaning row. The menu was not restructured and nothing was renamed. The two-item ruling in section 10ai rested entirely on review having no meaning code path; that premise is gone.

**Test note worth keeping.** `review-header-settings.test.tsx` now sets `bolo.meaningAudio` to `off` in its `beforeEach`. It asserts raw `playBase64Audio` call counts around silent-mode changes, and leaving the segment on puts a real 400ms timer plus a second synthesis and playback on every autoplay — a header test would then be timing-coupled to an audio beat it does not care about. The segment's own behaviour lives in the new `__tests__/review-meaning-audio.test.tsx` (on path, stored-off path, silent synthesis failure, preference persistence, and the coach-voice-off disabled state).

**New mobile suite baseline: 861 tests across 97 suites, fully green.** Owner-noted at this task's close — compare against this, not the 829/92 recorded under #1044. Device-pass note: real-device playback and earpiece routing for the review segment are unverified, but the segment rides practice's identical token and session handling, so it inherits practice's already-pending device pass rather than adding a second one. Check both in the same pass.


## 10al. A test file that could not be read was silently counted as zero (August 12, 2026, web tests only)

**The web suite has been running one file short since 17:02 today.** `artifacts/gujarati-coach/src/test/home-stats-banner.test.tsx` failed its esbuild transform (`The symbol "row" has already been declared`), so vitest reported it as one failed *suite* with no tests rather than as failing assertions. Its whole contribution vanished from the tally while the tally still said "86 files". The damage entered with the boarding-pass/referral commit and is the pasted-diff truncation failure mode again: the replacement hunk landed, the trailing hunks did not.

**Four separate injuries from that one truncation.** The body of `the three progress figures are one link to /progress` was deleted; the `test(...)` header of `keeps the reserved shell hidden while summary is pending` was deleted while its body survived, splicing into the test above it; two home-level stall tests (`the stall band shows the same balance as the Chai cell`, `the stall band opens the same wallet sheet`) were deleted outright; and a bare `const stall = screen.getByTestId("chai-stall-vignette")` was appended below the final `});`, which would have thrown at module evaluation even once the duplicate declaration was resolved.

**The pending-shell assertions were moved, not duplicated.** The error-state test (`summary failure shows visible feedback with a working retry`) still exists and is intact with its own `aria-hidden` assertion; the displaced `aria-hidden` / `invisible` / no-`couldn't load` trio belonged to the *pending* test, which existed nowhere else. It is restored as its own test in its original position.

**Corrected web baseline: 86 files / 714 tests, green, solo.** The previously recorded 708 was measured before the truncation, when this file still contributed all 21 of its tests and the tree had 85 test files — no single run ever produced "708 across 86". Measured here: at the corrupted HEAD, 695 tests / 85 passed | 1 failed (86), exit 1; repaired, 714 / 86, exit 0. The arithmetic closes exactly (708 pre-corruption + 8 tests the same commit added elsewhere - 2 stall tests not restored = 714). A full-tree `vitest list` collects all 86 files, so no other web test file contributes zero.

**Debt: the two home-level stall tests are still gone**, as is the pass-above-stall ordering assertion the truncated commit was adding (the orphaned `const pass` / `const stall` lines were its head and tail; `journey-pass-card` appears in no test today). `chai-stall.test.tsx` covers the component in isolation with props — it does not cover home wiring the stall to the page's single token query, nor the stall opening the same wallet sheet. Restoring those three is not in this task's scope.
## 10al. Chacha-ji's roadside stall: a gift, a line, and sometimes an offer (August 12, 2026, db + api + web + mobile)

**Where he stands is arithmetic, and the server owns it.** Chacha-ji appears at every fourth station from the third on the GLOBAL station index (3, 7, 11, ...), not the per-zone "Stop N of M" number, which repeats once per zone and would collide. The rule is `isEncounterStation` in `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/chachaEncounters.ts` (`ENCOUNTER_FIRST_STATION` 3, `ENCOUNTER_STRIDE` 4), and the clients carry their own copy purely to decide when to ask: `isChachaEncounterStation` in `gujarati-coach/src/lib/quick-games.ts` and `bolo-mobile/lib/chachaMemory.ts`. A client copy drifting cannot mint or skip a gift, because `arriveAtEncounter` re-derives the station from the DB: one window function over `lesson_groups` ordered by `categories.sort_order`, phrase-stage before sentence-stage, then position, which is exactly what the map flattens. A station that does not resolve is a 404, so a station that does not exist cannot be arrived at. The owner's draft read "every fourth stop per zone"; the ruling of Aug 12 2026 withdrew that in favour of the global index.

**Reach is checked before any chai moves.** Because where he stands is arithmetic, the index alone can never be enough: a caller could otherwise post every qualifying index in every seeded language and collect the gift without practising, including in a language their plan has not opened. `arriveAtEncounter` therefore resolves the station's lesson group and runs it through `getUnlockedGroupIds` (`lib/lessonGroupAccess.ts`) — the same unlock derivation the phrase-serving routes use, so the stall can never disagree with what the map lets a learner board. A real station of his that the learner has not opened is `403 station_not_reached`, and no encounter row, ledger row or phrase lookup happens. This also closes the locked-language case without a separate plan gate: reaching station 3 requires finishing stops that a locked language's own 402s make unfinishable.

**The gift is idempotent twice over.** `chacha_encounters` (migration 0047) is unique on `(user_id, language_code, station)` and the Chai rides the token ledger's own `(user, reason, ref)` index under `earn_chacha_encounter` with refId `<lang>:station-<n>`. A revisit, a double tap and two devices at once all produce one row and one gift. Only the arrival that actually inserted the LEDGER row reports `granted: true`, and that flag is the ONLY thing the celebration hangs off, so a replayed arrival is silent. The two writes are separate, and the order matters for who celebrates: the encounter row goes in first, so a racing request can find the row already there and still be the one that pours. Both branches therefore take `granted` from `grantTokensDetailed`'s own verdict; reading it off "did I insert the encounter row" would pay the learner correctly and then celebrate for nobody. The amount is `TOKEN_EARN_CHACHA_ENCOUNTER = 3` in `tokenEconomy.ts` (owner ruling: 3, not the draft's 5); nothing inlines it, and the copy shows `+3` from the response's `chaiGranted`, never a literal.

**The line he says is display and playback only.** It comes from the learner's own library: a mastered short phrase first (best attempt at or above the house threshold, read straight off `attempts`), otherwise one from a stop they have latched as `completed`/`tested_out`. One to three words, phrase stage only, measured on the same normalized text the phrase uniqueness index uses, so "what counts as one phrase" and "how long is it" never disagree about spacing. Selection is deterministic (`md5(phrase_id || ':' || station)`), never random, so a retried request cannot produce a second answer, and the recorded `phrase_id` is replayed on revisits. There is no mic, no score and no attempt row anywhere in this path.

**Deliberate narrowing worth knowing.** The fallback pool is filtered to non-premium rows for learners without `extendedLibrary`. A completed stop can still hold Plus-only phrases a Free learner has never been shown, and having Chacha-ji recite them would be a content leak dressed as charm. This is narrower than the contract's "any phrase from a completed stop" and is the one place the implementation is intentionally stricter than the AC.

**An offer rides at most every third encounter.** The cadence counts encounters per journey per language (`ordinal % 3`), not stations. Both filters are hard: never an outfit already owned, never one the balance cannot cover, cheapest first so the offer is reachable rather than aspirational. The price is compared against the balance AFTER the gift lands, so the chai he just poured counts. Nothing qualifying is an ordinary visit, not an error: he says "Come back soon, beta." and the dialog closes on "Thanks, Chacha-ji". Buying reuses `POST /outfits/buy` unchanged; there is no new purchase path and no real money anywhere near this.

**Client shape.** One endpoint does the whole encounter: `POST /journey/chacha-encounters { languageCode, station }`. Web mounts `ChachaEncounterDialog` (`gujarati-coach/src/components/chacha-encounter.tsx`) from `journey.tsx`, mobile mounts the same-named component (`bolo-mobile/components/journey/ChachaEncounter.tsx`) behind a `ChachaSoftStop` that follows the SignalEncounter discipline exactly: it waits for a lock, a signal dialog or an owed zone closeout to clear, fires once per station, and never appears in the showroom. Celebration is confetti plus MilestoneToast, per the ruling, and nothing else. One asymmetry to know: the "already offered here this session" memory is `sessionStorage` on web and AsyncStorage on mobile, because each mirrors that platform's existing signal memory; the server row, not the client memory, is what actually bounds the gift. That memory is written when the server ANSWERS, never before the request: marking on open would let one failed request cost the learner the encounter permanently (AsyncStorage survives the app). What stops a re-render firing a second arrival in the meantime is an in-flight ref inside the soft stop, which resets on remount so a failed arrival is simply retried the next time they open the map.

**Known debt (banked).** The read-only phrase card in both new dialogs is a THIRD copy of a card that already exists inline in web `practice.tsx` and mobile `practice/[id].tsx` + `review.tsx`. The owner ruled explicitly against extracting a shared card in this task (too much blast radius for a vignette), so the duplication is deliberate: any change to the phrase card's visual grammar now has three sites on web/mobile plus these two. The extraction is banked as its own task. Also banked: no device pass on either platform, and the `md5` ordering means a content edit that changes phrase ids can change which line he says at a station whose encounter row predates the edit only for learners who have not visited yet (recorded encounters replay their own phrase).

**Spec supersession.** `docs/specs/token-economy.md` section 4.5 names this character "Chaiwala" and calls for new flat train-world artwork drawn for the build. On naming and artwork it is superseded: the character is **Chacha-ji** (`chachaji` in code identifiers, ledger reason, files) and no new art was drawn — the stall renders the already-shipped `public/stall/` (web) and `assets/images/stall/` (mobile) PNGs. Section 4.5 otherwise concerns the SPEND vignette, which is a different surface and still belongs to the open task "Build the Chaiwala spend vignette so token spends feel rewarding" — that task was not touched here. Whoever picks it up must reconcile the naming with this section rather than re-introducing "Chaiwala".

## 10am. Chacha-ji's stall is on the map now, at every stop that pays (August 12, 2026, web + mobile)

**The stall is map scenery, at every encounter station, always.** Both journey maps now draw the trackside chai-stall scene at stations 3, 7, 11 and so on, whatever the learner's position — the ones behind them render exactly like the ones ahead. The station list is computed client-side from `planChachaStalls(totalStations)`, which loops the same `isChachaEncounterStation` predicate the arrival check uses (web `lib/quick-games.ts`, mobile `lib/chachaMemory.ts`), so the landmark cannot drift from the stop that actually pays. No new endpoint, no new field, no server round trip, and no artwork: it reuses the `chaiStall` scenery kind that already shipped in both scenery modules.

**RENDERING IS NOT TRIGGERING, and the layer enforces it.** The stalls live inside the existing scenery group, which is `pointer-events-none` / `aria-hidden` on web and non-interactive on mobile, and carries the parallax transform. Drawing a stall records no encounter, mints no Chai, marks no stop seen and makes no request; the arrival soft stop is untouched and remains the only thing that pays. Both platforms have a test asserting exactly this (no `chacha-` storage write on web, `useRecordChachaEncounter` never called on mobile), and the browser probe watches the network for `/journey/chacha-encounters` while rendering 15 stalls. Passed stations are NOT backfilled: a learner who walked past station 3 before this shipped sees the stall, not a gift.

**Chacha-ji himself is IN the stall, and he is the delivered art.** The first cut drew an unmanned prop (striped awning, counter, kettle) and the man the encounter is named after was nowhere on the map — the only place `chachaji.png` rendered was the home/wallet vignette (`chai-stall.tsx`, mobile twin `components/ChaiStall.tsx`). Both maps now draw the same isolated figure inside the stall group, at full height standing IN FRONT of the counter (24 x 32.3, his own 386:520 aspect), painted last so he overlaps his own counter. No new artwork was made: web imports `STALL_ASSETS` from `chai-stall.tsx` (safe, that module only pulls `cn` and its CSS), mobile inlines the `require` in `Scenery.tsx` rather than importing `ChaiStall.tsx`, which would drag the wallet's api hooks into the jest journey tests. `stall.png` is NOT usable here: it is a full opaque painted scene with no alpha. Gray/showroom handling stays group opacity 0.45 on both platforms rather than a web-only grayscale filter, so the platforms match.

**The map inserts a HALT ROW after every encounter station, and the stall stands on the RIGHT of the track in it.** `HALT_H = 74` (both platforms, exported on web as `SERPENTINE.HALT_H`). The layout loop pushes an extra path point of kind `halt` after any station the encounter predicate matches, at that station's own x, and advances `layoutY` by `HALT_H`. **The halt is not a stop**: `k` does not advance, so the serpentine phase, the stop numbers, the station count and every list in the app are exactly what they were; it draws no marker, no number and no card, and nothing in the row answers to a press. A halt takes the rail colour of the point it leads to (a post-pass over `pts`), so inserting one cannot change how any run of track is drawn, only how long it is. Cost: the map is about 13% longer on a 52-stop line.

**Seat: `STALL_PLACEMENT = { laneDx: 80, groundDy: 22, extentH: 49.2, shadowH: 5.1 }`, identical on both platforms, measured from the HALT POINT and not from the station.** Encounter stations are odd stops, so their 0-based index is even and the serpentine always puts their marker on the LEFT flank; the halt holds the rail on that same flank for a row, which is what frees the right side. `laneDx` is measured right from the rail there, `groundDy` centres the landmark in the row (y-27.2..y+27.1 of a 74-tall row). On a full-width map that lands the stall at x 153..187.5, y row+60..row+114 measured on the running app: 19px clear of the rail at its tightest, below the station card (which ends at row+39), above the next row's card and above a zone postcard when the stop closes a fare zone. `extentH` and `shadowH` exist so the clearance tests measure the drawing rather than the footprint — an earlier pass budgeted only the part above the ground line and the shadow pooled into the postcard band.

**The rail is a curve, so clearance is sampled, never assumed.** The rail leaves the halt point vertically and sweeps back out to the next station across the lower half of the halt row, reaching x≈127 by the stall's own bottom edge on a 390 map. Comparing the stall against the halt point alone would pass while the track ran under it. The web unit test samples the actual cubic (same control points the `d` strings use) inside the stall's y band, and the browser probe samples the drawn rail paths with `getPointAtLength` and reports the tightest gap.

**The ground shadow drives the bounding box, in both axes.** `GroundShadow` adds `SCENERY_GROUND_SHADOW.dx` (+2, the house down-light offset) to its centre; on a landmark this size that pushed the box 3px past the structure and straight into the signal glyph, which the geometry probe caught as a collision, so the stall passes `cx={-3}` to cancel it. Vertically the same ellipse (cy 1.2, ry = rx x 0.24) reaches below the ground line, which is what `shadowH` accounts for. Anything else grown to this size needs both corrections.

**The measured furniture in an encounter gap (map coordinates, `row` = station row centre y), and why the right side needed the halt.** Station card x 120..374, y row-39..row+39 — on a 390 map that is the whole right flank, leaving 16px to the map edge. Next station card x 16..268 from row+74 (row+148 with the halt). Trackside signal glyph x 42..82, y row+5..row+55, present at every odd stop and therefore at every encounter station. Zone postcard from row+60 whenever the encounter station ends a fare zone (station 43 does on the Hindi line). Right-flank zone scenery x 333..365 and zone signposts x 325..371 also land in the old gap. So without a halt the only universally free lane was x 0..42 on the LEFT, and the right pocket under the card was about 28px tall against a 54.3px landmark: the halt row exists because the stall could not be put on the right at the agreed size any other way.

**The decorative chai stall is retired from the theme table.** `ZONE_SCENERY_THEMES` used to place a `chaiStall` as ordinary decoration in zones 1 and 2, which would have put a second, meaningless stall a row or two from the landmark. Zone 1 now reads `tuktuk, fruitCart, banyan` and zone 2 `cycleRickshaw, tuktuk, marigolds`. The `chaiStall` asset itself is unchanged and still exported — on the map that art now means Chacha-ji and nothing else. A row hosting a stall also drops its planned decoration and is passed to `planZoneSignpost` as an avoided row (new optional third argument, web only; mobile has no signposts), so the landmark keeps its strip. Consequence worth knowing: in a six-zone line of 3-stop zones, the zone whose planned scenery row lands on an encounter station renders no decoration at all.

**Verified by eye on both platforms.** Web, learner standing on encounter station 3 (Ganga Line, viewport 320, the narrowest supported width): the stall stands in the empty halt row to the right of the track, clear of the current-stop marker, the train and the "Bolo is waiting here" card, with Chacha-ji visible. The browser probe (`qa/chacha-stall-geometry.mjs`) asserts the full set at 320, 360, 375, 390, 412 and 430 — every encounter station present, every stall fully inside the viewport, seated after its stop, right of every sampled point of the drawn rail, zero overlap with station markers, cards, postcards, signal glyphs, signposts or other scenery, Chacha-ji in every stall, and no encounter request. Mobile was photographed through the Expo web build (`qa/chacha-stall-mobile-shot.mjs`) and shows the identical seat in the identical halt row.

**Verified by measurement, not by eye alone.** `qa/chacha-stall-geometry.mjs` now loops `E2E_WIDTHS`, which DEFAULTS to the full sweep (320, 360, 375, 390, 412, 430) rather than a single width, resizing the viewport per width, and its FIRST check is viewport containment: every stall's page-coordinate box has to be fully inside the visible column at every width. That check did not exist in the first cut, which is exactly why "it falls off the screen" was reported and the probe was green. The probe also asserts a stall at every encounter station, each in its own gap, zero overlap with markers, cards, postcards, signal glyphs, signposts or other scenery, Chacha-ji's figure present in every stall group, and no `/journey/chacha-encounters` request while rendering 13 stalls. It exits non-zero when any check fails, so it can gate. Mobile is photographed through the Expo web build (`qa/chacha-stall-mobile-shot.mjs`) and shows the same figure in the same seat.

**Verified by eye on both platforms.** Web, learner at station 1 (Gujarat Express, 59 stops): stalls at 3 and 7 both visible ahead of them. Web, learner standing on encounter station 3 (Ganga Line): the stall sits clear of the current-stop marker, the train and the "Bolo is waiting here" card. A browser probe (`qa/chacha-stall-geometry.mjs`) asserts the full set — every encounter station present, each stall inside its own gap, zero overlap with station markers, cards, postcards, signal glyphs, signposts or other scenery, and no encounter request — and passed on both lines. Mobile was photographed through the Expo web build (`qa/chacha-stall-mobile-shot.mjs`) and shows the same relative seat beside the same signals.

**Debt.** The two scenery modules are still hand-mirrored copies, so `STALL_PLACEMENT` and `planChachaStalls` now exist twice and can drift; the mobile test pins the constants to the web numbers, which is the only thing holding parity. The mobile screenshot path needs a throwaway dev Clerk account with a password (the Expo build offers no ticket sign-in and Client Trust challenges new devices with a code); the probe creates one on demand and it should be deleted afterwards.


## 10an. One streak number, and a repair popup anchored on it (August 12, 2026, api + web + mobile)

**The defect.** The DAY STREAK banner counted attempt-days in the **active language only**; the streak-repair offer scanned attempt-days across **all** languages. Two expressions, one of them attached to a 25 Chai spend: the card could promise a 4-day streak the banner was structurally incapable of ever showing (observed: card 4, banner 1 and 2).

**The ruling, which is the new definition.** A streak day is a day the learner **completed a lesson OR played a mini-game, in any language**. Bare attempts do not earn a day. Existing learners may lose days retroactively; accepted (no production users of this surface).

**The single source: `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/streakDays.ts`.** `loadStreakLadder()` returns the earned days, the covered days and the ladder value, for a user across every language. It reuses the existing ladder climb (`streakFromDayKeys`) and the existing covered-day accessor (`listCoveredDayKeys`, pauses + prior repairs) untouched. Nothing is stored: the day set is derived per request over a 60-day scan window. Callers: `GET /progress/summary`, the analytics streak, `GET /tokens/streak-repair`, `POST /tokens/repair-streak`, `loadExtendedMetrics` (badges). `loadPracticeDayKeys` in `streakRepair.ts` — the other half of the defect — is deleted.

**The completion predicate is the client's, moved server-side.** An item clears when it reaches **good band or better** (`isGoodOrBetterBand`, now also in `lib/scoreBands.ts`) **or** the learner has taken **three** attempts at it that day (`STREAK_ITEM_ATTEMPT_LIMIT = 3`) — the three-strike pass. That is web `pages/practice.tsx` `isAdvanceUnlocked` / `ADVANCE_ATTEMPT_LIMIT` / `isGoodOrBetterBand` and its mobile twin in `lib/ui.ts`, verbatim. `isGroupCompleted` in `lessonGroupUnlock.ts` (an 80%-mastered ratio) is a DIFFERENT rule and was deliberately not adopted. Band is always re-derived with `bandFromScore()`, never read from the nullable legacy `attempts.band` column, and items are judged best-of-day because the client's advance gate is a latch.

**Completion days are derived from attempts, not from `lesson_group_progress`.** That table is a latch written when a read endpoint *observes* completion, so its timestamp is the observation day, not the practice day. Attempts are resolved to their lesson group through the phrase, bucketed into the learner's local day, and a day counts when every accessible item of some group cleared the predicate within it. Deriving this way means the entry point does not matter — a station session and a topic-wide session land on the same group. A **passed test-out** also earns its day, read from `lesson_group_testouts` and `zone_testouts` filtered to `passed = true` (those timestamps ARE the practice moment). A **Free learner's** accessible set is resolved inside the helper from the effective plan, so premium rows and `stage = 'sentence'` rows drop out and a Free learner can complete a station on the items they actually have; a group with no accessible items is skipped, never counted as vacuously complete.

**The mini-game clause reads `game_sessions`, not the phantom attempts.** Every mini-game write path inserts a `game_sessions` row alongside the streak-only phantom attempt — the generic game endpoint, daily quiz, and script trace — so it is a complete substitute, and it has to be, because attempts no longer earn days and the phantom mechanism therefore stops working. Script trace's asymmetry is carried over knowingly: it writes both rows only on the FIRST completion of a chapter, so a chapter replay anchored no day before this change and anchors none after it. **The phantom insertion is left exactly where it is** and is NOT dead code: those rows still feed attempt tallies, `attemptsToday`, and XP.

**The popup.** The inline home repair card is gone on both platforms. It is now a sheet anchored on the DAY STREAK cell — web shadcn `Sheet side="bottom"`, mobile RN `Modal` over a dismissing backdrop `Pressable`, both the ChaiWalletSheet shape — with the same copy, the same testids (`home-streak-repair-offer`, `home-repair-streak`, `home-repair-balance`) and the same 4s notice expiry. It opens only on interaction, never on load; it dismisses without charging; and when there is no repairable break it does not open at all — the cell keeps taking the learner to progress, as it always did. DAY STREAK is therefore out of the three-cell progress link on both platforms (a button cannot nest inside an anchor); Total XP and Mastered are still one link. The chevron affordance appears only when there is a repair behind the tap.

**Badges.** All six streak badges (`lib/badges.ts`, targets 3/7/14/30/60/100, `metric: "currentStreakDays"`) now read the same user-level all-languages number through `loadExtendedMetrics`, so badge progress and the banner cannot disagree. They are still awarded per-language; only the metric feeding them changed.

**Pinned by:** `src/routes/streakRepair.test.ts` (rewritten, 18 tests) — including the headline **multi-language** case, where a learner's completions are spread across two languages and the offer's `restoresStreakDays`, the repair's `restoredStreakDays` and the post-repair `/progress/summary.currentStreakDays` are asserted to be one number *in both languages* — plus direct coverage of the bare-attempts-only case, the mini-game clause (asserting zero attempt rows exist), and the Free-learner partial item set. Client behaviour is pinned by `gujarati-coach/src/test/home-stats-banner.test.tsx` and `bolo-mobile/__tests__/home-quiz-gate.test.tsx`.


## 10ao. The Settings error could not say what broke, and an unreadable token was a 500 (August 12, 2026, api + bolo-mobile + shared client)

**The rejection.** App Review rejected build 34 under Guideline 2.1(a): sign in with Apple, tap the Settings gear, "an error message shows". Settings is the ONLY screen in the mobile app that surfaces an API failure — home and the tabs fall back silently — so it is the only place a reviewer could have seen one. Its single error surface fires when `useGetAccount()` fails, and TanStack Query never retries a 4xx, so the message appears instantly rather than after a spinner.

**What was ruled out, and how.** The Apple-specific-null theory (no first/last name, `@privaterelay.appleid.com` email) does not hold: every field the screen reads is null-safe and a dev account shaped that way loads Settings cleanly. The server path for a brand-new account is sound — `requireAuth` → `ensureLocalUser` upserts the row before any route runs, the Clerk identity fetch and pending-invite consumption are both best-effort, `loadEntitlements` falls back to Free, and `GET /account` only 404s if the row is missing. **The reviewer's own session is unrecoverable**: deployment logs retain roughly six hours, and the rejection is older than that. Anything about that specific request is now inference, which is why the rest of this section is about making the next one self-describing.

**The production Clerk instance is the one the store build signs into — verified from the outside, no account created.** `qa/clerk-instance-probe.mjs` decodes the Frontend API host out of the publishable key the build ships (`pk_live_…` → `clerk.bolo-india.app`), reads that instance's JWKS, then sends the deployed API a syntactically valid but unsigned JWT and reads the key ids Clerk names in the `x-clerk-auth-message` rejection. Both sides report the same single kid, `ins_3H8cV5k38D9Ms6wk7nnxgYdTulH`; dev's is a different id, as it should be. The probe needs no secret, mints no session and touches no user, so it can be re-run against production at any time (this is the standing answer to "is prod pointed at the right Clerk instance?").

**The probe found a real defect: an unreadable token was answered 500.** Clerk's verifier returns a signed-out state for most bad tokens (`Bearer abc` → 401 `token-invalid`; no header → 401 `session-token-and-uat-missing`), but it THROWS on a few shapes — a signature segment that is not valid base64url raises `SyntaxError: Unexpected end of data` inside `decodeJwt` — and that rejection fell through to the global error handler. A client carrying a corrupt token therefore got `500 Internal server error`, which is indistinguishable from the server being down. `middlewares/unreadableTokenGuard.ts` now wraps `clerkMiddleware` and answers 401 with `x-bolo-auth-error: <reason>`.

**That translation is deliberately narrow, and the narrowness is the point.** It fires only when the caller actually presented a token (a Bearer header or Clerk's `__session` cookie) AND the error is about that token: a `SyntaxError` from decoding it (reported as `token-unreadable`), or a Clerk `TokenVerificationError` whose reason is token-shaped (`token-invalid`, `token-expired`, `token-invalid-signature`, `token-invalid-algorithm`, `token-invalid-authorized-parties`, `token-not-active-yet`, `token-iat-in-the-future`). **Everything else is re-thrown to express and stays a 500**: `jwk-remote-failed-to-load`, `jwk-kid-mismatch`, `secret-key-invalid`, `token-verification-failed`, and any unexpected internal or network error all mean the verification service or our configuration is unhealthy, not that the learner's sign-in lapsed. Laundering an outage into a 401 would march every learner to the sign-in screen and hide the incident from Sentry. Two further traps: the wrapper is now what express sees, so it must catch the rejected promise itself or the rejection goes unhandled; and a throw on a request with NO token cannot be about a token, so it stays a 500. Confirmed against the running dev API: corrupt token 401 + reason header, `Bearer abc` still 401 `token-invalid`, no header still 401 `session-token-and-uat-missing`. One accepted divergence from Clerk's own handling: a merely-invalid token leaves the request signed-out (so a PUBLIC route still answers 200), whereas an undecodable one 401s the request outright, public route included — a credential we cannot parse is answered rather than ignored, and only a broken client can send one.

**The mobile app now distinguishes a sign-in failure from a connection failure, and says which request failed.** `lib/apiErrors.ts` classifies any query error into `auth` (401/403), `connection` (no response at all), `server` (5xx) or `client`, and Settings renders the matching line — a rejected session reads "couldn't confirm your sign-in — try signing out and signing back in", never "check your connection". Under it sits a deliberately visible technical footnote, `/api/account — HTTP 401 · session-token-and-uat-missing`: endpoint, status and the rejection reason, so a screenshot from a reviewer or a learner is diagnostic on its own. The reason is read from `x-clerk-auth-reason` and, failing that, from the guard's own `x-bolo-auth-error` — the two sides are coupled by nothing but those header names, so a real 401 served the way the guard serves it is run through the mobile formatter in the mobile suite. That was the whole gap in the build 34 report. Status is read duck-typed (`name === 'ApiError'`) rather than with `instanceof`, because the shared client can exist as more than one module instance and this code's entire job is to describe failures.

**Every non-2xx response now leaves a Sentry breadcrumb, and a visible failure is captured.** `setFailedResponseObserver` (new, `lib/api-client-react/src/custom-fetch.ts`) fires for every non-2xx just before the `ApiError` is thrown, passing method, URL, status and the live headers; it is registered only by the mobile app (`installApiFailureBreadcrumbs` at `app/_layout.tsx`), so web behaviour is unchanged and Sentry stays out of the shared library. Breadcrumbs carry method, endpoint (query string stripped), status and `authReason` — never bodies, tokens or emails. Read the header THROUGH the headers object (`get.call(headers, name)`); a detached `Headers.get` throws "Illegal invocation" against a real response. Settings additionally reports its failure with `reportApiFailure('account.load', …)`, tagged `apiFailureKind` and `httpStatus` (`network` when there was no response). Verified end to end against the running API: a real 401 fired the observer with its Clerk reason, and a 200 did not.

**Pinned by:** `bolo-mobile/__tests__/account-error-states.test.tsx` (11 tests — auth vs connection vs 5xx copy, the visible endpoint/status/reason line, the Sentry payload for both a status failure and a transport failure, silence on a healthy load, both breadcrumb shapes, and a real guard-shaped 401 served over HTTP and read back through the formatter) and `api-server/src/middlewares/unreadableTokenGuard.test.ts` (9 tests — all three throw channels, Clerk's token-shaped reasons, the untouched happy path, and the pins that keep an unhealthy verifier, an unexpected error and a token-less request at 500). `jest-setup.js` gained `addBreadcrumb` on the Sentry mock.

**Residual, and the honest state of the diagnosis.** Step 1 of the task — creating a fresh Sign in with Apple account on a real iPhone against the production build and walking the reviewer's steps — was NOT performed: it needs a physical device and an owner Apple ID, neither of which exists in the workspace. The cause of the build 34 error is therefore still unconfirmed. The leading hypothesis is unchanged and now instrumented: the request reaches `/api/account` without a usable Bearer (a 401 pair on `/api/account` + `/api/entitlements`, 2ms and 1ms apart, is present in the surviving production logs and is the signature of a client sending no valid token — Clerk rejects before any DB work). Whatever it is, the next occurrence names itself on screen and in Sentry.

**Debt worth knowing.** `customFetch` treats every non-`response.ok` status as an error, so a `304` — which the API does emit for `/api/account` — throws `ApiError(304)` if a client ever sends a conditional request without the fetch layer handling the revalidation; `shouldRetry` does not short-circuit 3xx either. Not implicated in this rejection (React Native's fetch handles the 304 before it reaches this code), but it is a live trap for any other caller.
## 10ap. The boarding pass names the stop you are on, and the card stopped shouting (August 12, 2026, web + mobile, client only)

**The header's "current station" was the count of finished ones.** The pass rendered `{doneCount}/{totalCount} stations`, so the slot a learner reads as "the stop I am on" carried the number of stops behind them: 2 done while the map lit up stop 3. Both numbers now come off `allStations`, the one flattened list the map, the server payload and the Chacha encounter logic already share — the total is its length, the stop number is the very index `isChachaEncounterStation` checks — so the header cannot disagree with the highlighted stop. It reads `Stop N of M stations`, `All M stations complete` on a finished line, and plain `M stations` when there is no current stop. Identical derivation on both platforms.

**The `52` was never a client bug.** Neither platform has ever held a station constant; both fetch the six fixed zone endpoints and count what comes back, and the server route returns every group row regardless of plan or stage. Verified against both databases: dev holds **59** Gujarati lesson groups (11/10/8/10/10/10), production holds **53** (10/9/7/9/9/9). Production's creation history is 28 groups on 2026-07-28 plus 24 on 2026-07-29 — exactly 52 — with a single 53rd (zone 1, position 5, 2 phrases) written by the replenisher on 2026-08-12; every other production language still sits at exactly 52. **The shortfall is production content lag against the curated-library growth dev has, not client arithmetic**, and no client change can close it. Gujarati reads 59 in dev today and will read 59 in production only once the content is seeded there.

**The header line wraps now instead of truncating.** It carries the number the whole fix is about, and at 320px the route alone filled the ticket — `truncate` (web) / `numberOfLines={1}` (mobile) cut the stop count clean off the end. Both now allow a second line: measured unclipped at 320px, and still one line once there is room.

**`STATION_H` is 88 on both platforms, down from 100.** The current-stop card lost `" · Bolo is waiting here"` in every state — Bolo herself already stands beside it, so the fragment only ever said in words what the mascot says in the art, and it was what pushed the status onto a second line at narrow widths. The remaining status copy is untouched ("In progress", "Now boarding · N phrases"). Height came out of padding and line boxes only, never the type scale: web `py-2`→`py-1.5`, current-stop `pt-3`→`pt-2.5`, progress row `mt-1`→`mt-0.5`, `leading-tight` on the title and status; mobile `paddingVertical` 8→6, current-stop `paddingTop` 14→10, `cardStatus` `marginTop` 2→1 with `lineHeight: 14`, `cardProgressRow` `marginTop` 4→2, `cardTitle` `lineHeight: 18`. Title stays 14, status 11, track 80x6, terminus label 12. The title is held on one line by `whitespace-nowrap` / `flexShrink: 0`, so the chips wrap instead. **Measured on the running app at 320px: the current-stop card is 49px tall inside an 88px slot**, and a browser probe re-checked all 15 Chacha stalls for card overlap at the new rhythm — zero.

**The terminus label moved below the dot.** It used to flank the dot on whichever side the serpentine ended, which put it under the festival bunting (strung at `termY-34`, flags hanging to `termY+3`) and right-aligned a wrapped second line whenever the last stop landed on the right. It now sits at `termY + TERM_LABEL_DY` (18, a new constant on both platforms), spans the full column at `left: 12 / right: 12`, and is always centred — so wrapping cannot drift to an edge. Verified at 320px against every leaf shape drawn in the map SVG, bunting flags included: no overlap.

**No em dash survives on the journey map.** Swept on both platforms: the terminus line (now a comma), the station accessibility label, the sentence-stop chip title and the locked-stop dialog copy (now colons). Pinned by a jsdom sweep over the whole rendered tree plus a browser sweep over every text node and every `aria-label`/`title` on the page.

**Opening the map lands you on your stop, once.** Web mounts a small `AutoScrollToCurrentStop` child inside the map JSX — all of `Journey`'s hooks sit above its early returns, and mounting with the map gives "once per visit" for free. Mobile rides the map View's `onLayout`, the earliest moment the offset is known, guarded by `autoScrolledRef` / `userScrolledRef`. Both lead the stop into comfortable view rather than pinning it to an edge, clamp to the top of the line for an early learner, bail out entirely if the learner has already touched the wheel or begun a drag, and jump rather than animate under reduced motion. Measured at 320px: a learner on stop 12 of 59 opens at `scrollY 1414` with their card at y 186 of a 700px viewport.

**Debt.** The two journey screens remain hand-mirrored, so `STATION_H`, `TERM_LABEL_DY` and the header derivation now exist twice and can drift; only the mobile test pinning the web numbers holds parity. The browser probe (`qa/task1082-journey-polish.mjs`) needs a Plus account standing mid-line on Gujarati, which means seeding and then reverting lesson-group progress on a throwaway dev user — it does not create or clean that account for you.


## 10aq. The paywall links the Terms of Use and the privacy policy (August 13, 2026, bolo-mobile + web, client only)

**Why.** App Review rejected build 34 under Guideline 3.1.2(c): the subscription purchase flow carried no functional link to the Terms of Use (EULA) or the privacy policy. Nothing about pricing, products, RevenueCat configuration, plan titles, durations or per-unit prices was touched by the fix; the rejection is about disclosure, not commerce.

**The URLs are the ones already published.** `https://bolo-india.app/terms` and `https://bolo-india.app/privacy` both return HTTP 200 with no redirects and are reachable signed out. Plain curl is not evidence here: every route returns the same 3913-byte SPA shell, so both pages were rendered in headless chromium to confirm the real content ("Terms of Service", last updated July 13, 2026; "Privacy Policy", last updated August 2, 2026). Note the page is titled Terms of **Service** while App Review asks for Terms of **Use**; the link label says "Terms of Use" and points at `/terms`.

**Mobile: `lib/legal.ts` gained a terms URL and a pinned legal domain.** It previously built a privacy URL only, from `EXPO_PUBLIC_DOMAIN`, which is undefined in dev and injected at build time by `scripts/build.js` (which prefers `REPLIT_INTERNAL_APP_DOMAIN`, then `REPLIT_DEV_DOMAIN`). A missing env var used to mean no link at all, which is exactly the rejection, so `TERMS_OF_USE_URL` and `PRIVACY_POLICY_URL_ALWAYS` fall back to the production domain and are therefore always defined. The old `PRIVACY_POLICY_URL` keeps its may-be-undefined shape because the home screen only offers the link when a domain was injected and a test pins that.

**Mobile placement is above the purchase button.** `LegalLinks` (in `app/(app)/paywall.tsx`) renders straight after the trial disclosure line and before the status text and the `ChunkyButton` CTA, so both links are on screen without scrolling past the button. It also renders in the "subscriptions aren't available in this build" branch, next to Restore purchases: the disclosure belongs to the whole subscription surface, not only the branch that can transact. Each link is its own `Pressable` with `accessibilityRole="link"`, a 10pt hit slop, and the destination URL in its accessibility hint; both open in an in-app browser via `expo-web-browser`.

**Web needed a relabel only.** `FinePrint` in `pages/upgrade.tsx` already sat directly under the checkout button and already linked `/terms` and `/privacy`; the anchor text "Terms" is now "Terms of Use" so both platforms say the same thing.

**Pinned by:** `bolo-mobile/__tests__/paywall-legal-links.test.tsx` (5 tests — both links render and open a `https://…/terms` and `https://…/privacy` URL, both still render with no domain injected, both sit before the CTA in the rendered tree, and the annual/monthly prices and per-unit strings are unchanged) and two added tests in `gujarati-coach/src/test/upgrade-paywall.test.tsx` (hrefs on the All-Access flow, presence on the Family flow). Photographed on both platforms by `qa/paywall-legal-links-shot.mjs` (Expo web) and `qa/paywall-web-shot.mjs` (signed-in web). Full suites green: mobile 900/900, web 745/745, typecheck clean.

**The two disclosure URLs are pinned, not derived (amended August 13, 2026).** They were briefly built from `EXPO_PUBLIC_DOMAIN` with a production fallback, which meant a real build overrode the fallback with whatever `scripts/build.js` resolved: `REPLIT_INTERNAL_APP_DOMAIN`, else `REPLIT_DEV_DOMAIN`, else `EXPO_PUBLIC_DOMAIN`. Measured in this workspace, `REPLIT_INTERNAL_APP_DOMAIN` is unset, so a build here injects the `*.replit.dev` **dev** domain, and that is what the disclosure links would have carried into build 35. Both hosts do serve real legal pages (rendered in headless chromium, not curl, because every route returns the same SPA shell: dev domain `/terms` 200 h1 "Terms of Service" 5700 chars, `/privacy` 200 h1 "Privacy Policy" 4791 chars; `bolo-india.app` the same pages at 5560 and 4651 chars), so this was a mismatch-with-App-Store-Connect risk rather than a dead link. `TERMS_OF_USE_URL` and `PRIVACY_POLICY_URL_ALWAYS` are now built from a hardcoded `LEGAL_DOMAIN = 'bolo-india.app'` and read no environment variable at all, so no build environment can move them. Every other consumer of the injected domain is untouched: `lib/store.ts`, `lib/referral.ts`, `app/_layout.tsx`, and `PRIVACY_POLICY_URL` (the home screen's link, which still follows the build environment and still hides itself when nothing was injected). Pinned by three added tests in `paywall-legal-links.test.tsx` covering no domain, a hostile domain, and the untouched home-screen behaviour.

**Debt worth knowing.** The ASC metadata itself is still unverified from here: `APP_STORE.md` and `PLAY_STORE.md` document `https://<domain>/privacy` for the listings and mention terms at `/terms`, and neither pins the custom domain, so nobody has confirmed that the URL filed in App Store Connect is `https://bolo-india.app/privacy` rather than a Replit host. The home screen's privacy link can now point at a different host than the paywall's, which is legitimate (the pages are identical) but is a divergence someone will eventually notice.


## 10ar. The paywall's two legal links are exact URLs, not routes and not a domain (August 13, 2026, web + mobile, client only)

**Supersedes the URL half of section 10aq.**

**The rejection was 3.1.2(c), and a present link was not enough.** Build 34 was rejected because the purchase flow carried no Terms of Use (EULA) or privacy link at all; the partial fix that followed rendered the row but pointed it at the wrong places — mobile's "Terms of Use" opened the app's own `/terms` marketing page (which is not the EULA this app subscribes under), mobile's privacy link was rebuilt from `EXPO_PUBLIC_DOMAIN` (so a build could point a reviewer at a Replit dev domain), and the web paywall used internal wouter routes. Both surfaces now carry **the same two exact, owner-verified absolute URLs**, verified 200 with zero redirects on August 13, 2026:

- Terms of Use: `https://www.apple.com/legal/internet-services/itunes/dev/stdeula/` (Apple's Standard EULA)
- Privacy Policy: `https://bolo-india.app/privacy`

**Hardcoded is the decision, not a shortcut.** In `bolo-mobile/lib/legal.ts`, `TERMS_OF_USE_URL` and `PRIVACY_POLICY_URL_ALWAYS` are literal strings with no dependence on `EXPO_PUBLIC_DOMAIN` — a domain-derived URL is precisely what made the earlier links unreliable, because that env var is injected at build time and has been absent before. The web page holds its own pair of literals at the top of `gujarati-coach/src/pages/upgrade.tsx` (no shared legal module exists on web) with a comment tying them to the mobile pair. Do not shorten, redirect, re-derive or "DRY up" either pair against a domain constant. The separate, optional home-screen `PRIVACY_POLICY_URL` (undefined when no domain was injected) is deliberately untouched — a mobile test pins that behaviour and the home link is not part of a purchase flow.

**Placement and legibility are part of the requirement.** Mobile's `LegalLinks` row renders on BOTH paywall branches — the purchasable one (below the trial disclosure, above the CTA, with "Restore purchases" in the same view) and the "subscriptions aren't available in this build yet" one. Link text is `colors.primary` + underline: indigo-600 on light, indigo-500 on dark. Web keeps the existing wording, placement and muted-foreground sentence, but the two links are now `<a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">` with an always-on `underline` instead of hover-only. Titles, plan lengths, prices and per-unit prices on both paywalls are byte-for-byte unchanged.

**Pinned whole-string, because a substitution is the rejection.** `bolo-mobile/__tests__/paywall-legal-links.test.tsx` asserts the exact URL each link hands to `WebBrowser.openBrowserAsync` (previously a `^https://[^/]+/terms$` pattern, which would have passed on the wrong host), including with no domain env var present; `gujarati-coach/src/test/upgrade-paywall.test.tsx` asserts both `href`s whole-string plus `target`/`rel`, on the All-Access and Family branches. `qa/paywall-legal-links-shot.mjs` (Expo-web mobile capture + URL status) and `qa/paywall-web-shot.mjs` (headless-Clerk `__clerk_ticket` sign-in for the auth-gated `/upgrade`) photograph both surfaces; shots at `qa/shots/paywall-mobile-legal-links.png` and `qa/shots/paywall-web-legal-links.png`.

**Caveat.** Expo web can only render the "subscriptions unavailable" branch (no RevenueCat in the browser), so the purchasable branch's link row is covered by the jest render + position pin rather than by a photograph.
## 10ar. OAuth sign-in completes for a first-time user (August 13, 2026, bolo-mobile, client only)

**Why.** A Sentry `AuthIncompleteStateError` from build 34 (iOS 26.6, production, believed to be the App Review session) recorded `authContext: sso.oauth_google` stopped at status `needs_identifier`. `needs_identifier` with a `transferable` first-factor verification is Clerk's way of saying "this person has no account yet, transfer the sign-in into a sign-up", the flow ended signed out, and Settings then called `/api/account` unauthenticated and rendered its error card.

**The transfer call was already being made, by the SDK.** Read from the installed dist rather than assumed: `@clerk/expo` 3.7.4's `startSSOFlow` reloads the sign-in with the rotating token nonce, runs `signUp.create({ transfer: true })` itself when the verification is `transferable`, and returns `signUp.createdSessionId ?? signIn.createdSessionId`. So the defect was not a missing call — it was that when that transfer came back incomplete, the app read only `signIn.status` (still `needs_identifier`), printed it, and discarded the sign-up resource holding the actual reason. `completeSsoFlow` now reads the sign-up, reports its missing and unverified fields, and makes the transfer call itself **only** when the SDK did not attempt one, so a version that does not auto-transfer still works and the current version is never asked to transfer twice.

**The provider label was not wrong.** The event was pulled from the Sentry API (org `lark-enterprises-llc`, project `bolo-mobile`) rather than inferred: its factor list is `google_one_tap, ticket, oauth_apple, oauth_token_apple, oauth_google`, which is the instance's enabled strategies for an identifier-less sign-in, not the provider that was used. The reviewer tapped **Continue with Google**. The label was a hardcoded literal per component (`'sso.oauth_apple'`, `'sso.oauth_google'`) that could drift from the strategy actually passed to `startSSOFlow`; it is now derived by `ssoContext(strategy)`, so it cannot.

**There was a second, separate defect: a session nobody picked up.** Nineteen seconds after that Sentry event, production Clerk created a user via `oauth_apple` (private relay email) **with an active session** — and that user has no row in the production application database, so no authenticated call ever reached the API. The handshake worked, Clerk minted a session, and the app stayed signed out showing "Apple sign-in did not finish", a branch that deliberately reported nothing. `completeSsoFlow` now adopts such a session before any error copy is shown, and reports the recovery so it is never invisible.

**The recovery only ever adopts a session that this attempt created.** The session ids on the Clerk client are snapshotted *before* `startSSOFlow` runs, and recovery requires both a successful `authSessionResult` and an `active` session whose id was not in that snapshot. Adopting `lastActiveSessionId`, or any already-present active session, would sign the app in as whoever used the device last — a wrong-identity hole, caught in review before this shipped and now pinned by two tests (a pre-existing active session is never adopted; a cancelled sheet recovers nothing). A cancelled or dismissed browser is also now classified from `authSessionResult` rather than from an absent status, so closing the sheet no longer produces an alarming diagnostic or a Sentry event.

**One shared completion path.** `lib/ssoAuth.ts` holds the whole decision tree and both buttons call it: an existing `createdSessionId`; else a transferable sign-in answered with `signUp.create({ transfer: true })`; else a transferable external account answered with `signIn.create({ transfer: true })`; else the client-session recovery; else a described stop; else a dismissal.
