Every branch ends in a session, a message, or a dismissal — never a signed-out screen with no explanation. `reportAuthIncompleteState` gained an optional non-PII `extra` so each report carries the phase, the verification statuses and the sign-up fields still missing, all of which were unreadable in the reviewer's event.

**Apple's quirks need no special casing.** A private relay address is a normal verified email to Clerk (two production accounts are on `privaterelay.appleid.com`), and the name Apple sends only on the first authorization is already optional everywhere in the app. A transfer that genuinely cannot complete names the missing fields on screen instead of a generic failure.

**Evidence for the diagnosis.** Both Clerk instances and both databases were read (read-only; the development instance is `ins_3H8VaWllfPUHnspKABmqEru4Hpb`, and nothing was written to production). Since July 30 the only OAuth accounts created are `oauth_apple`: development one, production three. **No `oauth_google` account has ever been created from mobile**, which is the transfer gap showing up in the data.

**Pinned by:** `__tests__/sso-transfer-flow.test.tsx` (15 tests — both transfer directions, the SDK's own transfer read rather than repeated, recovery of a genuinely new session, the two ways recovery must refuse, the missing-fields message and its Sentry extras, the silent dismissal, the derived provider label, and the Google button wired end to end). Mobile suite 918/918, typecheck clean.

**Debt worth knowing.** The reviewer's Apple dead end is fixed by recovery, not by removing its cause: the browser handoff can still return nothing. The instance has `oauth_token_apple` enabled, so iOS could pass Apple's native credential straight to Clerk and skip the browser entirely, which is filed as follow-up work because it can only be verified on a device. None of this has been exercised against a real provider handshake from here; the transfer path is proven by unit tests only.

## 10as. The two web Sentry issues, and why the traces were blank (August 13, 2026, gujarati-coach)

**The traces were destroyed by our own scrubber, not by missing source maps.** `beforeSend` runs every event through `scrubValue`, which returned the literal string `"[depth-limit]"` past depth 6. A stack frame's fields sit at depth 7: event(0) → `exception`(1) → `values`(2) → `values[0]`(3) → `stacktrace`(4) → `frames`(5) → `frames[n]`(6) → `filename`/`function`/`lineno`(7). Every frame therefore shipped as `{filename: "[depth-limit]", function: "[depth-limit]", lineno: "[depth-limit]"}`, which is why both issues read as unresolved frames. Proven by feeding a realistic event through the committed function, not by inspection. The guard is now a runaway/cycle backstop only: depth 24 plus a `WeakSet` cycle guard returning `"[circular]"`. Key-based redaction and email masking are unchanged, so the PII policy in §7b still holds — `vars` and other nested payloads are now traversed and redacted by key instead of being blanket-stringified. Pinned by `src/test/sentry-scrub.test.ts` (frames survive, exception type/message survive, deep `transcript`/`phrase` still redacted, emails still masked, cycles terminate).

**No web source maps are uploaded to Sentry, and none are even generated.** `artifacts/gujarati-coach/vite.config.ts` sets no `build.sourcemap`, the web package has only `@sentry/react` (no `@sentry/vite-plugin`, no `sentry-cli`), and a production build emits zero `.map` files. Web frames will stay minified after the scrubber fix — readable (real filename, function, line) but pointing at `journey-<hash>.js`. Mobile is the only app wired for upload (`@sentry/react-native/expo` plugin, needs `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` as an EAS secret — still open in debt); api-server ships no upload step either. `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` in this workspace cannot read the Sentry API (`GET /api/0/organizations/` → 403), so events cannot be pulled from here for the web project.

**BOLO-WEB-2 `planChachaStalls is not defined` does not exist in the committed tree.** `planChachaStalls` is exported by `src/components/journey-scenery.tsx` and imported by `src/pages/journey.tsx` in every revision that references it — checked across all six commits that touch either file; no revision uses it without importing it. The dev server's transformed modules carry the import and the export, a production `vite build` completes with no "is not exported by" warning, and a real signed-in browser load of `/journey` (fresh load and client-side nav from `/app`) raises zero console or page errors and renders the full map. All 44 events were development-only, which fits an intermediate uncommitted editing state during the stall work rather than shipped code. Nothing to fix, and nothing was changed in the journey map.

**Why no test or typecheck would have caught it anyway.** A missing import is a compile error, so `tsc -b` would fail the build — it passes. The failure mode this class of event describes (a reference that exists in a running dev module graph but not in the source) is invisible to both, because both read the source. What covers it is the real-browser probe: the web suite renders journey with jsdom and cannot execute the scenery geometry path the same way.

**BOLO-WEB-1 `useLanguage must be used within a LanguageProvider` could not be reproduced and no code path explains it.** `LanguageProvider` wraps `TooltipProvider`, `EquippedOutfitProvider`, `ReferralRedemptionProvider`, the router, the prefetcher and the toaster in `App.tsx`; only `ClerkAuthSync` and `AnalyticsIdentitySync` sit outside it and neither touches language. `/app` renders Guard → AppShell → Home, all inside. The provider has no early return, there is one React root, and both portals (`XpArc`, brand splash) inherit context. Its position last moved on July 12, 2026. Every caller of `useLanguage`/`useNativeText`/`useSpeechCapability` (both navs, the language picker, `XpCounter`, the Chacha encounter, and the page modules) renders below it. A signed-in browser load of `/app` raises zero errors. **The July 28 "first seen" date is when web Sentry was added** (`src/lib/sentry.ts`, commit a93d42c), not the date of a regression — the issue is as old as the reporting. Development dominates the counts (71 of 78), which fits Vite HMR of a mixed-export module (`language-context.tsx` exports a component *and* hooks, so it is not a React Refresh boundary) briefly leaving a consumer on a different module instance than the provider. The 7 production events remain unexplained from source; with frames restored, the next occurrence names the component and the file in one event. Nothing was swallowed or defensively defaulted: `useLanguage` still throws.

**Same depth-6 scrubber still lives in `api-server/src/lib/sentry.ts` and `bolo-mobile/lib/sentry.ts`** — identical defect, identical blank frames, left untouched here because this task was scoped to the web app. Worth a follow-up.

## 10at. The scrubber depth fix extended to api-server and mobile (August 13, 2026)

**Same defect, same fix, now in all three apps.** `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/sentry.ts` and `artifacts/bolo-mobile/lib/sentry.ts` carried the identical `depth > 6` guard described in §10as, so every stack frame in every event from both projects has been blank since Sentry was added on July 28, 2026. Both now use the depth-24 backstop plus a `WeakSet` cycle guard returning `"[circular]"`. `SENSITIVE_KEYS` and the email regex are byte-for-byte unchanged in both files — the fix changes only how far the walk goes, never what it redacts. Each project mirrors the web test: `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/sentry.test.ts` (node:test, imports the module with `SENTRY_DSN` deleted so the import cannot start a reporting client) and `artifacts/bolo-mobile/__tests__/sentry-scrub.test.ts` (jest; `@sentry/react-native` is already globally mocked in `jest-setup.js`), 5 tests each.

**Measured, not assumed.** HEAD and working-tree versions of both files were bundled and run against a realistic event. One frame, before and after:

| App | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| api-server | `{filename: "[depth-limit]", function: "[depth-limit]", lineno: "[depth-limit]"}` | `{filename: "/app/dist/index.js", function: "listLessonGroups", lineno: 412}` |
| bolo-mobile | `{filename: "[depth-limit]", function: "[depth-limit]", lineno: "[depth-limit]"}` | `{filename: "app:///lib/ssoAuth.tsx", function: "completeSsoFlow", lineno: 214}` |

The same run confirmed the PII policy still holds after the fix on both: `user.email` → `[redacted]`, a `transcript`/`phrase` pair nested five levels deep → `[redacted]` (its sibling `score: 82` survives), free-text email → `[email]`, and a self-referencing event terminates at `[circular]`.

**api-server needs no source map upload, and that is by design.** `build.mjs` emits `sourcemap: "linked"` (`dist/index.mjs.map`, ~14 MB, with a `//# sourceMappingURL` comment), and the process starts as `node --enable-source-maps ./dist/index.mjs` in both the dev script and the deployment command in `.replit-artifact/artifact.toml`. Node itself rewrites `Error.stack` to original TypeScript positions before `@sentry/node` ever reads it, so the frames arrive in Sentry already pointing at `src/**.ts`. Nothing to upload; the only thing that was destroying those frames was the scrubber.

**Mobile source map upload is configured and unchanged by this task.** `app.json` still carries `["@sentry/react-native/expo", { organization: "lark-enterprises-llc", project: "bolo-mobile" }]` (not touched), and `eas env:list --environment production` shows both `EXPO_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` and `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` present, the latter as a builder-only secret — which closes the §7b debt row that said the token was still missing. Caveat on the word "confirm": this is configuration-level evidence. The upload log line from the most recent production build (iOS build 35, `dc4681c9-ed17-4088-a328-de8d225964c8`, FINISHED 2026-08-13T14:38Z) was not read, because EAS does not expose raw build logs to the CLI here and the workspace `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` is 403 against the Sentry API (§10as), so the uploaded artifact bundles cannot be listed from this workspace either.

**Worth stating plainly for the app in review:** uploaded source maps could never have helped mobile until now. Symbolication needs frames to symbolicate, and every frame was the string `[depth-limit]` before it left the device. Builds from this change onward are the first that can produce a readable mobile trace.

---


## 10au. The web bottom nav floats like the mobile pill (August 13, 2026, gujarati-coach only)

**What it is now.** `src/components/layout/bottom-nav.tsx` root is `fixed left-0 right-0 z-40 nav-float bg-card border border-border lg:hidden`. `.nav-float` (`src/index.css`, `@layer utilities`) carries the geometry copied from the mobile tab bar: `left/right: 14px`, `bottom: max(env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px), 14px)`, `border-radius: 32px`, `box-shadow: 0 10px 20px rgba(15,23,42,0.16)`. Opaque card background, 1px border all round, no blur — the same as `artifacts/bolo-mobile/app/(app)/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` `tabBarStyle`. It is still mounted once in `AppShell`, deliberately outside the `PageTransition` subtree (§ the fixed-position/animated-ancestor rule still applies), and still `lg:hidden`; the desktop sidebar and the shell's `lg:pl-64` are untouched.

**Measured geometry at 320px (real chromium):** pill 292×106 at `left 14 / right 14 / bottom gap 14`. Inside it: the XP strip (32px: `pt-1.5` plus the 24px chrome XpCounter) then the icon row at exactly mobile's bar height — `h-[74px] pt-1.5 pb-2`, so the 60px slot inside matches mobile's `74 − 6 − 8`. The raised Bolo circle is `h-[58px] w-[58px] rounded-full border-[2.5px]` holding a `size={44}` Mascot — mobile's `boloBubble` (58/29/2.5) and `boloImage` (44) exactly. It is anchored from the BOTTOM of its slot like mobile (`absolute bottom-[63px]`), which puts its lower edge 34px below the pill's top edge and 24px of the circle above it — the same two numbers mobile produces (circle bottom 34px below the bar's top edge, 24px protruding, i.e. 32% of the 74px bar).

**Drift found against the task brief's Step-0 measurements:** the brief recorded the mobile protrusion as "roughly 16px, about 22% of bar height". That treats the tab slot as the full 74px. Yoga's `height` includes padding, so the slot is `74 − 6 − 8 = 60`, its bottom sits 8px above the bar's bottom, and `boloBubble { bottom: 32 }` therefore lands the circle bottom 34px below the bar top with **24px** protruding (32%). The 24px figure is what the web nav mirrors; it is also what makes mobile's own `boloLabel` clear the circle (label top 40 vs circle bottom 34), which the 16px reading would not.

**Where the XP strip and mobile's pixels pull against each other, and the call made.** Web's nav carries an XP strip mobile does not have, so the pill is 106px tall, not 74. Matching the circle's protrusion *above the visible pill edge* (24px) and its depth *into the pill* (34px) simultaneously is possible, and that is what was chosen — the circle's relationship to the chrome the learner actually sees is identical on both platforms. The consequence is that the "Bolo" label cannot ride directly under the circle the way it does on mobile (6px gap); instead the Bolo link fills its slot (`flex h-full w-full flex-col items-center justify-end pb-1.5`) and the label sits on the same baseline as Home/Games/Progress/GU, with the circle absolutely positioned above it. Label-row rhythm beats reproducing mobile's 6px circle-to-label gap. The circle stays `bg-card` opaque in every state (the active treatment is a gradient overlay on top), and at 320px the XP numbers end at x=63 while the circle starts at x=131 — the numbers are never overlapped; only the centre of the XP progress track passes behind the opaque circle, as it did before this change.

**The centre label reads "Bolo Chat", in the brand colour (amendment, August 13, 2026).** First, the finding behind it: the centre label was **not** styled differently from its siblings. Measured at 320px, "Bolo", "Games" and "Progress" all computed to the identical `text-muted-foreground` — `rgb(101,117,139)` light, `rgb(163,176,194)` dark — at 10px/700; the only visibly paler label in the row is the language `gu`, which is deliberately `text-muted-foreground/70`. The centre label's paleness was perceptual, not chromatic: it is a short word sitting alone with ~47px of empty card between it and the circle above (siblings sit 4px under their icons), so it has almost no neighbouring ink and reads as detached. The one real style difference was `leading-none` (a 10px line box vs the siblings' 15px), which moves the baseline by under half a pixel.

It is now `text-primary` in both the active and inactive state — the raised circle is the primary destination, and the active/inactive distinction is already carried by the circle's border and gradient. Contrast holds in both themes (light `rgb(80,72,229)` on the white card, dark `rgb(145,140,242)` on the dark card — the same value the active Home label uses).

The text is two hard-broken lines, "Bolo" over "Chat", at `text-[11px] font-bold leading-[1.1]`, each line `whitespace-nowrap`. **Why two lines and not one:** at 320px each tab slot is 55px wide, and one line of "Bolo Chat" at 11px bold measures ~56px — it would wrap or clip. Stacked, the lines measure 24px and 26px, comfortably inside the slot. All four constraints hold, measured: the icon row is still exactly 74px and the pill 106px (the label block is bottom-aligned inside a fixed-height slot, so it cannot grow the bar); the other four labels' boxes are unchanged at 658–673; nothing is below 11px; and neither line wraps or truncates. The block keeps `justify-end pb-1.5`, so its **last** line sits exactly where the single "Bolo" line used to (bottom 671) and the shared label baseline is preserved — the first line stacks upward into the empty gap under the circle, which is also what closes the "orphaned" look.

**Mobile still says "Bolo".** The mobile centre tab renders a single 12px semibold label, muted when unfocused and brand-coloured when focused. It was left untouched (out of scope) and filed as its own task; until it lands, this label is the one visible difference between the two bars.

**One shared clearance value.** `.pb-nav` in `src/index.css` is web's equivalent of mobile's `TAB_BAR_CLEARANCE`: `padding-bottom: calc(138px + env(safe-area-inset-bottom, 0px))` = 14 bottom gap + 106 pill + 18 breathing room. It is wrapped in `@media (max-width: 1023.98px)` deliberately — Tailwind v4 emits user `@layer utilities` CSS after its own generated utilities, so an unscoped `.pb-nav` would beat `lg:pb-12` at desktop width. Applied (replacing `pb-28`) on the seven AppShell routes' scroll containers and the home skeleton: `pages/home.tsx`, `components/home-skeleton.tsx`, `pages/progress.tsx`, `pages/friends.tsx`, `pages/phrasebook.tsx` (whose `min-h-screen` was also brought to `min-h-[100dvh]` like the rest), `pages/games/index.tsx`, `pages/outfits.tsx` (which gained an `lg:pb-12` it never had), and `pages/journey.tsx`'s `main`.

**Not every nav-bearing route is an `AppShell` route.** The game screens are NOT wrapped in `AppShell`, but each one imports `BottomNav` and mounts it itself: `pages/games/word-match.tsx`, `speed-round.tsx`, `listen-and-pick.tsx`, `phrase-builder.tsx`, `script-trace.tsx`, `bolo-quiz.tsx`, `_coming-soon.tsx`, and `quick-game-frame.tsx` (which backs `/games/ticket-check`, `/games/wrong-platform`, `/games/luggage-match`, `/games/express-listening` and `/games/signal-lights`). All eight were moved from `pb-28` to `pb-nav` too — `pb-28` is 112px and the pill's footprint reaches 120px, so they would have hidden their own bottom content. **The rule to apply when adding a page: grep for `BottomNav` imports, not for `AppShell` routes.** Practice, review and category-detail genuinely render no nav; their `pb-28` clears nothing and was left alone (recorded as debt rather than changed, since removing it is a visual change to the practice flow).

**Pre-existing defect found while probing (not fixed here, filed separately).** `/games/speed-round` throws `Rendered fewer hooks than expected` for a Free learner: the route is Plus-gated, the phrases request 402s, and the screen's early return on that 402 happens after some hooks have run. The learner gets a blank crashed page instead of the paywall the other gated games show. Nothing in the nav change touches it — the nav task's diff on that file is one className.

**Toast stack.** `components/ui/toast.tsx` viewport moved from `bottom-[calc(5.5rem+…)]` (which assumed a flush 88px nav) to `bottom-[calc(8.25rem+…)]` = 132px, clearing the pill's 120px top edge by 12px. `z-[100]` unchanged.

**Layering re-checked, unchanged:** nav is z-40; dialogs/sheets/alert-dialogs/drawer/zone-closeout/confetti and the Chai wallet (a Sheet) are z-50, badge unlock and XpArc z-[60], toasts and brand splash z-[100]. Nothing needed touching.

**No fade mask on web.** Mobile added a gradient mask below its ScrollView because Home's recent-plays rows read as a leftover practice feedback bar peeking under the pill. Web's equivalent surfaces carry no feedback vocabulary, and the pill idiom is meant to show page content in the side margins and bottom gap, so the mask was deliberately not ported. Revisit only if a "phantom bar" report appears on web.

**Evidence.** `qa/task1098-nav-float-probe.mjs` (new, QA-only): signs in a real Clerk user, measures the pill (insets, bottom gap, radius, opacity, border, shadow, position/z-index), the circle (size, protrusion, depth, opacity, distance from the XP numbers), then for each of the **18 routes that render the nav** (seven AppShell routes plus eleven game routes) asserts the nav is present and measures the last visible leaf element against the pill's top edge after scrolling to the bottom; it also checks the journey map's auto-scroll landing position and, at 1440px, that the sidebar is still 256×full-height with no pill and `lg:pl-64` intact. All 47 checks pass, worst clearance 18px. Screenshots at 320px (light + dark, plus a bottom-of-page shot per route) and 1440px land in `qa/shots/task1098/`.

**Measuring the Plus-gated game routes.** `/games/speed-round`, `/games/phrase-builder`, `/games/script-trace` and `/games/bolo-quiz` need Plus, so a Free QA account either crashes (speed-round, above) or is redirected to the paywall, and their clearance cannot be observed. They were measured by temporarily setting the QA account to Plus **in the development database only** (`executeSql` default environment) and reverting the row to its exact prior values (`tier free`, all subscription columns NULL) immediately afterwards. Production was never written to; a read-only production check confirmed the account has no row there. Rule for future probes: never grant an entitlement in production and never use a tier-minting dev override — use the development instance and say so, or report the routes as unmeasured.

Web suite after the change: 88 files / 750 tests, all passing — `src/test/bottom-nav.test.tsx` needed no edits because destinations, labels, routes and the canonical mascot PNG are unchanged. (The completion review saw 2 failures in `practice-start-position.test.tsx`; that file passes 9/9 on its own and the review runs the api, web and mobile suites concurrently, which is the known load-flake condition for the web suite.)
## 10au. Chacha-ji speaks his own three lines, in his own voice (August 13, 2026, api + web + mobile)

**He was silent.** Everything he "said" at the roadside stall was on-screen text; the only audio in his modal was the learner's practice phrase, read in the coach voice for pronunciation reference. He now speaks three fixed lines of his own at the three moments of the encounter.

**The lines are the character, not content to learn.** They are fixed romanized Hindi for every learner regardless of journey language, and they are never localized, scored, graded or recorded — no attempt row, no evaluation token, no progress write:

| key | text | on-screen English |
|---|---|---|
| `greeting` | `Aao, aao. Chai piyo.` | Come, come. Have some chai. |
| `gift` | `Yeh lo. Garam hai.` | Here you go. It's hot. |
| `farewell` | `Phir aana, beta.` | Come again, beta. |

"beta" stays untranslated in the farewell gloss by owner ruling: it is a term of affection, not vocabulary.

**His synthesis identity is his own, and shares nothing with the phrase or chat voice.** `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/chachaStrings.ts` owns the texts, the provider and model (`gpt-4o-mini-tts`), the voice (`echo` — male, deliberately not `PHRASE_AUDIO_DEFAULT_VOICE`), the format (`mp3`) and the delivery instructions. It does **not** call `phraseAudioIdentity()` and does not read the phrase or chat constants; `greetingStrings.ts` is the pattern it follows, not the phrase one. Both the prewarm and the route call `chachaLineCacheKey()` — neither derives voice, model or instructions independently.

**Cache namespace:** `bolo-chacha-<version>::<provider>::<model>::<voice>::<instructionsDigest>::<lineKey>`, currently at `v1`. It shares nothing with the phrase key scheme or the greeting key, so a phrase-instructions edit or a phrase provider switch can neither orphan nor collide with his clips. The voice, model and 8-hex instructions digest all ride in the key, so changing any of them orphans stale audio automatically; the version tag exists to be bumped when a **line's wording** changes, which the key cannot otherwise see.

**The em dash in his instructions string is deliberate — do not "fix" it.** `CHACHA_TTS_INSTRUCTIONS` reproduces, character for character, the string the owner-approved voice samples were generated with. The project's no-em-dash house rule governs copy we author; this is the reproduction of a verified artifact, and editing it changes the voice that was signed off on (and rotates the cache digest, re-synthesizing everything). `src/lib/chachaStrings.test.ts` pins the Tone line verbatim so a well-meaning sweep fails loudly.

**Settings ruling: `bolo.coachVoice` governs his lines; `bolo.silentMode` ("Autoplay phrase") does not.** His lines are flavour dialogue, not a pronunciation reference — there is no recording to get out of the way of and no replay affordance, so silencing them with a control labelled "Autoplay phrase" would be a surprise. But a learner who switched Bolo's voice off entirely must not suddenly hear a new voice, so the master gate applies and suppresses **the request as well as the playback**: voice off ⇒ the stall is completely silent and `GET /openai/chacha-lines` is never called. Web reads the pref synchronously once per open (a mid-encounter toggle cannot cut him off mid-word); mobile reads it from AsyncStorage and holds the request until it answers.

**One endpoint, deliberately separate from the arrival.** `GET /openai/chacha-lines` (authed, read-only, no parameters) returns all three clips in one response, cache-served with on-demand synthesis when the prewarm has not landed. It is kept entirely apart from `POST /journey/chacha-encounters` so nothing about the Chai grant, the balance or the celebration can be delayed or failed by audio — web fires the two in parallel, never chained. A line whose synthesis fails is **omitted** from `lines` rather than failing the response, so callers must look lines up by key, never by index.

**Prewarm at startup, off the boot path.** `warmChachaLines()` in `ttsPrewarm.ts` runs after `warmGreetings()` on the same fire-and-forget path. It synthesizes only what is missing, is a no-op once all three are cached, swallows every failure (one line failing costs that line only), and deliberately does **not** run through `synthesizeVerifiedPhraseAudio` — the transcribe-and-retake ladder exists for pronunciation reference audio, and nothing is graded against these.

**Playback is a module-scope queue on both platforms** (`gujarati-coach/src/lib/chachaVoice.ts`, `bolo-mobile/lib/chachaVoice.ts`). Two reasons, both load-bearing: his lines arrive from three unrelated events and must never overlap (a line waits for the previous one to finish), and the farewell is queued as the dialog is already closing — anything owned by the dialog would be torn down before it spoke. On mobile that specifically means it cannot be the player the modal's unmount cleanup stops. Every failure path resolves the queue rather than stalling the lines behind it, and playback failure is silent: no error UI, no retry ring.

**Web needed a fifth blessed audio singleton.** `getChachaAudioElement()` joined the four in `iosAudio.ts` and is blessed by `blessAudioPlayback()` on every entry gesture — a per-play element carries no WebKit blessing and would be silent on iOS (see §7 and `.agents/memory/webkit-element-blessing.md`). The journey map's own entry gestures already call `blessAudioPlayback()`, which blesses all five. `src/test/ios-audio-bless.test.ts` pins the set at five.

**The phrase reference audio in the same modal is untouched.** It stays on the existing coach-voice `synthesizeSpeech` path and stays tap-to-play, because the same phrase must not be modelled in two different voices depending on which screen the learner is standing on. `PHRASE_AUDIO_DEFAULT_VOICE`, `BOLO_MINI_TTS_VOICE`, `phraseAudioIdentity()`, the phrase cache key scheme and the constant-pinning test in `parrotChat.test.ts` are all unchanged.

**Standing absence, not a regression: the encounter's phrase does not autoplay on arrival.** On either platform. There is no autoplay code path for it and no spec text or test that ever required one — it has always been tap-to-play. The greeting landing on open is **not** a fix for it and must not be mistaken for one. If it is ever wanted, the cheap seam is the same one the greeting uses (the coach element is already blessed by the journey entry gesture, so the missing piece is only a call, not plumbing) — but it would need a ruling first, because that audio *is* a pronunciation reference and would therefore fall under "Autoplay phrase", unlike Chacha's lines.

**Pinned by:** `api-server/src/lib/chachaStrings.test.ts` (10 — approved wording, glosses, the em-dash Tone line, voice separation from the phrase/chat identity, key namespace and distinctness, and the prewarm's cold/warm/partial/one-line-fails behaviour), `gujarati-coach/src/test/chacha-voice.test.ts` and `bolo-mobile/__tests__/chachaVoice.test.ts` (the queue: no overlap, one element, fail-silent release, caption hooks), and the encounter suites on both platforms (greeting on open with its gloss on screen, gift only when granted and absent on a replayed arrival, farewell on each close path, each line at most once, master-voice-off silence with `enabled: false`, the phrase card still on the coach path, and only the single arrival write).


## 10av. The XP strip counts toward the next train class, not the attempts goal (August 13, 2026, shared lib + web + mobile)

**The defect was a unit error, not a miscalibrated goal.** The XP strip divided today's XP by `users.daily_goal` — the learner's target number of practice **attempts** per day (schema comment in `lib/db/src/schema/users.ts`, API description "Target attempts per day (1–100)", account-settings presets 3/5/10/15/20/30). Dividing an XP total by an attempts target produced "254/10 XP" with the bar clamped full, which reads as broken. Nobody ever chose an XP goal; there is no XP goal anywhere in the product. `dailyGoal`'s other consumers — the home attempts line, the Day Streak arc, and the `MilestoneToast` that fires off `attemptsToday` crossing it — read it correctly against attempts and are untouched.

**The ladder.** Four classes, ascending: **100 Local, 200 Superfast, 400 Rajdhani, 800 Shatabdi**. The denominator is always the first rung **strictly above** today's XP, so the numerator is always strictly below it and overflow is impossible **by construction rather than by clamp**. Below 100 the meter fills toward Local and names no class. At 800 or more there is no denominator at all: the class name stands alone with no bar and no fraction. The thresholds are calibrated against real award amounts (base XP is `5 + difficulty * 5` per scoring attempt in `api-server/src/lib/xpEngine.ts`; a 14-phrase station is roughly 100–200 XP; a full day with the daily quiz and a mini-game is roughly 200–300 XP; the Express multiplier can double the pronunciation share), not picked for roundness. **How XP is earned and awarded is completely unchanged** — no edits to the award engine, band thresholds, the Express multiplier or any ledger write.

**"Express" is deliberately absent from the ladder, by owner ruling.** In this product the word already means five other things — the XP multiplier, the express stamp, the express test-out, the Express Listening mini-game, and three journey line names. Superfast holds the 200 rung instead. Do not "restore" Express to the ladder.

**Do not use bare "tier" for a rung.** "Tier" already means the subscription plan (free / one_language / plus / family). The approved words are `rank`, `class` and `trainClass`, and nothing in `@workspace/train-class` or either component says "tier".

**`todayXp` is per-language, and that is deliberate — do not unify it with the streak.** The streak is all-language; this number is not. The reason: the meter sits in a lesson header inside one language's journey, so the number must describe **that** journey. Two adjacent day-bucketed numbers with different scopes is exactly the kind of inconsistency a later reader helpfully "fixes", so it is written down here. The scope is the only thing that differs — both bucket their day the same way (below).

**One shared pure helper owns everything, and it is the single source.** `lib/train-class` (`@workspace/train-class`, private, `type: module`, exports straight from `src/index.ts`, composite tsconfig emitting declarations only — the shape of `lib/referral-link`) exports `TRAIN_CLASS_LADDER` and one function, `dailyTrainClassMeter(todayXp)`, returning everything a screen renders: `{ xp, target, heldClass, fill, atTop }`. **No caller may re-derive any of these from the raw XP.** That is the acceptance criterion most likely to be quietly violated — a component computing its own percentage, or a second copy of the thresholds, reintroduces the class of bug this replaces. There is deliberately **no `Math.min` on `fill`**: a clamp would let a future overflow bug hide itself, which is precisely how the original defect stayed invisible. Both artifacts reference the package as a TypeScript project reference, and they typecheck against its **built** `.d.ts`, so `tsc -b lib/train-class` must run before their typecheck will pass (see §10 on stale referenced dists).

**The same helper owns the day boundary, and both platforms now agree with the server.** `msUntilNextLocalDay(timeZone)` computes the wall-clock time in the learner's **stored** IANA zone via `Intl.formatToParts`, with the fallback chain stored → device → UTC that `resolveLearnerTimeZone` implements — the same chain the server's `localDayKey` bucketing uses. Both clients previously read their **device's** calendar fields, so any learner whose stored zone was not their device's saw the strip reset at the wrong moment, or not until an app restart. It finds the **instant** of the next local midnight rather than subtracting elapsed wall-clock time from a fixed 24 hours: a local calendar day is 23 or 25 hours long on the two days a year a zone changes offset, and fixed arithmetic reschedules an hour late in spring and an hour early in autumn. A 365-day sweep in a DST zone pins that the chosen boundary really does render as `00:00:00` in the zone. One divergence from the server is deliberate and specified: when **nothing** is stored the server buckets in UTC while the client tries the device zone first. The window is transient (clients auto-report their zone on reconcile) and the cost is only *when* the client refetches, never *what* it displays — the number always comes from the server. The timezone reaches both strips through the existing language contexts (`gujarati-coach/src/lib/language-context.tsx`, `bolo-mobile/contexts/LanguageContext.tsx`), which already hold the account query; adding the field is additive, so a test that mocks the context without it degrades to the device-zone fallback rather than breaking.

**One optimistic writer, not three.** `applyOptimisticTodayXp(queryClient, lang, xpAwarded)` lives in the same package and replaced three hand-rolled copies of the same cache increment (the web practice screen, the mobile practice screen, the mobile review screen). Exactly one function writes the optimistic daily XP; the class, the denominator and the bar all follow from `dailyTrainClassMeter` reading what it wrote. This is why the package carries deps on `@tanstack/react-query` and `@workspace/api-client-react` and is not the zero-dep shape of `lib/referral-link`. There is no second place where displayed XP is computed.

**The class name appears in both variants, including the compact in-session one.** This is forced by the top state rather than chosen for decoration: at 800+ the class name **is** the whole strip, so dropping it from the compact variant would leave an empty slot in the practice and review headers. On the compact variant it sits on its own line below the numbers row; inline it would push the strip past the language chip at 320px. Web's session minimum width went 72px → 84px and mobile's session track 44px → 64px (chrome 60px → 76px) to hold a three-digit numerator over a three-digit denominator.

**The nav variant right-aligns the class name, and that is load-bearing.** The web floating nav's row runs the full width of the pill and the raised Bolo circle breaks through its middle (§10au), so a name rendered straight after the numbers disappears behind the bird. It is pushed to the right end of the row instead, and the badge shrink-wraps in both variants — a full-width box would sit over the circle even when the ink does not. At the top of the ladder the name replaces the numbers and therefore stays where they were. The XP-arc targeting registration (`registerXpCounter` / `registerXpCounterPop`) and the landing pop are unchanged; **no celebration, confetti, sound or animation was added on class change.**

**Pinned by:** `gujarati-coach/src/test/train-class.test.ts` (the helper: zero, below the first rung, exactly on each of the four rungs, between them, at and above the top, negative/non-finite/fractional input, and a sweep asserting the numerator never reaches the denominator), a three-state component test per platform (`gujarati-coach/src/test/xp-counter.test.tsx`, `bolo-mobile/__tests__/xp-counter.test.tsx`: no class yet, mid-ladder, top class). **Evidence:** `qa/task1103-trainclass-shots.mjs` (web, 320px) and `qa/task1103-trainclass-mobile-shots.mjs` (mobile, Expo web at 412×824) force `todayXp` by rewriting the `/api/progress/summary` response and assert, on the running app, the fraction, the class name, bar presence, that the bar is not pinned full, that the strip clears the language chip and the settings gear in the header, that the nav's class name clears the raised Bolo circle, and that the numbers row does not wrap. Both probes pass in full; shots in `qa/shots/task1103/` and `qa/shots/task1103-mobile/`.
## 10av. A Plus gate that returned before its last hook (August 13, 2026, gujarati-coach only)

**The defect was hook order, not the phrases request.** `/games/speed-round` did not fail because `GET /categories/{id}/phrases` answered 402 — a Free learner never got far enough to make that request. `SpeedRoundPage` placed its `if (!isLoading && !isPlus) return <Redirect to="/upgrade" />` guard **above** its last hook call, `const handleDone = useCallback(...)`. The first render happens while entitlements are still loading, so React records the full hook list; the moment the snapshot resolves `plan: "free"` the guard fires, the `useCallback` never runs, and React throws `Rendered fewer hooks than expected. This may be caused by an accidental early return statement.` The screen unmounts to a blank page. The fix moves the guard below every hook and pins the reason in a comment; nothing about the gate's semantics changed.

**The three sibling gated games were already correct, and were checked, not assumed.** `phrase-builder.tsx`, `script-trace.tsx` and `bolo-quiz.tsx` all place the same guard after their last hook (bolo-quiz explicitly, after its `useCallback`), which is why only Speed Round crashed. The audit was mechanical, not by eye: `qa/hook-order-audit.cjs` walks every component in a directory with the TypeScript parser, finds the first top-level conditional `return`, and reports hook calls in the statements after it — ignoring hooks nested inside closures, which a grep or a regex cannot do. Run across **all 17 files** in `src/pages/games/` (`bolo-quiz`, `_coming-soon`, `express-listening`, `game-previews`, `index`, `listen-and-pick`, `luggage-match`, `phrase-builder`, `quick-game-frame`, `script-trace`, `signal-lights`, `speed-round`, `ticket-check`, `word-match`, `wrong-platform`, plus `game-config.ts`/`game-previews.css` which hold no components), then across **every `.tsx` in `artifacts/gujarati-coach/src`** and **every `.tsx` under `artifacts/bolo-mobile/app`**, it reported exactly one violation: `SpeedRoundPage`. There is no second instance of this shape anywhere in either client.

**jsdom cannot catch this class of bug, so the evidence is a real browser.** The fault only exists across two renders with a real entitlements round-trip, which is why 764 passing web tests never saw it. `qa/task1101-speed-round-gate-probe.mjs` signs in through a dev Clerk `__clerk_ticket`, confirms `GET /api/entitlements` reports the expected plan, then walks the four gated routes plus `/games/word-match` as a free control, asserting the landed path, that the paywall actually painted (`#root` > 500 chars, not a blank shell), and that no `pageerror`/`console.error` fired. With the fix stashed it failed exactly four checks, all on `/games/speed-round`, all naming the hooks error, with `rootHtml=0 chars` — the blank page, reproduced. With the fix restored: 42/42 PASS, both tiers, console clean.

**Sentry says this never reached a production learner: 0 events, 0 users.** Both recorded events (group `7670260183`, `2026-08-13T16:37Z` and `16:41Z`) are `environment: development`, one distinct user, one URL — the dev host's `/games/speed-round`. This is not a reporting gap: `bolo-web` holds 188 events since `2026-07-28`, of which **10 are `environment: production` from `https://bolo-india.app`** (nine ClerkJS network errors and the original synthetic verification event), so the production DSN demonstrably reports. The match was made on message + `url` tag, not on stack frames, per the `[depth-limit]` caveat.

**Correction to §10as: the workspace `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` is not blind, it is project-scoped.** That row records `GET /api/0/organizations/` → 403 and concludes "events cannot be pulled from here for the web project." The org-level and **issue**-level endpoints do 403 (`/organizations/`, `/organizations/{org}/issues/`, `/projects/{org}/{proj}/issues/`), but `GET /api/0/projects/` and **`GET /api/0/projects/{org}/{proj}/events/?full=true`** both return 200 and paginate via the `Link` header. Event-level triage from this workspace is therefore possible; issue-level aggregates (counts, `userCount`, first/last seen) are not, and must be recomputed from the event list.

**Debt worth knowing.** Nothing enforces the ordering rule. A future gated screen can reintroduce the identical defect and every suite will stay green, because the only thing that catches it is a real browser on a Free session. `qa/hook-order-audit.cjs` is committed but nothing runs it — it is a tool someone has to remember, not a gate — and `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks`, which would flag this statically at zero cost, is not wired into this workspace: there is no ESLint run in any package script and no `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` dependency, only inline `// eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` comments addressed to a linter that never runs. Also unenforced: the probe's Plus leg needs a Plus session, and it got one by flipping a dev probe user's `tier` to `plus` in the dev database and restoring it to `free` afterwards (both verified by query) — there is no standing Plus fixture account, so the next person will do the same dance.

**New observation, unrelated to this fix: the mobile suite now flakes on the 5s default timeout under validation.** The completion run reported `bolo-mobile` 4 failed / 99 passed (930/934 tests), every failure the same `Exceeded timeout of 5000 ms` with no assertion error: `luggage-match`, `quick-game-shell`, `sso-transfer-flow`, `wrong-platform`. Run targeted and serial (`npx jest <those four> --runInBand`) they are **4 suites / 130 tests, all passing, in 6.7 s** — so this is jest's default parallel fan-out across 103 suites starving timer-driven tests, not broken code (nothing in this task touches `artifacts/bolo-mobile`). It is the mobile analogue of the web/api contention already recorded in `.agents/memory/parallel-suite-flakes.md`. Treat a mobile failure whose only symptom is a 5000 ms timeout as unsettled until it is re-run targeted; a real fix is either a raised `testTimeout` or `maxWorkers` in `jest.config`.
## 10av. The mobile centre tab reads "Bolo Chat", on one line (August 13, 2026, bolo-mobile only)

**What changed.** `artifacts/bolo-mobile/app/(app)/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` — `BoloTabButton`'s visible label is now the literal `Bolo Chat`, `numberOfLines={1}`, `colors.primary` in **both** focused and unfocused states (it was muted-grey when unfocused), at `fontSize: 11` (was 12), still `AppFonts.semibold`. `boloOuter` gained `paddingHorizontal: 0`. Nothing else moved: `tabBarStyle` height stays 74, `boloBubble` stays `bottom: 32`, `boloOuter` keeps `justifyContent: 'flex-end'` + `paddingBottom: 10`, and the other four tabs are untouched.

**One line, not web's two — the bar has no room and the owner ruled height wins.** Web's version stacks "Bolo" over "Chat" because its card leaves ~47px of empty space under the raised circle. Mobile has 22px of label room (bar 74, circle bottom 32, label block bottom-anchored with 10px padding). Two 11px Inter SemiBold lines need ~26.6px at natural leading and would overlap the circle; forcing `lineHeight: 11` leaves zero clearance and clips ascenders. The constraint order the owner set was: (1) bar height unchanged, (2) sibling label baselines unchanged, (3) no font below 11px, (4) no truncation or mid-word wrap at the narrowest width — two lines cannot satisfy all four, one line can.

**The blocker at 320pt was slot padding, not the font.** measured with opentype.js against the shipped `Inter_600SemiBold.ttf`, "Bolo Chat" is 51.34px at 11px, inside the 58px centre slot. But react-navigation forwards `paddingHorizontal: 5` to every tab slot, clamping the label box to 48px — the first probe run rendered `Bolo C…`. `paddingHorizontal: 0` on `boloOuter` **only** restores the full 58px (6.7px slack). That is a content-box change: the `Pressable`, its hit area, and every other slot are unchanged.

**Baseline is preserved by the bottom anchor.** Because the block is bottom-aligned, 12px → 11px moves the baseline only by the descender delta; measured Δ 1.00px against siblings whose own spread is 0.00px.

**Accessibility deliberately unchanged.** `accessibilityLabel` stays `'Bolo'`, and `'Release to send'` while a chat recording is held; the screen's `title: 'Bolo'` is untouched. VoiceOver announces exactly what it did before — the visible label and the accessible name are separate strings here on purpose (web's equivalent link kept its own accessible name too).

**The web change this mirrors is not in this tree.** `artifacts/gujarati-coach/src/components/layout/bottom-nav.tsx` at the task's base commit still renders a single-line "Bolo" at `text-[10px] font-bold`; "Bolo Chat" appears nowhere in web source. The two-line web nav presumably lives on a branch not yet merged here. No web file was touched.

**Pinned by:** `artifacts/bolo-mobile/__tests__/tabs-layout.test.tsx` (`describe('Centre tab label')` — brand colour when unfocused, `numberOfLines === 1`, `fontSize: 11`, and the accessible name still "Bolo") and `qa/task1106-nav-label-shots.mjs`, a real-browser Expo-web probe sweeping 320/360/375 in light and dark that asserts bar height 74, one line, `scrollWidth <= clientWidth`, in-viewport, 11px, sibling baseline spread, centre baseline match and circle clearance, in both focused and unfocused states. Shots in `qa/shots/task1106/`.

**Pre-existing, left alone:** the 12px `Progress` label (52.4px) already ellipsises to `Progr…` at 320pt in the shipped bar, for the same slot-padding reason. Out of this task's scope.

## 10aw. Chai packs on iOS: Apple's till, the same catalog, and a purchase that recovers itself (August 13, 2026, api + bolo-mobile + web fixtures + docs)

**A pack is worth half what it was, and only one file says so.** Owner ruling, August 13, 2026: the Chai each pack grants HALVES — small 50→25, medium 150→75, large 400→200 — with the prices unchanged at $1.99 / $4.99 / $9.99. Zero pack purchases exist on either platform, so nothing already sold was redefined. The amounts live in `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/chaiPacks.ts` and nowhere else, on any platform: web reads them through the pricing catalog, iOS is TOLD them by `GET /chai-packs`, and both credit paths read `pack.chai` off the same rows. The pack `productName`/`description` fields were restated to match ("Bolo! Chai — 25", etc.) because Stripe shows them on the checkout page and a product still saying "50" while crediting 25 lies to a paying learner. **The Stripe seeder must NOT be re-run against the live products** — `scripts/seedStripeProducts.ts` matches by exact name, so a run after the rename creates duplicates. Renaming the three live Stripe products is the owner's dashboard job. The top pack landing at exactly the 200-Chai outfit rack total is deliberate and accepted; the catalogue is due to grow.

**The accidental all-access grant is closed, and the fallback was the bug.** `concernedEntitlement` in `revenuecatSync.ts` used to return `"plus"` when an event named no entitlement at all — a backwards-compatibility fallback from before entitlement ids were reliably present. It now returns `null`: no event may grant, extend or modify a subscription without saying which entitlement it concerns. `NON_SUBSCRIPTION_PURCHASE` is ALSO in the ignored-for-subscription set, so the guard holds twice over — by event type and by the absence of an entitlement — and a dashboard misconfiguration that attaches our entitlement to a consumable still cannot move a tier, because the consumable branch returns before `applyFromEvent` is ever reached. This fix landed BEFORE any consumable code existed, since creating the SKU is what would have fired the defect.

**Apple's SKUs sit in the catalog, not in the app.** Each pack carries `appleProductId` (`bolo_chai_cutting` / `bolo_chai_kulhad` / `bolo_chai_kettle`, created and priced in App Store Connect by the owner). `GET /chai-packs` (`routes/chaiPacks.ts`) serves pack id + Apple product id + Chai amount and **no price** — deliberately NOT the Stripe-priced `/pricing` endpoint, which 503s when Stripe is unreachable and would make the iOS shop depend on the web payment processor. The price a learner sees on iOS is the StoreKit product's own `priceString`; no money number crosses from our server to the phone.

**The refId is the TRANSACTION id, prefixed — never the original.** iOS credits are written under their own ledger reason `purchase_chai_pack_ios` with refId `apple_tx:<transaction_id>`. Two decisions, each load-bearing: the separate reason means the Stripe and Apple idempotency domains cannot collide even if an id string were shared; and `transaction_id` (unique per purchase) rather than `original_transaction_id` (reused across every purchase of the same product) is what lets a learner buy the same pack twice and be credited twice. Keying on the original id would have made the second kettle collide with the first and silently credit nothing — the ledger index eating a real payment. Both directions are pinned by name in `routes/revenuecat.test.ts`: distinct purchases credit twice, one replayed purchase credits once.

**A paid purchase that did not credit is recovered by replay at launch.** Owner ruling, settled: at launch the app reads the customer's non-subscription transactions, asks `POST /chai-packs/credited` which of them the ledger already has, and calls `Purchases.syncPurchases()` to make the store re-deliver the rest (bounded to two attempts, then next launch tries again). The client only ever ASKS — it never asserts an amount, never mints Chai, and keeps no local books; double-crediting is refused by the `(userId, reason, refId)` index, not by client bookkeeping. `POST /chai-packs/credited` is a caller-scoped read: it answers only for the authenticated user, dedupes and caps at 100 ids. The RevenueCat SDK's wide "purchases are completed by my app" mode was deliberately NOT enabled — taking over completion for every subscription to solve a consumable problem is the wrong trade on a money path that works. (Caveat worth knowing: RevenueCat documents `syncPurchases` as intended for apps not purchasing through the SDK. It is used here as the only replay lever compatible with that ruling; it re-posts a receipt Apple has already charged for and cannot buy anything.)

**A consumable purchase has a fourth outcome, because a consumable grants no entitlement.** The subscription flow's success test is "did an entitlement appear" — which reports a perfectly good pack purchase as a failure. `purchaseChaiPack` in `contexts/PurchasesContext.tsx` reuses the existing provider wholesale (SDK config, Clerk id as store user id, sandbox confirmation guard, outcome shape) and changes only the success signal: a purchase succeeded when the SERVER shows the transaction credited (`waitForCredit`, polling ~9s). If the credit has not landed by then the outcome is `pending`, not `success` and not `error` — the money is real and the Chai is not there yet, and the copy says exactly that ("Chai on the way"). `lib/chaiPurchase.ts` holds the store- and React-free logic so all of this is testable without a device.

**The mobile shop ships dark, exactly as web's does.** `CHAI_PACKS_LIVE` in `components/ChaiPackShop.tsx`, same shape as the web flag: an exported constant, `false`, overridable per render by a `live` prop so tests exercise both states. It gates the SURFACE only — the catalog endpoint, the purchase call, the webhook credit, the ledger write and launch recovery are all live and exercised beneath it. The shop also renders nothing when there is no purchases provider, no catalog, or no priced store products.

**Test notes.** `jest-setup.js` now mocks `react-native-purchases` globally (the wallet renders the shop, so any wallet test reaches the native module); a test that exercises purchasing declares its own mock, which wins. That own mock must expose the SDK through a **getter** (`get default() { return mockPurchases; }`) — babel hoists the factory above the `const`, so a direct reference captures `undefined`. `__tests__/chai-pack-shop.test.tsx` calls `render()` OUTSIDE `act()` (RNTL 13 throws "Can't access .root on unmounted test renderer" otherwise) and drives the poll/recovery timers with `jest.advanceTimersByTimeAsync`.
## 10aw. The referral code is the friend code, and email lookup is gone (August 13, 2026, db + api + web + mobile)

**Production had zero friendships when this was built.** Measured against the production database before any code was written: `friendships` 0 rows (all statuses), `referral_redemptions` 0, `friend_invites` 0, `users` 12 — 6 of whom had ever been minted a referral code. The leaderboard had no audience at all. Nothing in this section should be read as maintaining an existing social graph; it is the first one.

**One code, two jobs.** A learner's referral code is now also their friend code. There is no second code and no second alphabet: `REFERRAL_CODE_ALPHABET` in `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/referral.ts` is 32 characters (`ABCDEFGHJKLMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789` — no `0`/`O`, no `1`/`I`) at length 6, so the space is 32^6 ≈ 1.07e9 (2^30). Minting retries on the `users_referral_code_idx` unique violation, so a collision is an arbitration, never a duplicate.

**The accept step is load-bearing, and reuse is only safe because of it.** Referral codes are designed to be broadcast — flyers, WhatsApp groups, events. Every code-initiated add therefore lands as a **pending** friend request the recipient must accept. Delete the accept step and every flyer silently becomes an open friend list. A safety note recording this sits at the code's definition (`referral.ts`, above the alphabet), at `referralCode` in `lib/db/src/schema/users.ts`, and at the accept handler in `artifacts/api-server/src/routes/friends.ts`. The one exception is referral redemption, which auto-friends instantly — redeeming someone's link is already an explicit act by both parties.

**`POST /friends/requests/by-code`** (operationId `sendFriendRequestByCode`) is the only way to create a friend request from a code. It normalizes with the shared `normalizeReferralCode`, matches **exactly** (no prefix, no fuzzy, no "did you mean"), and always inserts `status: 'pending'`. Rejection wording is uniform on purpose: an unknown code, a near-miss of a real code, and a code belonging to someone the caller already has a relationship with all return the **same 404 with the same sentence** (`REFERRAL_COPY.unknownCode`), so the endpoint is not an oracle for which codes exist. Self-add is the deliberate exception and answers 400 — the caller already knows their own code.

**Rate limiting is database-backed, not the in-memory middleware.** `middlewares/rateLimit.ts` is per-instance and resets on deploy, which is too weak for a guessing surface. `friend_code_attempts` (migration `0048`) follows the zone test-out pattern: a cheap non-authoritative `SELECT` fast path, then an authoritative recheck and the log insert inside one transaction serialised by two advisory locks (`friend_code:user` then `friend_code:ip`, always in that order, so concurrent callers cannot deadlock). Limits: **10 attempts per rolling hour per account, 30 per rolling hour per IP** — the per-IP axis exists because a per-account limit alone does not bound an attacker who spreads guesses across accounts. IPs are stored as an HMAC-SHA256 of `SESSION_SECRET`, never in the clear. Attempts are logged on **hits as well as misses**: counting only misses would let a guesser refill their budget by interleaving a code they already know is good. 429 carries `Retry-After` plus `retryAfterSeconds`.

**Auto-friend on redemption is idempotent in both directions.** `ensureAcceptedFriendship` in `artifacts/api-server/src/lib/friendship.ts` returns `"created" | "promoted" | "already"`, and `findFriendshipBetween` checks **both** ordered pairs — the unique constraint covers only one, and would not stop a duplicate reverse row. A pending request in either direction is promoted to accepted rather than duplicated. The call sits after the redemption insert in a try/catch: a social-graph failure must never affect attribution or Chai. **The Chai grant did not change**: redemption still grants nothing, activation still pays both sides on first completed practice.

**Email lookup is gone.** `GET /friends/search` and the email-based `POST /friends/requests` are deleted from the router and from `lib/api-spec/openapi.yaml`; the generated clients and the search fields on both platforms went with them. What remains is the email **invite** flow, which mails a download link to someone who has no account and creates a pending request when they sign up — that is not a lookup and is deliberately kept, behind a link rather than a field.

**QR is JS-only on both platforms; scanning is native and mobile-only.** Web uses `qrcode.react` (`src/components/friend-qr.tsx`, black on white, level M, `data-value` carries the payload for probes); mobile uses `react-native-qrcode-svg` on the `react-native-svg` the app already ships (`components/FriendQr.tsx`). Both encode the **join link**, not the bare code, so an ordinary phone camera opens Bolo! instead of showing six letters to retype; `parseReferralScan` in `@workspace/referral-link` accepts either shape and rejects anything else, so scanning a poster or a wifi QR fails locally rather than burning a rate-limited attempt. Scanning uses `expo-camera` (`components/QrScannerSheet.tsx`), which is a **native module** — the scanner does not exist in any build shipped before it and needs a new store build. `app.json` needed no edit: `NSCameraUsageDescription` and `android.permission.CAMERA` already arrive via the expo-image-picker plugin, verified with `expo config --type introspect` and a real `expo prebuild --platform ios` (generated `ios/` deleted afterwards). The scanner latches its first accepted read in a ref, because the camera fires `onBarcodeScanned` on every frame that holds a code and one QR would otherwise burst the limiter.

**Scan-to-redeem was investigated and deliberately NOT wired.** A new learner scanning a friend's QR could redeem the referral in the same action — one extra call. The blocker is not the scan: a signed-in **existing** learner can already redeem via a `/join/<code>` link today. `pages/join.tsx` stores the code for any visitor, `ReferralRedemptionProvider` fires it for any signed-in user, and `redeemReferralCode` has no account-age guard. So a pair of long-standing accounts can already trade links and each collect referee and referrer Chai once. That is an **existing hole, not one this task would open** — but wiring scan-to-redeem would widen its mouth from "follow a link" to "point a camera", so it was left unwired and reported instead. An account-age or never-practised guard on redemption is the fix, and belongs to whoever picks that up.

**Remove-friend was not touched and still works.** `DELETE /friends/:userId` now resolves the row through `findFriendshipBetween` instead of its own inline pair check. Verified in a real browser end to end (web: request → accept → list → remove → empty, with the row confirmed gone from the database). Note for future probes: **react-native-web has no `Alert` implementation**, so mobile's confirm dialog — and therefore mobile's own Remove button — cannot be driven in the Expo web build; mobile removal is covered by the RNTL test and the shared API test instead.

**Evidence.** `qa/task1111-friends-shots.mjs` (web, 1280×900) and `qa/task1111-mobile-shots.mjs` / `-mobile-list-shots.mjs` / `-mobile-empty-shot.mjs` (Expo web, 412×824 @2x) drive the real dev API through the actual product flow — no row is ever inserted by hand, the "with friends" state is produced by typing a code and accepting the request. Shots in `qa/shots/task1111/` (gitignored). Mobile's friends screen is reached from Account, not the tab bar (`href: null` in the tabs layout); the probes navigate to `/friends` directly.


## 10ay. A train skin in the friend row background was measured, and it failed (August 13, 2026, qa only, nothing shipped)

**This was a mock and a measurement, not a feature.** No product file changed: no catalog, no `equipped_train_skin` column, no route, no change to `friends.ts`, the OpenAPI spec, the generated client, or either friends screen. The deliverable is two probe scripts under `qa/` and their shots.

**What was being decided.** Showing a learner's equipped train skin to their friends was ruled to be Option C — the train sits in the row's **background**, costing zero layout width, so the name column is untouched. C was conditional on this measurement. There was no fallback to Option A (a train beside a 56-64px mascot leaves roughly four to six name characters at 360px).

**Verdict: C is not viable.** In the background treatment at 25% opacity the engine never reaches the 3:1 bar for a graphical object (WCAG SC 1.4.11) against any of the three backgrounds, on either platform, in either theme. Best role contrast measured: web light 1.43:1 on the indigo self-row / 1.74:1 on `bg-card`; web dark 1.55:1 / 1.94:1; mobile light 1.42:1 / 1.75:1; mobile dark 1.46:1 / 1.93:1. The same engines rendered inline (treatment A) reach 6.8-16.5:1 on card rows (web light 13.5-16.5, web dark 8.0-12.2, mobile light 13.5-16.5, mobile dark 6.8-10.4), so the failure is the treatment, not the artwork. The one inline reading that also misses 3:1 is the indigo-adjacent livery, which sits on the indigo self-row: 2.59:1 web light, 2.54:1 mobile light, 3.58:1 mobile dark, 5.51:1 web dark. Indigo on indigo is hard in any treatment; the background treatment is what makes it hopeless for all four.

**The indigo-adjacent livery is the sharpest failure, as predicted.** On the caller's own indigo self-row its body reads 1.03:1 (mobile light, painted `#5249E6` on `#4F46E5`) and 1.05:1 (mobile dark). It is not faint, it is absent. The swatch was not adjusted to rescue the reading.

**Skins stay distinguishable only away from the self-row.** Body CIEDE2000 between provisional liveries in the background treatment: 4.7-13.9 on the indigo self-row (JND is about 2.3, so above threshold but small) and 15.2-35.2 on `bg-card`. Pairwise WCAG contrast between two skins is 1.0-1.2:1 throughout, i.e. they differ in hue, never in lightness.

**The naming test is what actually kills it.** Asked which full-size reference a row's train came from, nearest-reference matching on the painted three-role signature picks the **indigo livery for three of the four** on the self-row, in both themes on both platforms, with hue shifts of 50-147 degrees. On `bg-card` the hue mostly survives (shift under 20 degrees) but chroma collapses to 13-25% of the reference, and in dark mode two of four still mismatch. A learner cannot reliably match a background train back to the skin they bought.

**Cost side, for completeness.** XP text painted over the background engine clears 4.5:1 in seven of the eight platform/theme/background combinations (web light 5.97-12.59:1, web dark 4.59-14.58:1, mobile light self-row 6.08-7.24:1, mobile dark 4.50-5.58:1). The exception is mobile light on `bg-card`, where it drops to 3.00-3.34:1 against 4.76:1 for the same text on the plain row. Legibility is therefore not the main failure, but it is not untouched either; visibility and identifiability are what kill the treatment.

**Two measurement traps this run, both of which produced confident wrong numbers first.**
1. Sampling a background-treatment row on the live row samples the **XP glyphs painted over the engine**, not the engine (it read dE00 = 0 between two different skins). Both harnesses therefore carry a `strip` section of identical rows with the row's own content at `visibility: hidden` / `opacity: 0`, so samples hit the engine composited over the row background. Treatment A is still measured on live rows, since nothing is painted over an inline engine.
2. Sampling a row's own background at `(x+6, y+6)` lands **outside the 16px corner radius** and reads the page behind the card. Sample at the row's horizontal centre, a few pixels below its top edge.

**Harnesses.** `qa/task1117-web-skin-mock.mjs` copies a standalone Vite app from `qa/skins/web/` into `artifacts/gujarati-coach/src/__qa_skin_mock/` at run time (so bare imports, the `@` alias and Tailwind's `src/`-rooted scan resolve), spawns Vite detached, captures, and deletes the copy in a `finally`. `qa/task1117-mobile-skin-mock.mjs` writes a temporary **top-level** Expo route from `qa/skins/mobile/qa-skin-mock.template.tsx` (top-level renders outside the `(app)` auth gate but inside the providers; Metro cannot resolve imports from outside the project root, so palettes are injected as JSON) and deletes it in a `finally`. Both use the row markup copied verbatim from the real screens, the real train components, and a labelled placeholder for the dressed mascot. Web recolouring pins the same four theme tokens on a wrapper that the Chai wallet art tiles already pin; mobile uses `TrainEngine`'s existing `palette` prop. Shots in `qa/shots/task1117/` (gitignored), measurements in `web-measurements.json` / `mobile-measurements.json`.

**A parked engine never paints steam**, so only three of the four palette roles are visible in a row at all. Any future skin idea that leans on the steam colour is buying a role the learner cannot see on this surface. **This carries to First Class** (the gold-train replacement for skins): the signal encounter site has no motion either, so a gold palette that leans on the steam role would read differently across its three render sites. Put the gold in the body, chassis and trim roles, and treat steam as decoration that may never appear.

**Option C dying retires the train skin catalog.** The reason to buy a skin was social visibility, and this measurement says there is none. Ruled August 13, 2026: no `equipped_train_skin` column, no catalog, no buy or equip routes. First Class absorbs the idea — the text pill is the flex, the gold train is a private 24-hour bonus. Anyone reopening "let learners buy a train livery their friends can see" should re-read the numbers above first; they were taken from painted pixels, not from an opinion.

**RN-web sampling note.** A single tall-window screenshot cannot be trusted for an Expo web page: the ScrollView scrolls an inner div, so rows outside the visible band sample as out-of-bounds black. Screenshot each section element and take geometry relative to that section.

## 10ax. Friend rows wear the outfit their owner bought (August 13, 2026, api + web + mobile)

**The purchase had no audience.** An outfit costs 40 Chai and, before this, `equippedOutfit` was returned by exactly one endpoint (`GET /tokens`), self-only. Every row that names another learner — the friends list and the XP leaderboard, on both platforms — drew a 40px (web) / 44px (mobile) circle with their initials in it. Rows are the only place another person's outfit is ever seen, so the rows now render the learner's own Bolo, dressed.

**The outfit rides the row payload, not a per-row fetch.** `loadUserSummaries` in `artifacts/api-server/src/routes/friends.ts` gained a single `LEFT JOIN` onto `user_token_state`, so `equippedOutfit` and `equippedAccessory` come back with the identity fields in the same round trip for the friends list, the leaderboard and the request lists alike. A learner with no `user_token_state` row (never opened the Chai stall) comes back `null` in both slots, not missing. `toSummary` takes the two fields as optional arguments because the friend-code lookup path holds a plain `users` row and has no token state to give it.

**Both slots travel, not just the garment.** Shipping only `equippedOutfit` would show a learner who bought a pagdi bare-headed on every row but their own. The accessory is the same join and the same row, so it goes too.

**The fields are optional in the spec, never required** (`UserSummary`, `Friend`, `LeaderboardEntry` in `lib/api-spec/openapi.yaml`) — an older mobile build must keep parsing these payloads.

**Row geometry was settled by looking, not by reasoning.** The avatar is **56px** on both platforms and the crop is **the bird minus her feet** — a 745×745 window at (125, 55) of the 1024px frame, which lands as a 77px mascot offset (-9, -4) inside the 56px circle. The tighter "upper body" crop the plan suggested was rejected on sight: the garments hang on her belly, so cutting at the chest throws away the part that differs. Pose is **`wave`** on every row — front-facing, friendly, and the only pose where no wing crosses her belly (`thumbsup` and `thinking` both cover the garment; `tryagain` has a worried face). Constants live next to the components as `ROW_AVATAR_PX` / `ROW_CROP` / `ROW_MASCOT_PX` / `ROW_MASCOT_POSE` in `artifacts/gujarati-coach/src/pages/friends.tsx` and `artifacts/bolo-mobile/app/(app)/(tabs)/friends.tsx`.

**Kurta versus sherwani is not the hard case the plan expected.** They are not both cream: the kurta is saffron with a gold placket, the sherwani is cream brocade. Side by side at 56px in a real browser, on both platforms, they read as different garments at a glance. Checked in the shots below, not by argument.

**Request rows still show initials.** Incoming and outgoing friend *requests* keep the `Avatar` circle on both platforms — they were out of this task's scope, which leaves the requests section slightly shorter than the list below it.

**Rows must pass an EXPLICIT `null`.** Both `Mascot` components fall back to the *viewer's* equipped outfit when `outfit` is `undefined` (that is what every other surface wants). A row that omits the prop therefore paints every undressed friend in the viewer's own clothes, which no test would catch unless it distinguishes "null" from "not passed" — the mobile test's Mascot stub encodes exactly that difference in its testID (`mascot-outfit-none` vs `mascot-outfit-inherited`).

**Evidence.** `qa/task1112-row-mascot-shots.mjs` (web, 1280×900 and 402×874) and `qa/task1112-mobile-shots.mjs` (Expo web, 412×824 @2x) sign in as a seeded learner whose four friends wear kurta, sherwani, nothing, and anarkali + pagdi, and read each row's `data-outfit` and resolved `<img>` src off the DOM as well as shooting it. Shots in `qa/shots/task1112/` (gitignored). The web probe signs in with a Clerk `sign_in_tokens` ticket; the Expo probe uses email + password and must then clear the **Client Trust** "new device" code, which for a `+clerk_test` address is always `424242`.

## 10ay. First Class sells 24h of gold-train status (August 13, 2026, db + api + web + mobile)

**Why it is cosmetic-only.** The friends leaderboard ranks on XP. A purchasable XP advantage would be buying leaderboard position on the exact surface this status is designed to flex on. The single complimentary 20-minute Express boost is the whole of the XP story, and that boost is already purchasable standalone for 10 Chai, so no new advantage exists.

**Cost and duration.** 25 Chai for 24 hours. Repurchase **adds** 24 hours to an active expiry — it never refuses with a conflict. The 15 Chai above the 10 Chai standalone boost is the price of the status itself. A horizon ceiling refuses extension beyond 30 days out (`FIRST_CLASS_HORIZON_DAYS = 30`) with a distinct 409 code (`first_class_horizon`).

**Idempotency.** The client generates a UUID when the purchase button is armed and reuses it on retry. The ledger unique index on `(userId, reason, refId)` makes a double-tap a free replay. A genuinely new purchase re-arms the button (via the `key` prop on the wallet row) and gets a new UUID. The refId is grammar-pinned to a UUID regex in the OpenAPI spec so it cannot collide with the `YYYY-MM-DD` shape that `listCoveredDayKeys` scans.

**Status extends by addition; boost extends by max — deliberately, for different reasons.** Adding 24 hours to an active First Class expiry lets repurchase extend a running window predictably. Setting the boost to `max(existing, now+20min)` prevents a running boost from being shortened if a learner boards 19 minutes into one: they always get at least 20 minutes from the instant of purchase, but never more than 20 minutes from any instant.

**The boost is not charged.** It is a direct write of the `expressMultiplierExpiresAt` column inside the First Class transaction. Routing it through `spendTokens` would charge a second 10 Chai and throw `multiplier_active`. The standalone express multiplier's own constants, cost, and 409 guard are untouched.

**Storage mirrors the express precedent.** One nullable `timestamptz` column (`first_class_expires_at`) on `user_token_state`. Migration `0049_whole_celestials.sql`. No generic status table.

**Gold palette (approved August 13, 2026).** chassis `#6B4A0F`, body `#E8B93C`, trim `#FFE39A`, steam `#FFF6E0`. Distinct from `INDIA.gold` (`#F0A32B`) used by ExpressTile: that tile has a navy chassis and teal trim; this palette is all-gold so the two read as different objects on the same screen.

**Three gold sites per platform, not five.** Journey rail marker, home boarding pass, signal encounter. StationPauseTile and ExpressTile are product icons representing items for sale — turning a product icon gold because the learner bought something else is wrong. On web, a `display:contents` wrapper pins the four CSS vars without affecting layout; on mobile, the `palette` prop on `TrainEngine` is threaded from the parents that already hold the tokens query.

**Naming.** "First Class" is a coach class. The daily XP ladder uses train *types* (Local/Superfast/Rajdhani/Shatabdi) — deliberately different vocabularies that must not be conflated in code or copy. Bare "tier" is avoided; it already means subscription plan.

**Mobile 2× indicator gap.** A mobile learner who boards First Class may never see that the complimentary Express boost started — mobile has only the wallet row today, with no 2× indicator anywhere on the session screens. The minimum honest indication would be a brief banner or a 2× badge on the session XP chip at the moment of purchase.

## 10az. The mobile Speed Round paywall gate moved out of the effect and into the render (August 14, 2026, bolo-mobile only)

**An effect cannot stop a paint.** The gate was a `useEffect` that called `router.replace('/(app)/paywall')` when entitlements resolved to non-Plus. Effects run after commit, so a Free learner saw the fully composed Speed Round setup screen — hero, topic list, Hard Mode toggle, Start Game — for at least one frame before the paywall replaced it. The gate is now two early returns at render time in `artifacts/bolo-mobile/app/(app)/(tabs)/games/speed-round.tsx`, so nothing paid ever reaches the screen.

**Loading is checked FIRST, and returns null rather than redirecting.** `EntitlementsContext` falls back to plan `'free'` while the request is in flight, so testing `isPlus` before `isLoading` would bounce a paying subscriber to the paywall on every cold open. Returning `null` during load, instead of redirecting somewhere neutral, is what keeps the screen blank in the one window where the answer is genuinely unknown.

**The returns sit after every hook, deliberately.** Thirteen hooks run consecutively in `SpeedRoundScreen`, ending at `handleExit`. The slot immediately before the first `return` is the only position where an early return cannot skip a hook on some render path. Removing the effect also left `const router = useRouter()` unused in that component; it was deleted. The `useRouter` import stays because `SetupScreen` and `DoneScreen` both still call it. `<Redirect>` replaces rather than pushes, so there is no back-stack regression against the old `router.replace`. The href needed no cast: `/(app)/paywall` is already in the generated typed routes.

**The Free plan pill is now unreachable on Speed Round.** `__tests__/game-setup-plan-label.test.tsx` previously set `isLoading = true` purely as a device to hold the effect off long enough to assert the "Free" pill for a non-Plus learner — an assertion that only existed because of the defect. A non-Plus learner can no longer reach the setup screen at all, so the pill's Free branch is dead UI on this screen. The Speed Round half of that test now asserts the gate instead: loading paints nothing and does not redirect, and non-Plus redirects to `/(app)/paywall` with no setup screen. The file's `expo-router` mock had to gain a `Redirect` stub that surfaces its `href`.

**Three sibling screens still carry the old shape.** `games/phrase-builder.tsx` and `games/bolo-quiz.tsx` still gate in an effect and have the same flash. `games/script-trace.tsx` has the effect too but already returns `null` for non-Plus, so it does not flash. All three were out of scope here and were not touched — which is also why the Phrase Builder half of the plan-label test still passes unchanged. `practice/daily.tsx` is a dispatcher, not an access gate, and is not part of this family.
